q_{m}\in\mathcal{U}_{p_{m}}
for all
-M\leq m\leq N.

If

M,N>-K\log_{\lambda}L+K

for some uniform constant
K\geq 1
, then
q_{0}
cannot be a critical point.

Proof.

Suppose towards a contradiction that
q_{0}
is a critical point with a critical direction
E_{q_{0}}^{*}
. Then

\partial_{\mathbb{P}}F^{N}(E_{q_{0}}^{*})\geq 1

and this implies

\|D_{q_{0}}F^{N}|_{E_{q_{0}}^{*}}\|\leq(\operatorname{Jac}_{q_{0}}F^{N})^{1/2}<\lambda^{(1-\eta)N/2}.

By Proposition
3.9
and
3.11
, we see that

\measuredangle(E_{q_{0}}^{*},E_{q_{0}}^{v})<{\bar{L}}\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}N}\lambda^{(1-\eta)N/2}<k

for some sufficiently small uniform constant
k>0
. It follows that

\|D_{q_{0}}F^{-M}|_{E_{q_{0}}^{*}}\|>{\bar{L}}^{-1}\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})M}.

Thus,

\partial_{\mathbb{P}}F^{-M}(E_{q_{0}}^{*})=\frac{\operatorname{Jac}_{q_{0}}F^{-M}}{\|D_{q_{0}}F^{-M}|_{E_{q_{0}}^{*}}\|^{2}}<\frac{{\bar{L}}\lambda^{-(1+\varepsilon)M}}{{\bar{L}}^{-2}\lambda^{-2(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})M}}<{\bar{L}}\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})M}<1.

This is a contradiction.
∎

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
p_{0}\in\Lambda
be Pesin
(L,\varepsilon)
-regular for some
L\geq 1
, and let

\{\Phi_{p_{m}}:\mathcal{U}_{p_{m}}\to U_{p_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

be a linearization of the full orbit of
p_{0}
. Then the regular radii (specified in Theorem
3.6
) can be chosen uniformly small so that
\mathcal{U}_{p_{m}}
does not contain a critical point for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Let
\{\Phi_{p_{m}}:\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{m}}\to\hat{U}_{p_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}
be a starting uniformization of the orbit of
p_{0}
. Recall that

\hat{U}_{p_{m}}:=\mathbb{B}(cL^{-C}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}|m|})

for some uniform constants
c\in(0,1)
and
C\geq 1
. Let
d\in(0,c)
and
D>C
, and denote

U_{p_{m}}:=\mathbb{B}(dL^{-D}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}|m|})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{U}_{p_{m}}:=\Phi_{p_{m}}^{-1}(U_{c_{m}}).

Suppose towards a contradiction that
q_{0}
is a critical point and
q_{0}\in\mathcal{U}_{p_{m}}
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then for
i\in\mathbb{N}
such that
q_{-i}\in\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{m-i}}
, we have

\operatorname{dist}(p_{m-i},q_{-i})<\lambda^{-(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}dL^{-D}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}|m|}.

Thus, we have
q_{-i}\in\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{m-i}}
as long as

cL^{-C}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}|m-i|}>dL^{-D}\lambda^{-(1+\eta)i}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}|m|},

or more simply,

(d/c)L^{C-D}<\lambda^{(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}.

Let
i=M
be the first value for which the above inequality fails. Then

M>(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})^{-1}\left(\log_{\lambda}(d/c)-(D-C)\log_{\lambda}L\right).

We also have

\operatorname{dist}(p_{m+i},q_{i})<\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}i}dL^{-D}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}|m|}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pti\geq 0.

Thus, we have
q_{i}\in\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{m+i}}
as long as

cL^{-C}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}|m+i|}>\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}i}dL^{-D}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}|m|},

or more simply,

(d/c)L^{C-D}<\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}i}.

Let
i=N
be the smallest value for which the above inequality fails. Then

N>{\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}\left(\log_{\lambda}(d/c)-(D-C)\log_{\lambda}L\right).

Hence, by choosing
d
and
D
sufficiently smaller and larger than
c
and
C
respectively, the result follows from Proposition
5.4
.
∎

5.2.
Regular critical values

A point
c_{1}\in\Lambda
is an
\varepsilon
-regular critical value
if
c_{1}
is both infinite-time forward and backward
(1,\varepsilon)
-regular, and

E_{c_{1}}^{*}:=E_{c_{1}}^{ss}=E_{c_{1}}^{c}.

We refer to
c_{0}:=F^{-1}(c_{1})

as an
\varepsilon
-regular critical point
. Note that the strong stable manifold
W^{ss}(c_{1})
and the center manifold
W^{c}(c_{1})
have a tangency at
c_{1}
. If this tangency is of order
2
, then we say that
c_{1}
is
of quadratic type
.

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
c_{1}\in\Lambda
be an
\varepsilon
-regular critical value. Then
c_{1}
is the unique general critical point in its orbit.

Proposition 5.7
.

Suppose
F
is
\eta
-homogeneous for some
\eta\in(0,\underline{\varepsilon})
. If
c_{1}\in\Lambda
is a regular critical orbit point, then there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that for
n\geq N
, the point
c_{1}
is an
\varepsilon
-regular critical value for the map
F^{n}
.

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Proposition
4.7
.
∎

For
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, denote

\mathbb{L}^{h}_{b}:=\{(x,b)\;|\;x\in\mathbb{R}\}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbb{L}^{v}_{a}:=\{(a,y)\;|\;y\in\mathbb{R}\}.

Additionally, let
f_{a}(x):=x^{2}+a
, and denote

\mathbb{P}^{h}_{a}:=\{(x,f_{a}(x))\;|\;x\in\mathbb{R}\}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbb{P}^{v}_{a}:=\{(f_{a}(y),y)\;|\;y\in\mathbb{R}\}.

Theorem 5.8

(Uniformization along a regular critical orbit)
.

Let
c_{1}
be an
\varepsilon
-regular critical value of quadratic type with the critical direction
E_{c_{1}}^{*}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{c_{1}}
. Then there exist linearizations

\{\Phi_{c_{1+n}}:\mathcal{U}_{c_{1+n}}\to U_{c_{1+n}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\{\Phi_{c_{-n}}:\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n}}\to U_{c_{-n}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}

of the forward orbit of
c_{1}
and the backward orbit of
c_{0}
respectively that induce the following Hénon-like transition at the critical moment:

F_{c_{0}}(x,y):=\Phi_{c_{1}}\circ F|_{\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}}\circ\Phi_{c_{0}}^{-1}(x,y)=(x^{2}-\lambda y,x)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt(x,y)\in U_{c_{0}}.

Proof.

Let
\{\Psi_{c_{1+n}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
and
\{\Psi_{c_{-n}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
be some linearizations of the forward orbit of
c_{1}
and backward orbit of
c_{0}
. Note that

\Psi_{c_{1}}(W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(c_{1}))\subset\mathbb{L}^{v}_{0}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Psi_{c_{0}}(W^{c}(c_{0}))\subset\mathbb{L}^{h}_{0}.

We may assume that the regular radius
\rho_{0}:=l_{c_{0}}
at
c_{0}
is chosen uniformly smaller than the regular radius
\rho_{1}:=l_{c_{1}}
at
c_{1}
such that
F(\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}})\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. Denote
I^{i}:=(-\rho_{i},\rho_{i})
for
i\in\{0,1\}
, and
\hat{F}_{0}:=\Psi_{c_{1}}\circ F\circ\Psi_{c_{0}}^{-1}
.

The curve

\hat{F}_{0}(I^{0}\times\{0\})=\Psi_{c_{1}}(W^{c}(c_{1}))

is a quadratic vertical graph that has a unique point of genuine vertical tangency given by

v_{0}=(0,0)=\Psi_{c_{1}}(c_{1})\in W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(c_{1})\cap W^{c}(c_{1}).

Consequently, for
|y|<\rho_{0}
, the curve
\hat{F}_{0}(I^{0}\times\{y\})
is a quadratic vertical graph that has a unique point
v_{y}
whose tangent vector is genuine vertical. Moreover, there exist an interval
J^{1}\subset I^{1}
containing
0
; and
C^{r}
-maps
g_{0}:I^{0}\to I^{0}
and
g_{1}:J^{1}\to I^{1}
such that:

\hat{F}_{0}(g_{0}(y),y)=v_{y}=(u,g_{1}(u)).

The vertical graph
\Gamma^{v}(g_{0})
can be straightened to
\{0\}\times I^{0}
by a map of the form

\Phi_{0,0}(x,y)=(\phi_{0,0}(x,y),y).

Likewise, the horizontal graph
\Gamma^{h}(g_{1})
can be straightened to
J^{1}\times\{0\}
by a map of the form

\Phi_{1,0}(u,w)=(u,\phi_{1,0}(u,w)).

Since the derivatives
D\Phi_{0,0}
and
D\Phi_{1,0}
preserve the horizontal and vertical directions respectively, we see that the former preserves horizontal tangencies, while the latter preserves vertical tangencies.

Observe that
F_{0,0}:=\Phi_{1,0}\circ\hat{F}_{0}\circ\Phi_{0,0}^{-1}
maps
\{0\}\times I^{0}\subset\mathbb{L}^{v}_{0}
to
J^{1}\times\{0\}\subset\mathbb{L}^{h}_{0}
. Define

\psi_{1,1}(y):=\pi_{h}\circ F_{0,0}(0,y)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pty\in I^{0};

and let

\phi_{1,1}(u):=\lambda\psi_{1,1}^{-1}(u)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptu\in J^{1}.

Extend
\phi_{1,1}
to a
C^{r}
-diffeomorphism of
I^{1}\supset J^{1}
, and let
\Phi_{1,1}(u,w)=(\phi_{1,1}(x),y)
. Then for
F_{0,1}:=\Phi_{1,1}\circ F_{0,0}
, we have
F_{0,1}(0,y)=(-\lambda y,0)
.

The collection
\{F_{0,1}(I^{0}\times\{y\})\}_{y\in I^{0}}
forms a
C^{r}
-foliation of
F_{0,1}(U_{c_{0}})
by quadratic vertical curves in
U_{c_{1}}
. Extend this foliation to a
C^{r}
-foliation
\{\gamma^{v}_{z}\}_{z\in\hat{J}^{1}}
of a domain
\hat{U}_{c_{1}}\supset U_{c_{1}}
by quadratic vertical curves, so that
\hat{U}_{c_{1}}\cap\mathbb{L}^{h}_{0}=\hat{J}^{1}
, and for
(u,w)\in U_{c_{1}}
, there exists a unique value
\tau(u,w)\in\hat{J}^{1}
such that
(u,w)\in\gamma^{v}_{\tau(u,w)}
. Define

\Phi_{1,2}(u,w):=(u,\phi_{1,2}(u,w))\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt(u,w)\in U_{c_{1}},

where

\phi_{1,2}(u,w):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\sqrt{u-\tau(u,w)}&:w\geq 0\\
-\sqrt{u-\tau(u,w)}&:w<0;\end{array}\right.

and let
F_{0,2}:=\Phi_{1,2}\circ F_{0,1}
. Then for
y\in I^{0}
, we have
F_{0,2}(I^{0}\times\{y\})\subset\mathbb{P}^{v}_{-\lambda y}
.

Finally, let

\Phi_{0,3}(x,y)=(\phi_{0,3}(x,y),y)

be the map which straightens the vertical foliation given by
\{F_{0,2}^{-1}(I^{1}\times\{w\})\}_{w\in I^{1}}
to the genuine vertical foliation. Since
\Phi_{0,3}
preserves the genuine horizontal foliation, the image of a genuine horizontal line under
F_{c_{0}}:=F_{0,2}\circ\Phi_{0,3}^{-1}
is the same as
F_{0,2}
. It is now straightforward to check that

\Phi_{c_{0}}:=\Phi_{0,3}\circ\Phi_{0,0}\circ\Psi_{c_{0}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Phi_{c_{1}}:=\Phi_{1,2}\circ\Phi_{1,1}\circ\Phi_{1,0}\circ\Psi_{c_{1}}

provide the desired regular charts near
c_{1}
and
c_{0}
respectively.
∎

5.3.
Closest returns for regular critical orbits

Let
c_{1}\in\Lambda
be an
\varepsilon
-regular critical value. Let
\delta\in({\bar{\varepsilon}},1)
with
{\bar{\delta}}<1
.

Proposition 5.9
.

Let

\{\Phi_{c_{m}}:\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}}\to U_{c_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

be a uniformization of the orbit of
c_{0}
given in Theorem
5.8
. Then the regular radii can be chosen uniformly small so that
c_{0}\not\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}}\setminus\{c_{m}\}
for
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Let

\{\Phi_{c_{m}}:\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{m}}\to\hat{U}_{c_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

be a starting uniformization of the orbit of
c_{0}
. Recall that
\hat{U}_{c_{m}}:=\mathbb{B}(k\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}|m|})
for some uniform constant
k\in(0,1)
. Let
l\in(0,k)
, and denote

U_{c_{m}}:=\mathbb{B}(l\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}|m|})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}}:=\Phi_{c_{m}}^{-1}(U_{c_{m}}).

Suppose towards a contradiction that
c_{0}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}}\setminus\{c_{m}\}
. Note that by choosing
l
small, we can assume that
|m|
is uniformly large. Let
1\leq M,N\leq|m|
be the largest values for which we have
c_{i}\in\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{m+i}}
for
-M<i<N
. Arguing in a similar way as in the proof of Proposition
5.5
, we obtain

M>\min\{(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})^{-1}\log_{\lambda}(l/k),|m|\},

and

N>\min\{{\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}(l/k),|m|\}.

Thus, by choosing
l
sufficiently smaller than
k
, the result follows from Proposition
5.4
.
∎

Proposition 5.10

(Slow recurrence of the critical orbit)
.

There exists a uniformly small constant
r>0
such that the following holds. If

\mathbb{D}_{c_{m}}(r\lambda^{\delta|m|})\cap\mathbb{D}_{c_{0}}(r)\neq\varnothing

for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, then

|m|>\frac{1}{{\bar{\varepsilon}}}\log_{\lambda}\left(\operatorname{dist}(c_{m},c_{0})\right).

Proof.

For
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
\Phi_{c_{m}}
,
\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{m}}
and
\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}}
be the objects defined in the proof of Proposition
5.9
. Then

\operatorname{dist}(c_{m},c_{0})>\operatorname{radius}(\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}})>l_{c_{0}}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}|m|}.

Let
r:=\underline{l_{c_{0}}}
. Then we have

\operatorname{dist}(c_{m},c_{0})<r(1+\lambda^{\delta|m|})<\underline{l_{c_{0}}}.

Thus,

|m|>{\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}\left(\log_{\lambda}\left(\operatorname{dist}(c_{m},c_{0})\right)-\log_{\lambda}l_{c_{0}}\right)={\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}\left(\operatorname{dist}(c_{m},c_{0})\right).

∎

Lemma 5.11
.

There exists a uniform constant
r>0
such that the following holds. Let
0\leq n<m
. If
\operatorname{dist}(c_{\pm m},c_{0})<r\lambda^{\delta n}
, then we have
m>{\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}n
. Moreover, for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
|k|\leq{\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}n
, the value
\pm m+k
is not a Pliss moment in the forward/backward orbit of
c_{1/0}
.

Proof.

We prove the claim for
c_{m}
. The proof of the statement for
c_{-m}
is identical.

The first claim follows immediately from Proposition
5.10
. Denote
p_{0}:=c_{m}
, and let

\{\hat{\Phi}_{p_{l}}:\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{l}}\to\hat{U}_{p_{l}}\}_{-m<l<\infty}

be a linearization of the
(m-1,\infty)
-orbit of
p_{0}
. Then for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
|k|\leq n
, we have

\operatorname{diam}(\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{k}})\asymp\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}k}>r\lambda^{\varepsilon^{\prime}n}.

Hence,
c_{0}\in\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{k}}
. Arguing as in the proof of Proposition
5.9
, we obtain a contradiction.
∎

6.
Regularity Near a Critical Orbit

Let
F
be the diffeomorphism considered in Section
3
. Let
\eta,\varepsilon,\delta\in(0,1)
be small constants satisfying
\bar{\eta}<\varepsilon
,
{\bar{\varepsilon}}<\delta
and
{\bar{\delta}}<1
. Suppose that
F
is
\eta
-homogeneous on
\Lambda
. Moreover, suppose
F
has an
\varepsilon
-regular critical value
c_{1}\in\Lambda
of quadratic type with the critical direction
E_{c_{1}}^{*}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{c_{0}}
. Let

\{\Phi_{c_{m}}:(\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}},c_{m})\to(U_{c_{m}},0)\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

be the uniformization of the orbit of
c_{0}
given in Theorem
5.8
.

Proposition 6.1

(Recovery of forward regularity)
.

Let
p_{0}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
and
\tilde{E}_{p_{0}}^{v}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{0}}
. Write
z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0})
. Let
-n\leq 0
be a Pliss moment in the backward orbit of
c_{0}
. If

p_{-i}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{-i}}
for
0\leq i\leq n
, and we have

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}_{p_{0}}^{v},E^{v}_{p_{0}})>k|x_{0}|\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptn>(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})\delta^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|,

for some uniform constant
k\in(0,1)
, then
p_{-n}
is
n
-times forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}_{p_{-n}}^{v}
.

Proof.

Denote

\tilde{E}^{v}_{z_{0}}:=D\Phi_{c_{0}}(\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{0}})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\theta_{-i}:=\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{v}_{z_{-i}},E_{z_{-i}}^{gh})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq i\leq n.

By Theorem
3.6
ii) and Proposition
3.9
, there exist uniform constants
C,c>0
such that if
\theta_{-i}<C
, then

\theta_{-i}>c\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}\theta_{0},

and if
\theta_{-i}>C
, then

|\theta_{-i-1}-\pi/2|<\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})}|\theta_{-i}-\pi/2|.

Let
-j\leq 0
be the last moment such that
\theta_{-j}<C
. Observe

C>\theta_{-j}>ck\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})j}|x_{0}|.

This implies

j<(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|<(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})\delta n.

Observe that for
-n\leq m\leq-j
, we have

\measuredangle\left(\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{m}},E^{v}_{p_{m}}\right)<\nu

for some sufficiently small
\nu>0
. Thus, by Proposition
3.9
and the fact that
-n
is Pliss, we obtain

\|DF^{l}|_{\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}}\|<K\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})l}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq l\leq n-j,

where
K>0
is a uniform constant. The result now follows from the fact that

\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})(n-j)}<\lambda^{(1-\delta)n}.

∎

Proposition 6.2

(Obstruction to forward regularity)
.

There exists a uniform constant
L_{0}\geq 1
such that the following holds. For
L\geq L_{0}
, denote
L^{\prime}:=L^{2+{\bar{\delta}}}
. Let
p_{1}\in\mathbb{D}_{c_{1}}(1/L^{\prime})
;
\tilde{E}_{p_{1}}^{v}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{1}}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. If

p_{-i}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{-i}}
for
0\leq i\leq n
, and we have

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}_{p_{1}}^{v},E^{v}_{p_{1}})<1/L^{\prime}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt-(1+{\bar{\delta}})\log_{\lambda}L<n<-\frac{1-{\bar{\delta}}}{\delta}\log_{\lambda}L,

then
p_{-n}
is not
n
-times forward
(L,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}_{p_{-n}}^{v}
.

Proof.

Denote

z_{-i}:=\Phi_{c_{-i}}(p_{-i}),\hskip 14.22636pt\tilde{E}_{z_{-i}}^{v}:=D\Phi_{c_{-i}}(\tilde{E}_{p_{-i}}^{v})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\theta_{-i}:=\measuredangle(\tilde{E}_{z_{-i}}^{v},E_{z_{-i}}^{gh}).

By Theorems
5.8
and
3.6
ii), and Proposition
3.9
, we have

\theta_{-i}<K\lambda^{-(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}/L^{\prime}

for some uniform constant
K\geq 1
.

If
\theta_{-i}<\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}i}\nu
, where
\nu>0
is a sufficiently small uniform constant, then

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-i}},E^{h}_{p_{-i}})<\bar{\nu}.

Thus, by Proposition
3.9
, we obtain

\|DF^{-i}|_{\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{0}}}\|<\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}i}.

Let
j>0
be the smallest integer such that

K\lambda^{-(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})j}/L^{\prime}>\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}j}\nu.

Simplifying, we obtain

j>-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})\log_{\lambda}L^{\prime}.

(6.1)

Suppose
n<j
. By the assumed lower bound on
n
, we have

L^{-1}>\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})n}.

It follows that

\|DF^{n}|_{\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}}\|>\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}n}>L\lambda^{(1-\delta)n}.

Hence,
p_{-n}
is not
n
-times forward
(L,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}
.

Suppose
n>j
. Observe that

\|DF^{n}|_{\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}}\|>\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}j}\lambda^{(1+\eta)(n-j)}=\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})j}\lambda^{(\delta+\eta)n}\lambda^{(1-\delta)n}.

By (
6.1
) and the assumed upper bound on
n
, we have

\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})j}\lambda^{(\delta+\eta)n}>(L^{\prime})^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})^{2}}L^{-\frac{(1-{\bar{\delta}})(\delta+\eta)}{\delta}}>L.

Hence, we again see that
p_{-n}
is not
n
-times forward
(L,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}
.
∎

Proposition 6.3

(Recovery of backward regularity)
.

Let
p_{1}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
and
\tilde{E}_{p_{1}}^{h}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{1}}
. Write
z_{1}=(x_{1},y_{1}):=\Phi_{c_{1}}(p_{1}).
Let
n\geq 1
be a Pliss moment in the forward orbit of
c_{1}
. If

p_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq n
, and we have

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}_{p_{1}}^{h},E^{v}_{p_{1}})>k|x_{1}|\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptn>(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})\delta^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{1}|

for some uniform constant
k\in(0,1)
, then
p_{n}
is
n
-times backward
(O(1),\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}_{p_{n}}^{h}
.

Proof.

Denote

\tilde{E}_{z_{1}}^{h}:=D\Phi_{c_{1}}(\tilde{E}_{p_{1}}^{h})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\theta_{i}:=\measuredangle(\tilde{E}_{z_{i}}^{h},E_{z_{i}}^{gv})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt1\leq i\leq n.

By Theorem
3.6
ii), Proposition
3.9
and Lemma
3.10
, there exist uniform constants
C,c>0
such that if
\theta_{i}<C
, then

\theta_{i}>c\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}\theta_{1},

and if
\theta_{i}>C
, then

|\theta_{i+1}-\pi/2|<\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})}|\theta_{i}-\pi/2|+\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}.

Let
j\geq 1
be the last moment such that
\theta_{j}<C
. Observe

C>\theta_{j}>ck\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})j}|x_{1}|.

This implies

j<(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})\log_{\lambda}|x_{1}|<(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})\delta n.

Observe that for
j\leq m\leq n
, we have

\measuredangle\left(\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{m}},E^{h}_{p_{m}}\right)<\nu

for some sufficiently small
\nu>0
. Thus, by Proposition
3.9
and the fact that
n
is Pliss, we obtain

\|DF^{-l}|_{\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{n}}}\|<K\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}l}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq l<n-j,

where
K>0
is a uniform constant. Thus,

\|DF^{-n+j-l}|_{\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{n}}}\|<K\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}(n-j)}\lambda^{-(1+\eta)l}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq l<j.

The result now follows from the fact that

\lambda^{-(1+\eta)j}<\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})\delta n}.

∎

Proposition 6.4

(Obstruction to backward regularity)
.

There exists a uniform constant
L_{0}\geq 1
such that the following holds. For
L\geq L_{0}
, denote
L^{\prime}:=L^{2+{\bar{\delta}}}
. Let
p_{1}\in\mathbb{D}_{c_{1}}(1/L^{\prime})
;
\tilde{E}_{p_{1}}^{h}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{1}}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. If

p_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq n
, and we have

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}_{p_{1}}^{h},E^{v}_{p_{1}})<1/L^{\prime}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt-(1+{\bar{\delta}})\log_{\lambda}L<n<-\frac{1-{\bar{\delta}}}{\delta}\log_{\lambda}L,

then
p_{n}
is not
n
-times backward
(L,\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}_{p_{n}}^{h}
.

For
p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
t>0
, let

\mathbb{D}_{p}(t):=\{q\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\;|\;\operatorname{dist}(q,p)<t\}.

Proposition 6.5
.

For
t>0
, denote

\Lambda_{c_{0}}^{t}:=\Lambda\cap\bigcup_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{D}_{c_{-n}}(t\lambda^{\delta n}).

Then we have

\mu\left(\Lambda_{c_{0}}^{t}\right)\to 0
as
t\to 0.

Proof.

Fix a sufficiently small constant
t_{0}>0
such that

\mathbb{D}_{c_{-n}}(t_{0}\lambda^{\delta n})\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for all}\hskip 14.22636ptn\geq 0.

Let
K\geq 1
. We claim that there exists
N=N(K)\in\mathbb{N}
such that if
p_{0}\in\Lambda\cap\mathbb{D}_{c_{-n}}(t_{0}\lambda^{\delta n})
for some
n\geq N
, then
p_{0}
is not infinitely forward
(K,\underline{\delta})
-regular.

Suppose towards a contradiction that
p_{0}
is forward regular. Then by Proposition
3.4
,
p_{n+1}
is infinitely forward
(K\lambda^{-\delta n},\underline{\delta})
-regular. Write
z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0})
. By Theorem
3.6
, Proposition
3.9
and
\eta
-homogeneity, we have

|x_{0}|<t_{0}\lambda^{(\delta-\eta)n}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt|y_{0}|<t_{0}\lambda^{n}.

By Theorem
5.8
,

\operatorname{dist}(p_{n+1},c_{1})<\lambda^{(\delta-\bar{\eta})n}.

It follows that
p_{n+m}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}}
for all
1\leq m\leq M
, where
M\in\mathbb{N}
is the smallest integer such that

\lambda^{(\delta-\bar{\eta})n}\lambda^{-\eta M}<\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}M}.

This implies that
M>n/{\bar{\varepsilon}}
. By Proposition
3.11
, we have

\measuredangle(E^{ss}_{p_{n+1}},E^{v}_{p_{n+1}})<\lambda^{n/{\bar{\varepsilon}}}<\operatorname{dist}(p_{n+1},c_{1})

for all
n
sufficiently large. Observe that

\frac{1-{\bar{\delta}}}{(2+{\bar{\delta}})\underline{\delta}n}\log_{\lambda}(\operatorname{dist}(p_{n+1},c_{1}))\to\infty\hskip 14.22636pt\text{as}\hskip 14.22636ptn\to\infty.

Thus, by Proposition
6.2
,
p_{0}
is not infinitely forward
(K,\underline{\delta})
-regular for any finite constant
K
.

For
t\in(0,t_{0})
, let
N(t)\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
be the largest integer such that for all
q\in\Lambda_{c_{0}}^{t}
, there exists
n=n(t,q)\geq N(t)
such that
q\in\mathbb{D}_{c_{-n}}(t_{0}\lambda^{\delta n})
. Clearly,
N(t)\to\infty
as
t\to 0
. Thus, by above, we have

\Lambda_{c_{0}}^{t}\cap\Lambda^{+}_{K(t),\underline{\delta}}=\varnothing,

with
K(t)\to\infty
as
t\to 0
. The result now follows immediately from Proposition
3.24
.
∎

6.1.
Critical tunnels and valuable crescents

Let
{t}\in(0,l_{c_{0}}]
. Denote

\delta>\delta^{\prime}:=(1-{\bar{\delta}})\delta>\delta^{\prime\prime}:=(1-{\bar{\delta}})\delta^{\prime}.

For
n\geq 0
, let
j_{n}\geq 0
be the largest integer less than
n
such that
-j_{n}
is a Pliss moment in the backward orbit of
c_{0}
. Define the
(
1/\delta^{\prime\prime}
-pinched,
\delta^{\prime}
-truncated) critical tunnel at
c_{-n}
(of length
{t}
)
as

\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}=\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}({t}):=F^{-n}\left(\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}^{1/\delta^{\prime\prime},\delta^{\prime}j_{n}}({t})\right)

(see Subsection
3.4
). Note that
F(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n-1}})\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
, and the equality holds unless

n=j_{n}>\frac{\log_{\lambda}{t}}{\delta^{\prime}}.

Similarly, for
n\geq 1
, let
i_{n}\geq 1
be the largest integer less than
n
such that
i_{n}
is a Pliss moment in the forward orbit of
c_{1}
. Define the
(
1/\delta^{\prime\prime}
-pinched,
\delta^{\prime}
-truncated) valuable crescent at
c_{n}
(of length
{t}
)
by

\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}=\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}({t}):=F^{n-1}\left(\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}({t})\cap\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}^{\delta^{\prime}i_{n}}\right)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{where}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}({t}):=F(\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}({t})).

Note that
F^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}})\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{c_{n-1}}
, and the equality holds unless

n=i_{n}>\frac{2\log_{\lambda}{t}}{\delta^{\prime}}.

Lemma 6.6
.

For
{t}\in(0,l_{c_{0}}]
, let

N:=\frac{(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})\log_{\lambda}{t}}{\delta^{\prime}}.

Then

\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}({t})=\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}(l_{c_{0}})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptn>N

and

\mathcal{T}_{c_{1+n}}({t})=\mathcal{T}_{c_{1+n}}(l_{c_{0}})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptn>2N.

Proposition 6.7
.

For
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have
\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}}\setminus\{c_{m}\}.

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Lemma
3.15
(for
m\geq 1
) and Lemma
3.16
(for
m\leq 0
).
∎

Proposition 6.8
.

For
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}})<\lambda^{\delta|m|}.

Proof.

Suppose
m=-n\leq 0
. By Proposition
6.7
, we have

\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}=\Phi_{c_{-n}}^{-1}\circ F_{c_{0}}^{-n}\left(\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}^{1/\delta^{\prime\prime},\delta^{\prime}j_{n}}({t})\right).

Let
q_{-n}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
. Write

z_{-n}=(x_{-n},y_{-n}):=\Phi_{c_{-n}}(q_{-n}).

By Theorem
3.6
ii) and Proposition
3.9
, we have

|x_{-n}|<\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}n}|x_{0}|<\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}n}\lambda^{\delta^{\prime}n}<\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})\delta^{\prime}n}.

Moreover,

|y_{-n}|<\lambda^{-(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})n}|y_{0}|<\lambda^{-(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})n}|x_{0}|^{1/\delta^{\prime\prime}}<\lambda^{-(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})n}\lambda^{(1+{\bar{\delta}})n}<\lambda^{{\bar{\delta}}n}.

The result now follows from the fact that

\|\Phi_{c_{-n}}^{-1}\|_{C^{r}}=O(\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}n}).

A similar argument proves the case when
m\geq 1
.
∎

Proposition 6.9

(Invariance of tunnels and crescents)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
n>|m|
. Then the following statements hold.

i)

If

F^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n+1}})\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}\neq\varnothing,

then

n>{\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}|m|\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}=F^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n+1}}).

ii)

If

F(\mathcal{T}_{c_{n-1}})\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}\neq\varnothing,

then

n>{\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}|m|\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}=F(\mathcal{T}_{c_{n-1}}).

Proof.

Suppose

F^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n+1}})\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{-m}}=\varnothing

for some
0\leq m<n
.

Note that

\operatorname{dist}(c_{-n+m},c_{0})<\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}\cap\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}^{{\bar{\delta}}m})+\lambda^{-\eta m}\operatorname{diam}(F^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n+1}}))<\lambda^{{\bar{\delta}}m}.

The result now follows immediately from Lemma
5.11
.

The other cases can be proved in a similar way.
∎

If
p\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}({t})
for some
n\geq 0
, then the
{t}
-critical depth of
p
is defined as the largest integer
0\leq d_{-}:=\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p)\leq\infty
such that
p\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-d_{-}(p)}}({t}).
Similarly, if
q\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}({t})
for some
n\geq 1
, then the
{t}
-valuable depth of
p
is defined as the largest integer
1\leq d_{+}:=\operatorname{vd}_{t}(p)\leq\infty
such that

p\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{d_{+}(p)}}({t}).

Define the
critical dust of
c_{0}
as

\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}:=\bigcap_{n=0}^{\infty}\bigcup_{i=n}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}_{c_{-i}}({t})=\{p\in\Lambda\;|\;\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p)=\infty\}.

Similarly, define the
valuable dust of
c_{1}
as

\Delta^{v}_{c_{1}}:=\bigcap_{n=1}^{\infty}\bigcup_{i=n}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}_{c_{i}}({t})=\{p\in\Lambda\;|\;\operatorname{vd}_{t}(p)=\infty\}.

Lastly, define the
full critical dust of
c_{0}
as

\Delta^{fc}_{c_{0}}:=\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}\cup\Delta^{v}_{c_{1}}.

Proposition 6.10
.

We have

\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}=\bigcap_{{t}>0}\{p\in\Lambda\;|\;\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p)=\infty\}

and

\Delta^{v}_{c_{1}}=\bigcap_{{t}>0}\{p\in\Lambda\;|\;\operatorname{vd}_{t}(p)=\infty\}.

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Lemma
6.6
.
∎

Proposition 6.11
.

The sets
\Delta^{c/fc}_{c_{0}}
and
\Delta_{c_{1}}^{v}
are totally invariant. Consequently,
\mu(\Delta_{c_{0}}^{fc})=0
.

Proof.

Let
p\in\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}
. If
p\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
with
n>0
, then
p\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n+1}}
. Thus,

F(\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}})\subset\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}.

Let
k\geq 0
be the smallest integer such that
p\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-k}}
. Then by Proposition
6.9
, we have

F^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}})=\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n-1}}.

Thus,

F^{-1}(\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}})\subset\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}.

The total invariance of
\Delta^{v}_{c_{1}}
can be proved in a similar way.
∎

The
tunnel escape moment
of
p_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
is the unique integer
-j<0
such that

p_{-j+1}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-j+1}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptp_{-j}\not\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-j}}.

Similarly, the
crescent escape moment
of
p_{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}
is the unique integer
i>1
such that

p_{i-1}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{i-1}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptp_{i}\not\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{i}}.

Write
(x_{1},y_{1}):=\Phi_{c_{1}}(p_{1}).

If
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{1}}
is
k|x_{1}|^{1/2}
-vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
for some sufficiently small uniform constant
k>0
, then we say that
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}
is
sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. Similarly, if
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{0}}
is
k|x_{0}|^{2}
-horizontal in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
, then we say that
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{1}}
is
sufficiently center-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
.

Proposition 6.12

(Forward/backward regularity implies stable/center alignment)
.

Let
p_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
and
\tilde{E}^{v/h}_{p_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{0}}
. Write

z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\tilde{E}^{v/h}_{z_{0}}:=D\Phi_{c_{0}}(\tilde{E}^{v/h}_{p_{0}}).

Let

N>K\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|

for some sufficiently large uniform constant
K\geq 1
. If
p_{1}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
is
N
-times forward
(1,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}
, then
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. Similarly, if
p_{0}
is
N
-times backward
(1,\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{0}}
, then
E^{h}_{p_{0}}
is sufficiently center-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
.

Proposition 6.13
.

Let
p_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
. Write

z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0}).

If
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
, then the following statements hold.

i)

If
-j<0
is the tunnel escape moment of
p_{0}
, then
p_{-j}
is
j
-times forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}_{p_{-j}}^{v}
.

ii)

The direction
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n}}
for all
n>(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|
such that
p_{-i}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{-i}}
for
i\leq n
.

iii)

We have
\|DF^{n}|_{\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}}\|>\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}n}
for all
0\leq n<(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.3
, we have

j>(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})\frac{\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|}{\delta^{\prime}}>(1+{\bar{\delta}})\delta^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|.

The first claim now follows from Lemma
6.7
and Proposition
6.1
.

Write

\tilde{E}^{v}_{z_{0}}:=D\Phi_{c_{0}}(\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{0}})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\theta^{h/v}_{0}:=\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{v}_{z_{0}},E^{gh/gv}_{z_{0}}).

By Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
3.9
, there exists a uniform constant
k>0
such that for
0\leq n<j
, if we have

\theta^{h/v}_{-n}<k,

then

\lambda^{\mp(1\pm{\bar{\varepsilon}})}<\frac{\theta^{h/v}_{-n-1}}{\theta^{h/v}_{-n}}<\lambda^{\mp(1\pm{\bar{\varepsilon}})}.

Note that
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}
is sufficiently horizontal/vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n}}
if

\theta^{h/v}_{-n}<l\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}n}

for some uniform constant
l>0
. Since

\theta^{h}_{0}\asymp|x_{0}|

we see that for some uniform constant
K\geq 1
, we have

\theta^{h}_{-n}<K\lambda^{-(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})n}|x_{0}|<l\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}n}{\bar{K}}\lambda^{-(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})n}|x_{0}|<l\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}n}

for

0\leq n<(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|.

The third claim follows from Proposition
3.9
.

Observe that if

\theta^{h}_{-n}>k,

then

\theta^{v}_{-n-N}<k

for some uniform constant
N\geq 0
. Arguing similarly as above, we conclude the second claim.
∎

Proposition 6.14
.

Let
p_{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}
. Write

z_{1}=(x_{1},y_{1}):=\Phi_{c_{1}}(p_{1}).

If
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{0}}
is sufficiently center-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
, then the following statements hold.

i)

If
i>1
be the crescent escape moment of
p_{1}
, then
p_{i}
is
i
-times backward
(O(1),\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{i}}
.

ii)

The direction
\hat{E}^{h}_{p_{n}}
is sufficiently horizontal in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{n}}
for all
n>(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})\log_{\lambda}|x_{1}|
such that
p_{j}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{j}}
for
j\leq n
.

iii)

We have
\|DF^{-n}|_{\hat{E}^{h}_{p_{n}}}\|>\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})n}
for all
0\leq n<(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})\log_{\lambda}|x_{1}|
.

For
i\in\{0,1\}
, let
\Gamma_{i}\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{i}}
be a
C^{1}
-curve parameterized by its arclength
|\Gamma_{i}|
. We say that
\Gamma_{1}
is
sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
if the direction of
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. Similarly,
\Gamma_{0}
is
sufficiently center-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
if the direction of
\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}
is sufficiently center-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
.

7.
Mild Dissipativity

Let
F
the diffeomorphism as considered in Section
3
. Suppose additionally that
F
is mildly dissipative. Let
L\geq 1
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
with
{\bar{\varepsilon}}<1
.

A point
p\in\Lambda^{+}_{L,\varepsilon}
is said to be
proper forward regular
if
W^{ss}(p)
is proper in
\Omega
. In this case, let
W^{ss}_{\Omega}(p)
be the connected component of
W^{ss}(p)\cap\Omega
containing
p
. Lastly, denote the set of properly forward regular points in
\Lambda^{+}_{L,\varepsilon}
and
\Lambda^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}
by
\hat{\Lambda}^{+}_{L,\varepsilon}
and
\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}
respectively.

Lemma 7.1
.

The sets
\hat{\Lambda}^{+}_{L,\varepsilon}
and
\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}
are compact.

Proof.

Note that since
(L,\varepsilon)
-regularity is a closed condition, the sets
\Lambda^{+}_{L,\varepsilon}
and
\Lambda^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}
are compact. The result now follows immediately from Proposition
3.22
.
∎

For
p\in\Lambda^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}
, let

\Phi_{p}:\mathcal{U}_{p}\to U_{p}

be a regular chart at
p
. Recall that a regular box at
p
is given by

\mathcal{B}_{p}:=\Phi_{p}^{-1}(\mathbb{B}(1/L_{1}))\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for some}\hskip 14.22636ptL_{1}:={\bar{L}}

(see Subsection
3.5
). For
n\geq 0
, let
\mathcal{B}_{p}^{n}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{B}_{p}\cap F^{n}(\Omega)
containing
p
. Denote

B_{p}^{n}:=\Phi_{p}(\mathcal{B}_{p}^{n}).

Proposition 7.2

(Iterated image of
\partial\Omega
as top-bottom boundary)
.

There exists a uniform constant
M\in\mathbb{N}
with

M>-\log_{\lambda}\overline{L_{1}}

such that the following holds. Let
p_{0}\in\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{(L,\varepsilon)}
. Then for
n\geq M
, the set
\partial B_{p_{0}}^{n}\cap B_{p_{0}}
is the union of two
C^{r}
Jordan arcs
\gamma_{+}^{n}
and
\gamma_{-}^{n}
. Moreover,
\gamma_{\pm}
is the horizontal graph of a
C^{r}
-function
g^{n}_{\pm}:(-1/L_{1},1/L_{1})\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\lambda^{(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})n}<|g^{n}_{\pm}(x)|<\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})n}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt-1/L_{1}<x<1/L_{1}

and

\|(g^{n}_{\pm})^{\prime}\|,\|(g^{n}_{\pm})^{\prime\prime}\|<\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})n}.

Proof.

Let
\kappa\in({\bar{\varepsilon}},1)
be a uniformly small constant such that
\bar{\kappa}<1
. Let
\{-m_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
be the decreasing sequence of all negative Pliss moments in the full orbit of
p_{0}
such that
p_{-m_{i}}\in\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{1,\kappa}
. Choose
M=m_{i}
with
i
sufficiently large such that

M>-\log_{\lambda}{\bar{L}},

and consider

q_{0}:=p_{-M}\in\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{1,\kappa}.

Let

\{\Phi_{q_{m}}:\mathcal{U}_{q_{m}}\to U_{q_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

be a linearization of the full orbit of
q_{0}
. Then

\operatorname{radius}(U_{q_{m}})=l_{q_{m}}\asymp\lambda^{\bar{\kappa}|m|}.

For
a>0
and
n\geq 0
, denote

\mathcal{U}_{q_{m}}^{n}(a):=\Phi_{q_{m}}^{-1}(U_{q_{m}}^{n}(a))\hskip 14.22636pt\text{where}\hskip 14.22636ptU_{q_{m}}^{n}(a):=\mathbb{B}(al_{q_{m}}\lambda^{\bar{\kappa}n},l_{q_{m}}).

By Lemma
7.1
, there exists a uniform constant
N_{0}<M
such that for
n\geq N_{0}
, the curve

\mathcal{C}_{n}:=F^{n}(W^{ss}_{\Omega}(q_{0}))

is contained in
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(q_{n})
. By choosing a new domain
\tilde{\Omega}
such that
\Omega\supset\tilde{\Omega}\supset F(\Omega)
if necessary, we may assume that for some small uniform constant
a_{0}\in(0,1)
, the set
\partial F^{N_{0}}(\Omega)\cap\mathcal{U}^{0}_{q_{N_{0}}}(a_{0})
is the union of two
C^{r}
-Jordan arcs
\Gamma_{+}^{0}
and
\Gamma_{-}^{0}
. Moreover, by Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
3.9
, there exists a uniform constant
K\geq 1
such that the following holds. For
n\geq 0
, the curve

\gamma_{\pm}^{n}:=\Phi_{q_{N_{0}+n}}(\Gamma_{\pm}^{n})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{where}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma_{\pm}^{n}:=F^{n}(\Gamma_{\pm}^{0}\cap\mathcal{U}_{q_{N_{0}}}^{n}(a_{0}))

is the horizontal graph of a
C^{r}
-map
g_{\pm}^{n}:\mathbb{I}(a_{0}l_{0}\lambda^{\bar{\kappa}n})\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\|g_{\pm}\|_{C^{r}}<K\lambda^{(1-\bar{\kappa})n}

and

g_{\pm}^{n}(x)>K^{-1}\lambda^{(1+\bar{\kappa})n}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptx\in\mathbb{I}(a_{0}l_{0}\lambda^{\bar{\kappa}n}).

Observe that

\lambda^{\bar{\kappa}M},\lambda^{(1\pm\bar{\kappa})M}<1/{\bar{L}},\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\|\Phi_{q_{M}}^{-1}\|_{C^{r}}<{\bar{L}}.

Let

\{\Phi_{p_{m}}:\mathcal{U}_{p_{m}}\to U_{p_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

be a linearization of the full orbit of
p_{0}
. Then

\|\Phi_{p_{0}}\|_{C^{r}}<{\bar{L}}.

Since

\gamma_{\pm}=\Phi_{p_{0}}\circ\Phi_{q_{M}}^{-1}(\gamma_{\pm}^{M-N_{0}}),

the result follows.
∎

By Proposition
7.2
, we can form dynamically meaningful top-bottom boundaries of a regular box by choosing them as subarcs of
F^{M}(\partial\Omega)
for some sufficiently large
M\in\mathbb{N}
. The following result states that if
F
is homogeneous, then there is a uniform bound on how large
M
has to be (so that there is a uniform lower bound on the resulting height of the regular box).

Proposition 7.3
.

If
F
is
\eta
-homogeneous for some
\eta\in(0,\underline{\varepsilon})
, then we can choose
M
in Proposition
7.2
to be

M=-\log_{\lambda}\overline{L_{1}}.

Proof.

Consider the sequence of Pliss moments
\{-m_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
considered in the proof of Proposition
7.2
. By Proposition
4.4
and Proposition
4.3
, we have

m_{1}<-\kappa^{-1}\log_{\lambda}{\bar{L}}=-\log_{\lambda}{\bar{L}},

and if

m_{i}>-\log_{\lambda}{\bar{L}},

then

m_{i}>(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})m_{i+1}+\log_{\lambda}{\bar{L}}.

Hence, we may choose

M=-\log_{\lambda}\overline{L_{1}}.

∎

For
p\in\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{L,\delta}
and
a\in[0,1]
, define

\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(a):=\Phi_{p}^{-1}\left(B_{p}^{M}\cap U_{p}(a)\right),

where
M
is given in Proposition
7.2
(or Proposition
7.3
if
F
is homogeneous). Define an
enclosed regular box at
p
as

\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}:=\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1/2),

(7.1)

and the
outer boundary of
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
as

\bar{\partial}\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}:=\partial\mathcal{B}^{M}_{p}(1)\cap\partial F^{M}(\Omega).

(7.2)

A Jordan curve
\Gamma:(0,1)\to\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
is said to be
vertically proper in
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
if
\Gamma
extends continuously to
[0,1]
, and
\Gamma(0)
and
\Gamma(1)
are in distinct components of
\bar{\partial}\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
. More generally, a Jordan curve
\Gamma
in
\Omega
is said to be vertically proper in
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
if
\Gamma\cap\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1)
is.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
p\in\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}
. If
q\in\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
is
N
-times forward
(L,\varepsilon)
-regular for some

N>-\log_{\lambda}{\bar{L}},

then
W^{v}(q)
is proper in
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.18
,
W^{v}(q)
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{p}
. Moreover,

\operatorname{diam}(W^{v}(q))>\overline{\operatorname{diam}(\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p})}.

The result follows.
∎

Define

\Lambda^{\pm}_{\varepsilon}:=\bigcup_{L\geq 1}\Lambda^{\pm}_{L,\varepsilon}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Lambda^{P}_{\varepsilon}:=\bigcup_{L\geq 1}\Lambda^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}.

Let
\hat{\Lambda}^{+}_{\varepsilon}\subset\Lambda^{+}_{\varepsilon}
be the set of all mildly forward regular points in
\Lambda^{+}_{\varepsilon}
.

Proposition 7.5
.

Suppose that
F|_{\Lambda}
is uniquely ergodic. Then we have

\hat{\Lambda}^{+}_{\varepsilon}=\Lambda^{+}_{\varepsilon}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{\varepsilon}=\Lambda^{P}_{\varepsilon}.

Proof.

By replacing
F
by
F^{N}
for some sufficiently large
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we may assume that
F
is
\eta
-homogeneous for some sufficiently small constant
\eta\in(0,\underline{\varepsilon})
(see Proposition
4.1
).

Consider the following open set

{\mathbf{U}}:=\bigcup_{p\in\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{1,{\bar{\varepsilon}}}}\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}.

By Proposition
4.8
,

\alpha:=\mu({\mathbf{U}}\cap\Lambda)>\frac{\varepsilon-\eta}{\varepsilon+\eta}\simeq 1.

Let
p_{0}\in\Lambda^{+}_{L,\varepsilon}
for some
L\geq 1
. Let
\{n_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
and
\{m_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
be the increasing sequences of all moments in the forward orbit of
p_{0}
such that
p_{n_{i}}\in{\mathbf{U}}
and
p_{m_{i}}\in\Lambda^{+}_{1,{\bar{\varepsilon}}}
. By unique ergodicity, we have

\lim_{i\to\infty}\frac{i}{n_{i}}=\alpha.

Thus, by Proposition
4.2
, there exist
i,j\geq 1
such that
n_{i}=m_{j}
. The result now follows from Proposition
7.4
.
∎

8.
Infinite Renormalizability

Let
F
be the mildly dissipative diffeomorphism considered in Section
7
. Suppose additionally that
F
is infinitely renormalizable. Then there exists a nested sequence
\Omega=:\mathcal{D}^{0}\Supset\mathcal{D}^{1}\Supset\ldots
of Jordan domains and a sequence of natural numbers
1=:R_{0}<R_{1}<\ldots
such that
\mathcal{D}^{n}
is
R_{n}
-periodic and

r_{n-1}:=R_{n}/R_{n-1}\geq 2\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptn\in\mathbb{N}.

Recall that the renormalization limit set is given by

\Lambda:=\bigcap_{n=1}^{\infty}\bigcup_{i=0}^{r_{n}-1}F^{i}(D^{n}).

Consider the standard odometer
S:\mathbb{O}\to\mathbb{O}
defined by

\mathbb{O}:=\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(\mathbb{Z}/R_{n}\mathbb{Z}),

and

S(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n-1},a_{n},a_{n+1},\ldots):=(0,\ldots,0,a_{n}+1,a_{n+1},\ldots),

where
n\geq 1
is the smallest integer such that
a_{n}\neq R_{n}-1
. Denote

\mathbb{O}_{0}^{n}:=\{(0,\ldots,0,a_{n+1},a_{n+2},\ldots)\;|\;a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}/R_{i}\mathbb{Z}\hskip 8.53581pt\text{for}\hskip 8.53581pti\geq n+1\}.

Lastly, let
\mu_{0}
be the unique invariant probability measure for
T|_{\mathbb{O}}
. Note that

\mu_{0}(\mathbb{O}_{0}^{n})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{R_{i}}.

Proposition 8.1
.

There exists a unique continuous semi-conjugacy
\pi:\Lambda\to\mathbb{O}
such that

\pi\circ F|_{\Lambda}=S\circ\pi

and

\pi(D^{n})=\mathbb{O}_{0}^{n}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptn\in\mathbb{N}.

Moreover, for every
x\in\mathbb{O}
, the fiber
\pi^{-1}(x)
is a connected component of
\Lambda
.

Proposition 8.2
.

For
\mu_{0}
-almost every
x\in\mathbb{O}
, the fiber
\pi^{-1}(x)\subset\Lambda
is trivial. Consequently,
F|_{\Lambda}
has a unique invariant probability measure
\mu:=\pi^{*}\mu_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
\mu
be an ergodic probability measure on
\Lambda
. Note that
\mu
cannot have two negative Lyapunov exponents since it is not supported on a sink. By Proposition
3.24
, for any
\kappa>0
, there exists
L\geq 1
such that
\mu(\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{L,\varepsilon})>1-\kappa
(recall that if
p\in\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}
, then
p
is Pesin
(L,\varepsilon)
-regular, and
W^{ss}_{\Omega}(p)
is proper in
\Omega
).

Let
p\in\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}
. For
n\geq 0
, let
\mathcal{B}_{p}^{n}
be the connected component of
\mathcal{B}_{p}\cap F^{n}(\Omega)
containing
p
, where
\mathcal{B}_{p}
is a regular box at
p
. Suppose
q^{n}_{r},q^{n}_{l}\in\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}\cap\mathcal{B}_{p}^{n}
are points located to the right and left of
W^{ss}_{\Omega}(p_{0})\cap\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}^{n}
respectively. Then we can form a rectangle
\mathcal{B}_{p}^{n}(q^{n}_{r},q^{n}_{l})\subset\mathcal{B}^{n}_{p}
enclosed by
W^{ss}_{\Omega}(q^{n}_{r})
and
W^{ss}_{\Omega}(q^{n}_{l})
. If
q^{n}_{r}
and
q^{n}_{l}
are sufficiently close to
p
, then by Proposition
7.2
, we have

\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{B}_{p}^{n}(q^{n}_{r},q^{n}_{l}))=O(\lambda^{(1-\varepsilon)n}).

Let
Z_{p}
be the connected component of
\Lambda
containing
p
. If
q^{n}_{r},q^{n}_{l}\notin Z_{p}
for all
n
, then
Z_{p}\subset\mathcal{B}_{p}^{n}(q^{n}_{r},q^{n}_{l})
. This implies that
Z_{p}=\{p\}
.

Let
q\in\hat{\Lambda}_{L,\varepsilon}
, and let
Z_{q}
be the connected component of
\Lambda
that contains
q
. We claim that if
q
cannot be approached by points in
\hat{\Lambda}_{L,\varepsilon}\setminus Z_{q}
from both sides of
W^{ss}_{\Omega}(q)
, then there exists a rectangle
R_{q}\subset\mathcal{B}_{q}
containing
q
such that
R_{q}\cap\hat{\Lambda}_{L,\varepsilon}\subset Z_{q}
. The set of all such components
Z_{q}
is countable, and the result follows.

If
q
is isolated in
\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}
, or can only be approached from within
\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}
by points contained in
Z_{q}
, then clearly the claim holds. If
q
can only be approached by points in
\hat{\Lambda}^{P}_{L,\varepsilon}\setminus Z_{q}
from one side of
W^{ss}_{\Omega}(q)
, then we can form a rectangle
R_{q}
so that one of its sides is contained in
W^{ss}_{\Omega}(q)
, and
R_{q}\cap\hat{\Lambda}_{L,\varepsilon}\subset W^{ss}_{\Omega}(q)
. Since
W^{ss}_{\Omega}(q)\cap\hat{\Lambda}_{L,\varepsilon}\subset Z_{q}
, the claim again holds.
∎

Proposition 8.3
.

The unique invariant measure
\mu
for
F|_{\Lambda}
is not hyperbolic. That is, the Lyapunov exponents of
F|_{\Lambda}
must be
0
and
\log\lambda
for some
\lambda\in(0,1)
.

Proof.

Suppose
\mu
is hyperbolic. Then it has a period
p\geq 1
. Up to take an iterate, one can assume that
p=1
. Then for almost every points
x,y\in\Lambda
, there exists a homoclinic point
z
such that

\operatorname{dist}(f^{n}(x),f^{n}(z))\to 0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{dist}(f^{-n}(y),f^{-n}(z))\to 0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{as}\hskip 14.22636ptn\to\infty.

This is a contradiction, since one can choose
x,y
to belong to different connected components of
\Lambda
.
∎

9.
Regular Unicriticality

Let
F
be the mildly dissipative, infinitely renormalizable diffeomorphism considered in Section
8
. Let
\eta,\varepsilon,\delta\in(0,1)
be small constants such that

\bar{\eta}<\varepsilon
;

{\bar{\varepsilon}}<\delta
and

{\bar{\delta}}<1.

Suppose that
F
is
(\delta,{\bar{\varepsilon}})
-regularly unicritical. Let
\{c_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset\Lambda
be a regular critical orbit with

c_{1}\in\mathcal{D}^{n}
for all

n\in\mathbb{N}.

Denote

\mathcal{D}^{n}_{i}:=F^{i-1}(\mathcal{D}^{n})
for
i\in\mathbb{N}.

By Propositions
8.2
,
4.1
and
5.7
; and Remark
1.6
, we can replace
F
by
F^{R_{N}}|_{\mathcal{D}^{N}}
for some sufficiently large
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that the following properties hold:

i)

F
is
\eta
-homogeneous on
\Lambda\cap\mathcal{D}^{N}
;

ii)

c_{1}\in\mathcal{D}^{n}_{1}
is an
\varepsilon
-regular critical value for
F
; and

iii)

F
is
(\delta,{\bar{\varepsilon}})
-regular unicritical on
\Lambda\cap\mathcal{D}^{n}_{1}
.

Let

\{\Phi_{c_{m}}:(\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}},c_{m})\to(U_{c_{m}},0)\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

be a uniformization of the orbit of the
c_{1}
given in Theorem
5.8
. Let
t_{0}\in(0,1)
be a sufficiently small uniform constant so that

\mathbb{D}_{c_{-i}}(t_{0}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}i})\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{-i}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pti\geq 0.

Let
L_{0}:=L(t_{0})\geq 1
be the regularity factor given in Definition
1.3
ii).

By Proposition
3.2
,
c_{0}
is infinitely backward
(O(1),{\bar{\varepsilon}})_{v}
-regular along

E^{v}_{c_{0}}:=D_{0}\Phi_{c_{0}}^{-1}(E^{gv}_{0}).

Proposition 9.1
.

Let
t\in(0,t_{0})
. After enlarging
L(t)\geq 1
by a uniform amount if necessary, the following condition holds. If

c_{-n}\notin\bigcup_{i=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{D}_{c_{-i}}({t}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}i})

for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then
c_{-n}
is
n
-times forward
(L(t),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v}_{c_{-n}}
.

Proof.

By the regular unicriticality condition,
c_{-n}
is
n
-times forward
(L(t),\delta)_{v}
-regular along some direction
\tilde{E}^{v}_{c_{-n}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{c_{-n}}
. Let
0\leq j\leq n
be the largest integer such that
-j
is a Pliss moment in the backward orbit of
c_{0}
. This means that
c_{-j}
is
(\infty,j)
-times
(K,{\bar{\varepsilon}})_{v}
-regular along
E_{c_{-j}}^{v}
for some uniform constant
K\geq 1
. By Lemma
4.3
, we have

j>(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})n.

It follows from Proposition
3.4
that
c_{-n}
is
n
-times forward
(K\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}n},{\bar{\varepsilon}})_{v}
-regular along
E_{c_{-k}}^{v}
. Using Proposition
3.11
, we see that

\measuredangle(E^{v}_{c_{-n}},\tilde{E}_{c_{-n}}^{v})<\overline{L(t)}\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}n}\lambda^{(1-\delta)n}.

If

\measuredangle(E^{v}_{c_{-n}},\tilde{E}_{c_{-n}}^{v})<k\lambda^{(1+\eta){\bar{\varepsilon}}n}

for some uniformly small constant
k>0
, then

\measuredangle(E^{v}_{c_{-i}},\tilde{E}_{c_{-i}}^{v})<k\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for all}\hskip 14.22636ptj\leq i\leq n.

Thus, if

n>C\log_{\lambda}L(t)

for some uniform constant
C\geq 1
, then we see that
c_{-n}
is
n
-times forward
(\overline{L(t)},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v}_{c_{-n}}
.
∎

9.1.
Critical discs

Let
\varepsilon_{i}
with
i\in\{0,1,2\}
be small constants with

\varepsilon_{0}:=\varepsilon
and
{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{i}<\varepsilon_{i+1}<\underline{\delta}
for
0\leq i<2

such that

\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}})>{t_{0}}\lambda^{\varepsilon_{1}|m|}.

For
{t}\in(0,{t_{0}})
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
({t},\varepsilon_{2})
-disc at
c_{m}
is defined as

\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{m}}:=\mathbb{D}_{c_{m}}({t}\lambda^{\varepsilon_{2}|m|})\setminus\{c_{m}\}\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}}\setminus\{c_{m}\}.

Lemma 9.2
.

For
i\geq 1
, we have
F(\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-i}})\subset\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-i+1}}
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from the
\eta
-homogeneity of
F
.
∎

Let
p\in\Omega
. If
p\in\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-n}}
for some
n\geq 0
, let
e_{n}(p)>0
be the smallest integer such that

F^{-e_{n}(p)}(p)\notin\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-n-e_{n}(p)}},

and let
0\leq\operatorname{d}(p)\leq\infty
be the largest integer such that

p\in\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-\operatorname{d}(p)}}.

If, on the other hand,
p\notin\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-n}}
for all
n\geq 0
, then denote
\operatorname{d}(p)=\varnothing
.

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-n}}
. Then

e_{n}(p)<{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{2}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}\left(\operatorname{dist}(p,c_{-n})\right).

Proof.

By
\eta
-homogeneity, we have

\operatorname{dist}(F^{-i}(p),c_{-n-i})>\lambda^{\eta i}\operatorname{dist}(p,c_{-n}).

Since

\operatorname{radius}(\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-n-i}}):={t}\lambda^{\varepsilon_{2}(n+i)},

the claim follows.
∎

Lemma 9.4
.

If
c_{-n}\in\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{0}}
for some
n>0
, then

\operatorname{dist}(c_{-n},c_{0})>\left(\operatorname{radius}(\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-n}})\right)^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.10
, we have

\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}n}<\operatorname{dist}(c_{-n},c_{0}).

The claim now follows from the fact that

\left(\operatorname{radius}(\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-n}})\right)^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}={t}^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}\lambda^{\varepsilon_{2}{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}n}<\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}n}.

∎

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
p_{0}\in\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{0}}
. If
p_{-n}\in\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{0}}
for some
0<n<e_{0}(p_{0})
, then

\operatorname{dist}(p_{-n},c_{0})>\left(\operatorname{dist}(p_{0},c_{0})\right)^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}.

Proof.

Note that by Lemma
9.4

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}(p_{-n},c_{0})

\displaystyle>\operatorname{dist}(c_{-n},c_{0})-\operatorname{dist}(p_{-n},c_{-n})

\displaystyle>\left(\operatorname{radius}(\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-n}})\right)^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}-\operatorname{radius}(\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-n}})

\displaystyle\asymp\lambda^{\varepsilon_{2}{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}n}.

By
\eta
-homogeneity, we have

\operatorname{dist}(p_{0},c_{0})<\lambda^{-\eta n}\operatorname{dist}(p_{-n},c_{-n})<\lambda^{-\eta n}\operatorname{radius}(\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-n}})\asymp\lambda^{(\varepsilon_{2}-\eta)n}.

The result follows.
∎

Proposition 9.6
.

Let
p_{0}\in\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{0}}
. If
\operatorname{d}(p_{0})<\infty
, then there exists a smallest integer
0\leq\operatorname{e}(p)<\infty
such that
d\left(F^{-\operatorname{e}(p)}(p)\right)=\varnothing
. Moreover,
\operatorname{e}(p_{0})<(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1})e_{0}(p_{\operatorname{d}(p_{0})})
.

Proof.

Denote

q_{0}:=p_{\operatorname{d}(p_{0})}\in\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{0}}.

Note that
\operatorname{d}(q_{0})
may be greater than
0
.

Consider a sequence
\{n_{i}\}_{i=0}^{j}
defined as follows. Let
n_{0}:=0
. For
i\geq 0
, if

\operatorname{e}(q_{-n_{i}})>e_{0}(q_{-n_{i}}),

then let

n_{i+1}^{\prime}:=n_{i}+e_{0}(q_{-n_{i}}),

and let

n_{i}=n_{i}^{0}<n_{i}^{1}<\ldots<n_{i}^{k}\leq n_{i}^{\prime}

be a sequence of moments such that

q_{-n_{i}^{l}}\in\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{0}}
for
0\leq l\leq k.

Define
n_{i}=n_{i}^{l}
, where
0\leq l\leq k
is the integer such that
n_{i}^{l}+e_{0}(q_{-n_{i}^{l}})
is maximized.

We claim that
n_{i+1}>n_{i}
. Otherwise, we have

q_{-n}\not\in\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{0}}
for
n_{i}<n\leq n_{i+1}^{\prime},

and hence

\operatorname{d}(q_{-n_{i+1}^{\prime}})>\operatorname{e}_{0}(q_{-n_{i}}).

Denote

b_{i}:=n_{i+1}^{\prime}-\operatorname{d}(q_{-n_{i+1}^{\prime}}).

Then by Lemma
9.2
, we have

q_{-b_{i}}\in\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{0}}

and

b_{i}+\operatorname{e}_{0}(q_{-b_{i}})>n_{i+1}^{\prime}=n_{i}+\operatorname{e}_{0}(q_{-n_{i}}).

If
b_{i}>n_{k-1}\geq 0
for some
k\leq i
, then this contradicts the maximality of
n_{k}+\operatorname{e}_{0}(-n_{k})
. If
b_{i}<0
, then

\operatorname{d}(q_{-n_{i+1}^{\prime}})=n_{i+1}^{\prime}-b_{i}>n_{i+1}^{\prime}.

Hence,

\operatorname{d}(p_{0})=\operatorname{d}(q_{-\operatorname{d}(p_{0})})\geq\operatorname{d}(q_{-n_{i+1}^{\prime}})-n_{i+1}^{\prime}+\operatorname{d}(p_{0})>\operatorname{d}(p_{0}),

which is a contradiction. Thus, the claim holds.

Let

l_{i}:=\operatorname{dist}(c_{-n_{i}},c_{0}).

By Lemma
9.5
, we have

l_{i+1}>l_{i}^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}>l_{0}^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}^{i+1}}.

This immediately implies that
j<\infty
.

Denote

m_{i+1}:=n_{i+1}-n_{i}.

By
\eta
-homogeneity, we have

{t}\lambda^{\varepsilon_{2}m_{i}}=\operatorname{diam}(\mathbb{D}^{t}_{c_{-m_{i}}})>\operatorname{dist}(c_{-n_{i}},c_{-m_{i}})>\lambda^{\eta m_{i}}l_{i-1}.

So

m_{i+1}<{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{2}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}(l_{i})<{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{2}^{-1}{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}^{i}\log_{\lambda}(l_{0}).

Let

m_{j+1}:=\operatorname{e}_{0}(q_{-n_{j}}).

Then by Lemma
9.3
,

m_{j+1}<{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{2}^{-1}l_{j}<{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{2}^{-1}{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}^{j}\log_{\lambda}(l_{0}).

Finally, observe that by Proposition
5.10
, we have

\operatorname{e}_{0}(q_{0})>n_{1}>{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}(l_{0}).

Consequently,

\operatorname{e}(p_{0})\leq\operatorname{e}(q_{0})=\sum_{i=0}^{j+1}m_{i}<(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1})\operatorname{e}_{0}(q_{0})

as claimed.
∎

9.2.
Pinching at the critical point

Theorem 9.7
.

We have

\Lambda\cap\mathbb{D}_{c_{0}}(1/\overline{L_{0}})\subset\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}^{1/{\bar{\delta}}},

where
\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}^{1/{\bar{\delta}}}\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
is a
1/{\bar{\delta}}
-pinched regular neighborhood at
c_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
p_{0}\in\Lambda\cap\mathbb{D}_{c_{0}}(1/\overline{L_{0}})
. Write

z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0}).

Suppose towards a contradiction that
p_{0}\notin\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}^{1/{\bar{\delta}}}
. For
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, consider the
({t_{0}},{\bar{\varepsilon}})
-disc
\mathbb{D}_{c_{m}}^{t_{0}}
at
c_{m}
. By Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
3.9
, we have

|y_{-i}|>k\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}|y_{0}|>k\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}|x_{0}|^{1/{\bar{\delta}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq i<\operatorname{e}_{0}(p_{0})

for some uniform constant
k>0
. If

k\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}|x_{0}|^{1/{\bar{\delta}}}<{t_{0}}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}i}

then it follows by straightforward computation that

i<{\bar{\delta}}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|.

(9.1)

First, suppose that
\operatorname{d}(p_{0})=0
. By Proposition
9.6
and (
9.1
), we have

N:=\operatorname{e}(p_{0})<(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})\operatorname{e}_{0}(p_{0})<{\bar{\delta}}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|<\infty.

(9.2)

We also conclude from

\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}\overline{L_{0}}^{-1}<k\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}|y_{0}|<{t_{0}}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}i}

that

N>-\log_{\lambda}\overline{L_{0}}.

(9.3)

If
p_{0}=c_{-n}
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then for
l\in\mathbb{Z}
, denote

\tilde{E}_{p_{l}}^{v}:=E^{v}_{c_{-n+l}}.

Otherwise, denote

\tilde{E}_{p_{l}}^{v}:=E^{s}_{p_{l}}.

In the former case,
p_{-N}=c_{-(n+N)}
is
(n+N)
-times forward
(L_{0},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}_{p_{-N}}^{v}
by Proposition
9.1
. Consequently, by Proposition
3.4
, we see that
p_{1}=c_{-n+1}
is
(n-1)
-times forward
(L_{0}\lambda^{-{\bar{\delta}}N},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}_{p_{-n+1}}^{v}
. Similarly, in the latter case,
p_{-N}
is infinitely forward
(L_{0},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}_{p_{-N}}^{v}
, and thus,
p_{1}
is infinitely forward
(L_{0}\lambda^{-{\bar{\delta}}N},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}_{p_{-n+1}}^{v}
.

Let
M\in\mathbb{N}
be the largest integer such that

p_{1+i}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{1+i}}
for
0\leq i<M.

Then by Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
3.9
, we have

\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}M}<|x_{M}|<\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}M}|x_{1}|.

We also have

|y_{0}|<\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})N}.

(9.4)

If
|y_{0}|>|x_{0}|
, then
|x_{1}|\asymp|y_{0}|
. Hence,

\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}M}<\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}M}|y_{0}|<\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})N}.

It follows that
N<{\bar{\varepsilon}}M
. If
|y_{0}|<|x_{0}|
, then
|x_{1}|=O(|x_{0}|)
. So

\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}M}<\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}M}|x_{0}|<\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}M}|y_{0}|^{{\bar{\delta}}}<\lambda^{{\bar{\delta}}N}.

Hence, we again have
N<{\bar{\varepsilon}}M
.

Let

E^{v}_{p_{1}}:=D_{z_{1}}\Phi_{c_{1}}^{-1}(E^{gv}_{z_{1}}).\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\theta_{1}:=\left|\measuredangle(\tilde{E}_{p_{1}}^{v},E^{v}_{p_{1}})\right|.

By Proposition
3.11
, we have

\theta_{1}<L_{0}\lambda^{-{\bar{\delta}}N}\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})M}<L_{0}\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}N}<|y_{0}|^{1/{\bar{\varepsilon}}}.

Moreover, by Theorem
5.8
, we have

\theta_{0}<O\left(|x_{0}|+\theta_{1}\right).

If
|y_{0}|<|x_{0}|
, then

\theta_{0}<O\left(|x_{0}|\right).

This, (
9.2
), (
9.3
) and Proposition
6.2
contradict the
N
-times forward
(L_{0},\delta)_{v}
-regularity of
p_{-N}
along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-N}}
.

If
|y_{0}|>|x_{0}|
, then

\theta_{0}<O\left(|y_{0}|\right).

By Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
3.9
, we have

\theta_{-i}<K\lambda^{-(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}\theta_{0}<{\bar{K}}\lambda^{-(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})i}|y_{0}|.

(9.5)

Let
j\geq 0
be the smallest integer less than
\operatorname{e}_{0}(p_{0})
such that

\theta_{-j}>\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}j}\nu.

for some small
\nu>0
. By (
9.5
), we have

j>(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})(\log_{\lambda}|y_{0}|+\log_{\lambda}({\bar{K}})).

Thus, by (
9.4
), we see that

j>kN

for some uniform constant
k>0
. Finally, by Proposition
3.9
and (
9.3
), we have

\|DF^{-N}|_{\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{0}}}\|<\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}j}\lambda^{-(1+\eta)(N-j)}<\lambda^{-(1-k+{\bar{\varepsilon}})N}=\lambda^{(k-{\bar{\delta}})N}\lambda^{-(1-\delta)N}<L_{0}^{-1}\lambda^{-(1-\delta)N}.

This contradicts the
N
-times forward
(L_{0},\delta)_{v}
-regularity of
p_{-N}
along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-N}}
.

Finally, consider the case when
p_{0}\in\mathbb{D}_{c_{0}}(1/\overline{L_{0}})
, and
\operatorname{d}(p_{0})=n>0
. This means that
c_{-n}\in\mathbb{D}^{t_{0}}_{c_{0}}
. Write

(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(c_{-n}).

It follows by Proposition
5.10
that

\operatorname{radius}(D^{t_{0}}_{c_{-n}})<|x_{0}|^{1/{\bar{\varepsilon}}}.

Thus, by slightly increasing
{\bar{\delta}}
a uniform amount if necessary, we obtain

p_{0}\in D^{t_{0}}_{c_{-n}}\subset\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}^{1/{\bar{\delta}}}.

∎

9.3.
Refinement of regular unicriticality

Let

{t_{1}}:=1/\overline{L_{0}}<\underline{t_{0}}

be a sufficiently small uniform constant such that so that

\mathbb{D}^{t_{1}}_{c_{m}}\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}}\setminus\{c_{m}\}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptm\in\mathbb{Z}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Lambda\cap\mathbb{D}^{t_{1}}_{c_{0}}\subset\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}^{1/\underline{\delta}}.

Let
{L_{1}}:=L({t_{1}})\geq 1
be the regularity factor given in Definition
1.3
ii). By increasing
{L_{1}}
if necessary, we may assume that
{L_{1}}^{-\varepsilon}<{t_{1}}.

Denote

\delta>\delta^{\prime}:=(1-{\bar{\delta}})\delta>\underline{\delta}.

For
{t}\in(0,{t_{0}}]
and
n\geq 0
, let
\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}({t})
and
\mathcal{T}_{c_{1+n}}({t})
be the
1/\underline{\delta}
-pinched,
\delta^{\prime}
-truncated critical tunnel and valuable crescent at
c_{-n}
and
c_{1+n}
respectively of length
{t}
(see Subsection
6.1
).

Denote

\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{-n}}:=D_{0}\Phi_{c_{-n}}^{-1}(E^{gv}_{0})=DF^{-n}(E^{v}_{c_{0}})

and

\hat{E}^{h}_{c_{1+n}}:=D_{0}\Phi_{c_{1+n}}^{-1}(E^{gh}_{0})=DF^{1+n}(E^{v}_{c_{0}}).

Let
p\in\Lambda
. For
L\geq 1
, we say that
p
is
forward
(L,\delta)
-regular
(without specifying time or direction) if either

•

p=c_{-n}
for some
n\geq 0
, and
p
is
n
-times forward
(L,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{-n}}
; or

•

p\neq c_{-n}
for all
n\geq 0
, and
p
is infinitely forward
(L,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{p}:=E^{s}_{p}
.

Similarly, we say that
p
is
backward
(L,\delta)
-regular
if either

•

p=c_{n}
for some
n\geq 1
, and
p
is
n
-times backward
(L,\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{h}_{c_{n}}
; or

•

p\neq c_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
, and
p
is infinitely backward
(L,\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{h}_{p}:=E^{c}_{p}
.

Theorem 9.8

(Geometric criterion for regularity)
.

For
{t}\in(0,{L_{1}}^{-1}]
, let
L:=1/\underline{t}
. Then for all
p\in\Lambda
, the following statements hold.

a)

If

\displaystyle p\notin\bigcup_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}_{c_{-i}}({t}),

then
p
is forward
(L,\delta)
-regular. If
p
is also contained in
\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}({t})
, then
p
is forward
(1,\delta)
-regular.

b)

If

\displaystyle p\notin\bigcup_{i=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}_{c_{1+i}}({t}),

then
p
is backward
(L,\delta)
-regular. If
p
is also contained in
\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}({t})
, then
p
is backward
(1,\delta)
-regular.

c)

If

\displaystyle p\notin\bigcup_{i=-\infty}^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{T}_{c_{i}}({t})\cup\{c_{i}\}\right)

then
p
is Pesin
(L,\delta)
-regular.

Let
\varepsilon_{i}
with
i\in\{0,1,2\}
be constants satisfying

\varepsilon_{0}:=\varepsilon
and
{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{i}<\varepsilon_{i+1}<\underline{\delta}
for
0\leq i<2,

so that we have

\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}})>{t_{0}}\lambda^{\varepsilon_{1}|m|}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{diam}(\mathbb{D}^{t_{1}}_{c_{m}}):={t_{1}}\lambda^{\varepsilon_{2}|m|}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for all}\hskip 14.22636ptm\in\mathbb{Z}.

Let
p_{0}\in\Omega
. If
p_{0}\in\mathbb{D}^{{t_{1}}}_{c_{-n}}
for some
n\geq 0
, let
e_{n}(p_{0})>0
be the smallest integer such that

p_{-e_{n}(p_{0})}\notin\mathbb{D}^{{t_{1}}}_{c_{-n-e_{n}(p_{0})}},

and let
0\leq\operatorname{d}(p_{0})\leq\infty
be the largest integer such that

p_{0}\in\mathbb{D}^{{t_{1}}}_{c_{-\operatorname{d}(p)}}.

In this case,
0\leq\operatorname{e}(p_{0})<\infty
is the integer given by Proposition
9.6
(taking
{t}={t_{1}}
). If, on the other hand,
p_{0}\notin\mathbb{D}^{t_{1}}_{c_{-n}}
for all
n\geq 0
, then denote
\operatorname{d}(p_{0})=\varnothing
.

If
p_{0}\in\mathbb{D}^{2{t_{1}}}_{c_{-n}}
for some
n\geq 0
, let
\hat{d}(p_{0}),\hat{e}_{n}(p_{0})\geq 0
be the smallest integers so that

p_{0}\in\mathbb{D}^{2{t_{1}}}_{c_{-\hat{d}(p_{0})}}
and
p_{-\hat{e}_{n}(p_{0})}\notin\mathbb{D}^{2{t_{1}}}_{c_{-n-\hat{e}_{n}(p_{0})}}.

Otherwise, denote
\hat{d}(p_{0})=\varnothing
.

For
{t}\in(0,{t_{1}}]
, define the
{t}
-critical depth
\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{0})
and the
{t}
-valuable depth
\operatorname{vd}_{t}(p_{0})
of
p_{0}
as in Subsection
6.1
. Denote

\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}:=\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}({t_{1}})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptm\in\mathbb{Z};

and

\operatorname{cd}(p_{0}):=\operatorname{cd}_{{t_{1}}}(p_{0})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{vd}(p_{0}):=\operatorname{vd}_{{t_{1}}}(p_{0}).

Lemma 9.9
.

Let
p_{-n}\in\mathbb{D}^{2{t_{1}}}_{c_{-n}}
for some
n\geq 0
. Then one of the following statements hold.

i)

We have
\operatorname{cd}(p_{-n})\in\{\varnothing,\infty\}
, and

\operatorname{cd}(p_{1})=\operatorname{cd}(p_{-n}).

ii)

We have
\operatorname{cd}(p_{-n})=-n
, and

\operatorname{cd}(p_{1})=\varnothing.

iii)

We have
{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}^{-1}n<\operatorname{cd}(p_{-n})<\infty
, and

\operatorname{cd}(p_{1})=\operatorname{cd}(p_{-n})-n-1.

Proof.

Suppose that
p_{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-k}}
for some
k\geq 0
. Then by Proposition
5.10
, we have
k>{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}^{-1}n
. Observe that, for some uniform constant
K\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}(c_{-k+1},c_{0})

\displaystyle<K\operatorname{dist}(c_{-k},p_{1})+\operatorname{dist}(p_{0},c_{0})

\displaystyle<K\operatorname{dist}(c_{-k},p_{1})+\lambda^{-\eta n}\operatorname{dist}(p_{-n},c_{-n})

\displaystyle<\lambda^{{\bar{\delta}}k}+2{t_{1}}\lambda^{(\varepsilon_{2}-\eta)n}

\displaystyle<\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{2}n}.

Thus, by Lemma
5.11
, we have

p_{-n}\in F^{-n-1}(\mathcal{T}_{-k})=\mathcal{T}_{c_{-k-n-1}}.

∎

Lemma 9.10
.

Let
p\in\Omega
. If
\hat{d}(p)\neq\varnothing
, then
\operatorname{d}(c_{-\hat{d}(p)})=\varnothing
.

Proof.

Denote
\hat{d}:=\hat{d}(p)
. Suppose
c_{-\hat{d}}\in\mathbb{D}^{t_{1}}_{c_{-n}}
for some
n\geq 0
. By Lemma
9.9
, we see that
\hat{d}>{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}^{-1}n
. Thus,

\displaystyle\operatorname{dist}(p,c_{-n})

\displaystyle<\operatorname{dist}(p,c_{-\hat{d}})+\operatorname{dist}(c_{-\hat{d}},c_{-n})

\displaystyle<2{t_{1}}\lambda^{\varepsilon_{2}\hat{d}}+{t_{1}}\lambda^{\varepsilon_{2}n}

\displaystyle<2{t_{1}}\lambda^{\varepsilon_{2}n}.

So
p\in\mathbb{D}^{2{t_{1}}}_{c_{-n}}
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Let

\tilde{N}_{1}:=-\delta^{-1}\log_{\lambda}\overline{L_{1}},

and let
N_{1}
be the first Pliss moment larger than
\tilde{N}_{1}
in the forward orbit of
c_{1}
. By Proposition
4.2
, we have

\tilde{N}_{1}\leq N_{1}<(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1})\tilde{N}_{1}.

Denote

{t_{2}}:=\operatorname{radius}(U_{c_{1}}^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}N_{1}})\asymp\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}N_{1}}={L_{1}}^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}<{t_{1}},

and let

\mathbf{T}_{c_{n}}:=F^{n-1}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}({t_{2}}))\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq n\leq N_{1}.

Lemma 9.11
.

Let
p_{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}\setminus\mathbf{T}_{c_{1}}
.
If
\operatorname{d}(p_{1})=\varnothing
, then

\hat{e}(p_{1})<-{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}\log_{\lambda}{L_{1}}.

Proof.

Letting
t=2{t_{1}}
and applying Lemma
9.3
, we have

\hat{e}_{0}(p_{0})<{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{2}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}{t_{2}}=-{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}\log_{\lambda}{L_{1}}.

The result now follows from Proposition
9.6
.
∎

Lemma 9.12
.

Let
p_{N_{1}}\in\mathbf{T}_{c_{N_{1}}}
be
n
-times forward
({\overline{L_{1}}},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{N_{1}}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{N_{1}}}
with

n>-\log_{\lambda}{\overline{L_{1}}}.

Then
p_{1}
is
(n+N_{1})
-times forward
(1,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}
. Moreover,
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
.

Proof.

Since
p_{1}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}N_{1}}
, we see that
p_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq M
with

M>\varepsilon_{1}^{-1}({\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}N_{1})>{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}^{-1}N_{1}.

By Proposition
3.11
, we have for some uniform constant
K\geq 1
:

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{N_{1}}},E^{v}_{p_{N_{1}}})<K\lambda^{-\varepsilon_{1}N_{1}}{\overline{L_{1}}}\lambda^{(1-\delta)n}<K{L_{1}}^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}{\overline{L_{1}}}\lambda^{(1-\delta)n}<{\overline{L_{1}}}^{-1}<\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}N_{1}}.

Thus,
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{N_{1}}}
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{N_{1}}}
.

By Proposition
3.9
, we conclude that
p_{1}
is
N_{1}
-times forward
(1,{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1})_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}
. Since

\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1})N_{1}}{\overline{L_{1}}}\lambda^{(1-\delta)i}={\overline{L_{1}}}\lambda^{(\delta-{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1})N_{1}}\lambda^{(1-\delta)(N_{1}+i)}<\lambda^{(1-\delta)(N_{1}+i)}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pti\geq 0,

the result follows.
∎

Lemma 9.13
.

Let
p_{1}\in\mathbf{T}_{c_{1}}
. Then one of the following statements hold.

i)

We have
\operatorname{cd}(p_{1})\in\{\varnothing,\infty\}
, and

\operatorname{cd}(p_{N_{1}})=\operatorname{cd}(p_{1}).

ii)

We have
{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}^{-1}N_{1}<\operatorname{cd}(p_{1})<\infty
, and

\operatorname{cd}(p_{N_{1}})=\operatorname{cd}(p_{1})-N_{1}+1.

Proof.

Since
N_{1}
is a Pliss moment, and

\operatorname{dist}(c_{0},c_{m-N_{1}})<\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}N_{1}},

it follows from Lemma
5.11
that either

\operatorname{cd}(p_{1})=\varnothing
or
\operatorname{cd}(p_{1})>{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}^{-1}N_{1}.

Suppose
p_{N_{1}}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
for some
n>{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}^{-1}N_{1}
. Then by Proposition
6.8
, we have

\operatorname{dist}(c_{-n-N_{1}},c_{0})<\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n-N_{1}}})+\operatorname{diam}(\mathbf{T}_{c_{1}})<\lambda^{\delta(n+N_{1})}+\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}N_{1}}<\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}N_{1}}.

Thus, by Lemma
5.11
, the result follows.
∎

Lemma 9.14
.

Let
p_{1}\in\Lambda\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}
be a point such that
\operatorname{cd}(p_{1})=\varnothing
,
and let
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{1}}
be sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. Then for all
n\geq 1
such that
d_{\mathcal{T}}(p_{-n})=\varnothing
, the point
p_{-n}
is
n
-times forward
({L_{1}}^{1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}
.

Proof.

Let
-j_{0}<0
be the tunnel escape moment of
p_{0}
, and
\hat{e}_{0}:=\hat{e}_{0}(p_{0})
. By Proposition
6.13
and Lemma
9.10
, we see that for
j_{0}\leq i\leq\hat{e}_{0}
such that
\hat{d}(p_{-i})=i
, the point
p_{-i}
is
i
-times
(O({L_{1}}),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-i}}
.

Let
m_{1}>j_{0}
be the smallest integer such that
p_{-m_{1}}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
. We claim that
\hat{d}(p_{-m_{1}+1})=m_{1}+1
. Otherwise, there exists
0<m_{1}^{\prime}<j_{0}
such that

\hat{d}(p_{-m_{1}^{\prime}-i})=-i\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq i\leq m_{1}.

Then by Lemma
9.9
, we must have

j_{0}>\hat{e}_{0}(p_{-m_{1}^{\prime}})>m_{1},

which is a contradiction.

If
p_{-m_{1}}\notin\mathbf{T}_{c_{0}}
, then by Lemma
9.11
, we see that for

0\leq i\leq l\leq\hat{e}_{1}:=\hat{e}(p_{-m_{1}}),

we have

\|DF^{i}|_{\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-m_{1}-l}}}\|<\lambda^{-\eta i}=\lambda^{-(1-\delta+\eta)i}\lambda^{(1-\delta)i}\leq{L_{1}}^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}\lambda^{(1-\delta)i}.

Moreover, by Proposition
6.13
and Lemma
9.10
, the point
p_{-m_{1}+1}
is
(m_{1}-1)
-times
(O({L_{1}}),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-m_{1}+1}}
. Hence,
p_{-m_{1}-l}
is
(m_{1}+l)
-times
({L_{1}}^{1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-m_{1}-l}}
.

Suppose instead that
p_{-m_{1}}\in\mathbf{T}_{c_{0}}
, so that
p_{-m_{1}+N_{1}}\in\mathbf{T}_{c_{N_{1}}}
. By Proposition
6.13
, Lemma
9.10
and Lemma
9.13
, we see that
p_{-m_{1}+N_{1}}
is
(m_{1}-N_{1})
-times
(O({L_{1}}),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-m_{1}+N_{1}}}
. By Lemma
9.12
, the point
p_{-m_{1}+1}
is
(m_{1}-1)
-times
(1,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-m_{1}+1}}
, and in particular, is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
.

Proceeding by induction, the result follows.
∎

Lemma 9.15
.

If

p\in\Lambda\setminus\bigcup_{i\geq 0}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}_{c_{-i}}({L_{1}}^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}}),

then
p
is forward
({L_{1}}^{1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}},\delta)
-regular. If
p
is also contained in
\mathbf{T}_{c_{1}}=\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}({L_{1}}^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}})
, then
p
is forward
(1,\delta)
-regular.

Proof.

Let
p_{0}\in\Lambda\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
. If
p_{0}\notin\mathbf{T}_{c_{0}}
, then by Lemma
9.11
, we see that for

0\leq i\leq l\leq\hat{e}(p_{0}),

we have

\|DF^{i}|_{\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-l}}}\|<\lambda^{-\eta i}=\lambda^{-(1-\delta+\eta)i}\lambda^{(1-\delta)i}\leq{L_{1}}^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}\lambda^{(1-\delta)i}.

If
\operatorname{d}(p_{0})=\varnothing
, then this implies that
p_{-l}
is forward
({L_{1}}^{1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}},\delta)
-regular. Otherwise,
d_{1}:=\operatorname{d}(p_{0})>0
, and by Lemma
9.5
, we have

\operatorname{dist}(p_{d_{1}},c_{0})<\operatorname{dist}(p_{0},c_{0})^{1/{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}.

Proceeding by induction, we obtain a sequence
0=:d_{0}<d_{1}<\ldots<d_{M}
with
M\geq 0
such that
d_{i+1}:=\operatorname{d}(p_{d_{i}})
, and either
\operatorname{d}(p_{d_{M}})=\varnothing
, or
p_{d_{M}}\in\mathbf{T}_{c_{0}}
. In the former case, we can argue as before to conclude that
p_{d_{M}-l}
is forward
({L_{1}}^{1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}},\delta)
-regular for all
0\leq l\leq\hat{e}(p_{d_{M}})
.

It remains to prove the result for
p_{0}\in\mathbf{T}_{c_{0}}
satisfying
\operatorname{cd}(p_{0})<\infty
. By Lemma
9.9
ii) and Lemma
9.13
, we have

\operatorname{cd}(p_{d_{\mathcal{T}}(p_{0})+N_{1}})=\varnothing.

Thus, by replacing
p_{N_{1}}
with
p_{\operatorname{cd}(p_{N_{1}})+N_{1}}
, we may assume without loss of generality that
\operatorname{cd}(p_{N_{1}})=\varnothing
.

If there exists
M\geq 0
such that
p_{M}\in\mathbf{T}_{c_{N_{1}}}
and
\operatorname{d}(p_{M})=\varnothing
, then the result follows by Lemma
9.12
and Lemma
9.14
. Suppose this is not the case. Then there exists an infinite sequence
0\leq M_{1}<\ldots
such that

p_{M_{i}}\in\mathbf{T}_{c_{N_{1}}},\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{cd}(p_{M_{i}})=\varnothing\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{d}(p_{M_{i}})=M_{i+1}-M_{i}-N_{i}.

Let
E^{i}_{p_{M_{i}}}:=E^{v}_{p_{M_{i}}}
, so that

D\Phi_{c_{N_{1}}}\left(E^{i}_{p_{M_{i}}}\right)=E^{gv}_{\Phi_{c_{N_{1}}}(p_{M_{i}})}.

By Lemma
9.12
and Lemma
9.14
,
p_{0}
is
M_{i}
-times forward
({L_{1}}^{1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{i}_{p_{0}}
. Hence, by Proposition
3.11
,
E^{i}_{p_{0}}
converge exponentially fast as
i\to\infty
to
E^{s}_{p_{0}}
, along which
p_{0}
is forward
({L_{1}}^{1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}},\delta)_{v}
-regular.
∎

Let
p_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
. Write

(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0}).

Denote

\tilde{l}:=(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1})\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|.

By Lemma
4.2
, there exists a Pliss moment
-l<0
in the backward orbit of
c_{0}
such that

\tilde{l}\leq l<(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1})\tilde{l}.

Such a moment is referred to as a
direction aligning moment for
p_{0}
.

Lemma 9.16
.

Let
p_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
, and let
-l<0
be a direction aligning moment for
p_{0}
. If
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{1}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
, then
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n}}
for
l\leq n<\hat{e}_{0}(p_{0})
. If, additionally, we have

p_{-n}\in\mathbf{T}_{c_{1}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\hat{d}(p_{-n})=n,

then
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
.

Proof.

The first claim follows immediately from Proposition
6.13
ii).

Write

(x_{1},y_{1}):=\Phi_{c_{1}}(p_{-n+1})
and
(\tilde{x}_{1},\tilde{y}_{1}):=\Phi_{c_{1}}(c_{-n+1}).

By Lemma
9.4
, we have

|x_{1}-\tilde{x}_{1}|^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}<|\tilde{x}_{1}|.

(9.6)

Consequently,
c_{-n+1}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}N_{1}}
. Moreover, by Lemma
9.10
and Lemma
9.13
, we have
\operatorname{d}(c_{-m+N_{1}})=\varnothing
. Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
9.12
, we see that
c_{-n+1}
is forward
(1,\delta)
-regular, and hence,
\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{-n+1}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. Since
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n+1}}
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n+1}}
, (
9.6
) implies that
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-n+1}}
is also sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
.
∎

Lemma 9.17
.

Let
p_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
, and let
-l<0
be a direction aligning moment for
p_{0}
. If
p_{-n}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
for some
1\leq n\leq\hat{e}_{0}(p_{0})
, then either
n>l
or
l>\hat{e}_{0}(p_{-n})
.

Proof.

The result follows from a similar argument as the one used in the proof of Lemma
9.9
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
9.8
a).

Let
{t}\in(0,{L_{1}}^{-1}]
. Consider
\mathbf{p}\in\Lambda
such that

M:=\operatorname{cd}(\mathbf{p})\geq 0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{cd}_{t}(\mathbf{p})=\varnothing.

Then we have

p_{1}:=F^{M+1}(\mathbf{p})\in\mathbf{T}_{c_{1}}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}({t}),

and by Lemma
9.9
and Lemma
9.13
,

\operatorname{cd}(p_{1})=\operatorname{cd}(p_{N_{1}})=\varnothing=\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{1}).

Thus, it follows from Lemma
9.12
that
p_{1}
is forward
(1,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}
, and
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
.

Let
-l_{0}<0
be a direction aligning moment for
p_{0}
. By Lemma
9.16
, for

l_{0}\leq n\leq\hat{e}_{0}(p_{0}),

the direction
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-n}}
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n}}
. Denote the vertical direction at
p_{-n}
in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n}}
by
\tilde{E}^{0}_{p_{-n}}
, so that

D\Phi_{c_{-n}}(\tilde{E}^{0}_{p_{-n}})=E^{gv}_{\Phi_{c_{-n}}(p_{-n})}.

Let
m_{0}:=0
, and for
i\geq 1
, let

m_{i-1}+l_{i-1}<m_{i}<m_{i-1}+\hat{e}_{0}(p_{-m_{i-1}})

be an integer such that

p_{-m_{i}}\in\mathbf{T}_{c_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}({t})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\hat{d}(p_{-m_{i}})=-m_{i}+m_{i-1}.

Write

z^{i}=(x^{i},y^{i}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{-m_{i}}).

Then we have by Lemma
9.5
,

|x^{i}|>|x^{i-1}|^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}}.

(9.7)

Let

-l_{i}=-(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1})\log_{\lambda}|x^{i}|,

be a direction aligning moment for
p_{-m_{i}}
. By Lemma
9.16
,
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-m_{i}+1}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
, and for

l_{i}\leq n\leq\hat{e}_{0}(p_{-m_{i}}),

the direction
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-m_{i}-n}}
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n}}
.

We claim that the initially given point
p_{-M}=\mathbf{p}
is forward
(L,\delta)
regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-M}}
with
L=1/\underline{{t}}
. Let

0:=m_{0}<m_{1}<\ldots<m_{I}\leq M

with
I\geq 0
be a maximal sequence of returns to
\mathbf{T}_{c_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}({t})
as specified above.

For
0\leq i<I
, denote

n_{i}:=m_{i+1}-m_{i}.

Since for
1\leq n\leq n_{i}-l_{i}
, the direction
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-m_{i+1}+n}}
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n_{i}+n}}
, and
c_{-n_{i}}
is forward
(1,\delta)
-regular, we conclude that
p_{-m_{i+1}}
is
(n_{i}-l_{i})
-times forward
(1,\delta)
-regular. It follows that
p_{-m_{i+1}}
is
n_{i}
-times forward
(L_{i},\delta)
-regular, where

L_{i}<\lambda^{-(1-\delta-\eta)l_{i}}.

Denote

n_{I}:=M-m_{I}.

If
n_{I}>l_{I}
, then for
0\leq n\leq n_{I}-l_{I}
, the direction
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-m_{i+1}+n}}
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n_{i}+n}}
. Moreover, since

\hat{d}(p_{-M})=n_{i},

the point
c_{-n_{i}}
is forward
({L_{1}},\delta)
