-regular. Thus,
p_{-M}
is
(n_{I}-l_{i})
-times forward
({L_{1}},\delta)
-regular. It follows that (whether
n_{I}>l_{I}
or not)
p_{-M}
is
n_{I}
-times forward
({L_{1}}L_{I},\delta)
-regular, where

L_{I}<\lambda^{-(1-\delta-\eta)l_{I}}.

Lastly, observe that by (
9.7
), we have

l_{i}<{\bar{\varepsilon}}_{1}l_{i-1}
for
1\leq i\leq I,

and hence

\sum_{i=0}^{I}l_{i}<Kl_{0}<-K\log_{\lambda}|x^{0}|<K\log_{\lambda}{t}.

(9.8)

Concatenating the above regularities, we conclude that
p_{-M}
is forward
({L_{1}}L^{\prime},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-M}}
, where

{L_{1}}L^{\prime}<{t}^{-1}\lambda^{-(1-\delta-\eta)\sum_{i=0}^{I}l_{i}}<1/\underline{{t}}.

∎

Let
N\geq 0
and
K\geq 1
. We say that a point
p_{0}\in\Omega
is
N
-times consistently forward
(K,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{0}}
if, for all
0\leq i\leq N
, the point
p_{i}
is
(N-i)
-times forward
(K,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{i}}
.

Lemma 9.18
.

If for some
N\geq 0
and
K\geq 1
, a point
p_{0}\in\Omega
is
N
-times consistently forward
(K,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{0}}
, then
p_{i}
is
(i,N-i)
-times
(K,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{i}}
.

Lemma 9.19
.

Let
p_{0}\in\Omega
be
M
-times backward
(K,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{0}}
for some
M\geq 0
and
K\geq 1
. If
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{-M}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{-M}}
is a direction satisfying

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{-M}},\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-M}})>k

for some uniform constant
k>0
, then
p_{0}
is
M
-times backward
({\bar{K}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{0}}
. Moreover, we have

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{0}},\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{0}})>{\bar{K}}^{-1}.

Let
\delta_{1}=\underline{\delta}
, so that
\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
is a
1/\underline{\delta_{1}}
-pinched neighborhood. Denote

\delta_{1}^{\prime}:=(1-{\bar{\delta}}_{1})\delta_{1}.

For
{t}\in(0,{L_{1}}^{-1}]
and
n\geq 0
, let
{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-n}}({t})
be the
n
-times
1/\delta_{1}
-pinched,
\delta_{1}^{\prime}
-truncated tunnel of length
{t}
. Numbers
-l<-j<0
are the
slow and fast tunnel escape moment for
p_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}({t})
respectively if
l
and
j
are the smallest integers such that

p_{-l}\notin{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-l}}({t})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptp_{-j}\notin\mathcal{T}_{c_{-j}}({t}).

If
p\in{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-n}}({t})
for some
n\geq 0
, then denote by

d:=d_{{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{-}({t})}(p)\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0,\infty\}

the largest integer such that
p\in{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-d}}({t})
. Otherwise, denote

d_{{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{-}({t})}(p)=\varnothing.

Lemma 9.20
.

Let
{t}\in(0,{L_{1}}^{-1}]
and
L:=1/\underline{{t}}
. For
p_{0}\in\Lambda\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}({t})
, let
-l<-j<0
be the slow and fast tunnel escape moment for
p_{0}
. Suppose
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{0}},\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{0}}
are directions such that
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
; the point
p_{-l}
is
M
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{-l}}
for some
M\geq 0
; and

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-l}},\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{-l}})>{\bar{L}}^{-1}.

Then for
n\in\{0,j\}
, the point
p_{-n}
is
(M+l-n)
-times backward
(1,\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{0}}
. Moreover,

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{-j}},\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-j}})>k

for some uniform constant
k>0
, and
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{0}}
is sufficiently center-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
.

Proof.

Write

z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0})
;
E^{v}_{p_{0}}:=D_{z_{0}}\Phi_{c_{0}}^{-1}(E^{gv}_{z_{0}})
and
\tilde{E}^{h}_{z_{0}}:=D\Phi_{c_{0}}(\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{0}}).

Then

l=(1+{\bar{\delta}})\delta_{1}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptj<\underline{\delta}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|.

By Proposition
6.13
and Proposition
3.11
, we have

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-l}},E^{v}_{p_{-l}})<\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})l}<|x_{0}|^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})/\delta_{1}}<{\bar{L}}^{-(1-{\bar{\delta}})/\delta_{1}}\ll{\bar{L}}^{-1}

(9.9)

Hence,

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{-l}},E^{v}_{p_{-l}})>{\bar{L}}^{-1}.

Denote

\theta^{v/h}_{0}:=\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{h}_{z_{0}},E^{gv/gh}_{z_{0}}).

For
0\leq i\leq l
, if
\theta^{v}_{-i}<k
for some uniform constant
k>0
, then by Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
3.9
, we have

\theta^{v}_{-i+1}>\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})}\theta^{v}_{-i}.

(9.10)

Similarly, if
\theta^{h}_{-i}<k
, then

\theta^{h}_{-i+1}>\lambda^{1-{\bar{\varepsilon}}}\theta^{h}_{-i}.

(9.11)

Let
I_{1},I_{2}
be the smallest integers such that

\theta^{v}_{-l+I_{1}}>k
and
\theta^{h}_{-l+I_{2}}<k.

Inequalities (
9.10
) and (
9.11
) imply

I_{1}<-\log_{\lambda}{\bar{L}}<K\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptI_{2}<I_{1}+K

for some uniform constant
K\geq 1
.

Let
N>0
be the largest integer such that

\theta_{-N}^{h}<\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}(l-N)}.

By a straightforward computation, we obtain

N>l-I_{2}-{\bar{\varepsilon}}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|>\delta_{1}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|.

Consequently,

l-N<{\bar{\delta}}_{1}N\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptN-j>(1-\underline{\delta}^{-1}\delta_{1})N=(1-{\bar{\delta}}_{1})N.

Note that for
0\leq i\leq N
, the direction
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{-i}}
is sufficiently horizontal in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-i}}
.

By Proposition
3.9
,
p_{-n}
with
n\in\{0,j\}
is
(N-n)
-times backward
(1,{\bar{\varepsilon}})_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{-n}}
. For
0\leq i<l-N
, we have

\displaystyle\|D_{p_{-j}}F^{-(N+i-j)}\|

\displaystyle<\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}(N-j)}\lambda^{-(1+\eta)(l-N)}

\displaystyle<\lambda^{-{\bar{\delta}}_{1}N}

\displaystyle<\lambda^{-{\bar{\delta}}_{1}N}\lambda^{\delta(N+i-j)}\lambda^{-\delta(N+i-j)}

\displaystyle<\lambda^{\delta(1-{\bar{\delta}}_{1})N}\lambda^{-\delta(N+i-j)}

\displaystyle<{t}^{1/{\bar{\delta}}_{1}}\lambda^{-\delta(N+i-j)}

\displaystyle<{\bar{L}}^{-1}\lambda^{-\delta(N+i-j)}.

Concatenating with the
M
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regularity of
p_{-l}
along
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{-l}}
, we see that the point
p_{-n}
with
n\in\{0,j\}
is
(M+l-n)
-times backward
(1,\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{h}_{p_{-n}}
.

The other claims follow immediately from Lemma
9.19
and Proposition
6.12
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
9.8
b).

For
{t}\in(0,{L_{1}}^{-1}]
, let
L:=1/\underline{{t}}
be the forward regularity factor given in part a). Let
p_{0}\in\Lambda
be a point satisfying

\operatorname{vd}_{t}(p_{0})=\varnothing.

We prove that
p_{0}
is backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)
-regular.

First, suppose

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{0})=\varnothing,

so that
p_{0}
is forward
(L,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{0}}
. Let
N\geq 0
be an integer such that

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{-N})=\varnothing;

and let
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{-N}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{-N}}
be a direction satisfying

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{-N}},\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-N}})>k

for some uniform constant
k>0
. We claim that
p_{0}
is
N
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{0}}
.

Let
0\leq m_{1}<\ldots<m_{I}<N
for some
I\geq 0
be the sequence of all integers satisfying

p_{-m_{i}}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}({t})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{-m_{i}})=\varnothing.

Let
-l_{i}<-j_{i}<0
be the slow and fast tunnel escape moment for
p_{-m_{i}-1}
, and let
n_{i}>1
be the crescent escape moment for
p_{-m_{i}}
. By Proposition
6.9
, we have

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{-m_{i}-l_{i}})=\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{-m_{i}-j_{i}})=\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{-m_{i}+n_{i}})=\varnothing,

and

m_{i}+l_{i}<m_{i+1}-n_{i+1}.

Hence,

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{-m})=\varnothing\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptm_{i}+j_{i}\leq k\leq m_{i+1}-n_{i+1}.

By Lemma
9.18
,
p_{-m_{I}-l_{I}}
is
(N-m_{I}-l_{I})
-times backward
(L,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{-m_{I}-l_{I}}
. Applying Lemma
9.19
, Lemma
9.20
and Proposition
6.14
, we see that
p_{-m_{I}+n_{I}}
is
(N-m_{I}+n_{I})
-times backward
(O(1),\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{-m_{I}+n_{I}}}
, and

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{-m_{I}+n_{I}}},\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-m_{I}+n_{I}}})>k.

Proceeding by induction, we conclude that
p_{-m_{1}+n_{1}}
is
(N-m_{1}+n_{1})
-times backward
(O(1),\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{-m_{1}+n_{1}}}
, and

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{-m_{1}+n_{1}}},\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-m_{1}+n_{1}}})>k.

By Lemma
9.18
and Lemma
9.19
,
p_{0}
is
(m_{1}-n_{1})
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{0}}
. Concatenating the regularities, it follows that
p_{0}
is
N
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{0}}
.

Now, suppose

0\leq M:=\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{0})<\infty.

Denote
m_{0}:=M
. Let
-l_{0}<-j_{0}<0
be the slow and fast tunnel escape time for
p_{m_{0}}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}({t})
. Then

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{m_{0}+1})=\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{m_{0}-j_{0}})=\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{m_{0}-l_{0}})=\varnothing.

Observe that
j_{0}>m_{0}
. Let
m_{1}
be the largest integer less than
m_{0}
such that
p_{m_{1}}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}({t})
, and if
-l_{1}<-j_{1}<0
are the slow and fast tunnel escape time for
p_{m_{1}}
, then
j_{1}>m_{1}
. Clearly, we have

p_{m_{1}}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{m_{1}-m_{0}}}({t})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{cd}_{t}(c_{m_{1}-m_{0}})=\varnothing.

Moreover, by Proposition
6.9
, we have

0>m_{1}-l_{1}>m_{0}-j_{0}.

Proceeding by induction, we obtain the maximal sequence
M=:m_{0}>m_{1}>\ldots>m_{I}>0
with
I\geq 0
, such that for
0<i\leq I
, we have

p_{m_{i}}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{m_{i}-m_{i-1}}}({t})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{cd}_{t}(c_{m_{i}-m_{i-1}+1})=\varnothing;

and if
-l_{i}<-j_{i}<0
are the slow and fast tunnel escape time for
p_{m_{i}}
, then

j_{i}>m_{i}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt0>m_{i}-l_{i}>m_{i-1}-j_{i-1}.

Let
\hat{E}^{i}_{p_{m_{i}}}
be the vertical direction at
p_{m_{i}}
in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
, so that

D\Phi_{c_{0}}(\hat{E}^{i}_{p_{m_{i}}})=E^{gv}_{\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{m_{i}})}.

Then by the assumption on the critical depth of
c_{m_{i}-m_{i-1}+1}
, the direction
\hat{E}^{i-1}_{p_{m_{i}+1}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. Hence, by Lemma
6.13
,
p_{m_{i}-l_{i}}
is
l_{i}
-times forward
(1,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{i-1}_{p_{m_{i}-l_{i}}}
.

Let
N\geq l_{0}-m_{0}
be an integer such that

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{-N})=\varnothing;

and let
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{-N}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{-N}}
be a direction satisfying

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{-N}},\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-N}})>k

for some uniform constant
k>0
. We claim that
p_{0}
is
N
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{0}}
.

Denote

\hat{E}^{-1}_{p_{0}}:=\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{0}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptk_{0}:=N-(l_{0}-m_{0});

and

k_{i}:=(j_{i-1}-m_{i-1})-(l_{i}-m_{i})>0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt1\leq i\leq I.

Suppose that for
0\leq i\leq I
, the point
p_{m_{i}-l_{i}}
is
k_{i}
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{m_{i}-l_{i}}}
, and

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{m_{i}-l_{i}}},\hat{E}^{i-1}_{p_{m_{i}-l_{i}}})>{\bar{L}}^{-1}.

For
i=0
, this was proved in the previous case.

Observe that

p_{m_{i-1}-n}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}({t})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptn<j_{i-1};

and

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(c_{-m_{i-1}+m_{i}-n})=\varnothing\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptn\in\{-1,j_{i},l_{i}\}.

Hence,
\tilde{E}^{i-1}_{p_{m_{i}+1}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
, and
p_{m_{i}-l_{i}}
is
l_{i}
-times forward
(1,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{i-1}_{p_{m_{i}+1}}
. By Lemma
9.20
,
p_{m_{i}-j_{i}}
is
(k_{i}+l_{i}-j_{i})
-times backward
(1,\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{m_{i}-j_{i}}}
, and

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{m_{i}-j_{i}}},\hat{E}^{i}_{p_{m_{i}-j_{i}}})>k.

Since

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(c_{-m_{i}+m_{i+1}-l_{i+1}})=\varnothing,

it follows from the previous case that
p_{m_{i+1}-l_{i+1}}
is
k_{i+1}
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{m_{i+1}-l_{i+1}}}
, and

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{m_{i+1}-l_{i+1}}},\hat{E}^{i}_{p_{m_{i+1}-l_{i+1}}})>{\bar{L}}^{-1}.

By induction, we see that
p_{m_{I}-l_{j}}
is
(N-(j_{I}-m_{I}))
-times backward
(1,\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{m_{I}-j_{I}}}
, and

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{m_{I}-j_{I}}},\hat{E}^{I}_{p_{m_{I}-j_{I}}})>k.

Since

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(c_{-m_{I}})=\varnothing,

by applying the previous case again, we conclude that
p_{0}
is
N
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{m_{I}-j_{I}}}
.

Lastly, suppose that

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{0})=\infty.

Let
D\geq 0
be an integer such that
p_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-D}}({t})
. Note that

d_{{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{-}({t})}(c_{-D})\neq\infty.

Moreover, since

\operatorname{vd}_{t}(p_{0})=\varnothing,

we may assume by Proposition
3.9
that

\operatorname{vd}_{t}(c_{-D})=\varnothing.

Let
N>0
be an integer such that
-(D+N)<0
is the slow tunnel escape moment for
p_{D}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
. Observe that

\operatorname{dist}(p_{D},c_{0})<\lambda^{-\eta D}\operatorname{dist}(p_{0},c_{-D})<\lambda^{(1+{\bar{\delta}})\delta D}

for
D
sufficiently large by Lemma
6.8
. Consequently,

N>(1-{\bar{\delta}})\delta_{1}^{-1}\delta D>{\bar{\delta}}_{1}^{-1}D.

Let
\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{c_{0}}
be the vertical direction at
c_{0}
in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
, so that

D\Phi_{c_{0}}(\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{0}})=E^{gv}_{0}.

Let
\tilde{E}^{N}_{c_{-(D+N)}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{c_{-(D+N)}}
be a direction satisfying

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{N}_{c_{-(D+N)}},\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{-(D+N)}})>k.

By the previous two cases, we see that
c_{-D}
is
N
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{c_{-D}}
. By Proposition
3.9
, there exists
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{0}}
such that
p_{0}
is
N
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{0}}
.

We proved that for
N
arbitrarily large, there exists
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{0}}
such that
p_{0}
is
N
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{0}}
. By Proposition
3.12
, we conclude that as
N\to\infty
, the direction
\tilde{E}^{N}_{p_{0}}
converges exponentially fast to
E^{c}_{p_{0}}
, along which
p_{0}
is infinitely backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{h}
-regular.
∎

Proof of Theorem
9.8
c).

For
{t}\in(0,{L_{1}}^{-1}]
, let
L:=1/\underline{{t}}
. Let
\mathbf{p}\in\Lambda
be a point satisfying

\operatorname{vd}_{t}(\mathbf{p})=\operatorname{cd}_{t}(\mathbf{p})=\varnothing.

By Theorem
9.8
a),
\mathbf{p}
is forward
(L,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{\mathbf{p}}
. We show that
\mathbf{p}
is also backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{\mathbf{p}}
.

Let
-m<0
be the first moment in the backward orbit of
\mathbf{p}
such that

p_{0}:=F^{-m}(\mathbf{p})\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}({t}).

Write

z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0}).

Let
-l<-j<0
be the slow and fast tunnel escape moment of
p_{0}
respectively, and let
n>0
be the crescent escape moment of
p_{1}
. Recall that

l>\underline{\delta}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|>\underline{\delta}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}L\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptj,n>\delta^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|>-k\delta^{-1}\log_{\lambda}L

for some uniform constant
k>0
. Observe that

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{i})=\varnothing
for
1\leq i\leq m.

Moreover,

\operatorname{cd}_{t}(p_{i})=\operatorname{vd}_{t}(p_{i})=\varnothing\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pti\in\{-j,-l\}.

By Theorem
9.8
a) and Lemma
9.18
,
p_{m}
is
m
-times backward
(L,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{m}}
. Arguing as in the proof of Theorem
9.8
b), we see that
p_{0}
is
l
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{0}}
.

Let
m_{1}>1
be a Pliss moment in the forward orbit of
c_{1}
so that

Kn\leq m_{1}<(1+{\bar{\varepsilon}})Kn,

where
K\geq 1
is a sufficiently large uniform constant. If
n<m<m_{1}
, then arguing as in the proof of Lemma
9.12
, we see that
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{m}}
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}}
. Since
c_{m}
is
m
-times backward
(\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}m},\varepsilon)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v}_{c_{m}}
, we have

\|DF^{-m}|_{\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{m}}}\|>\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}m}\lambda^{-(1-\varepsilon)m}>\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})m}.

If
m>m_{1}
, then again,
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{m_{1}}}
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{m_{1}}}
, and we have

\|DF^{-m_{1}}|_{\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{m_{1}}}}\|>\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})m_{1}}>\lambda^{-(\delta-{\bar{\varepsilon}})Kn}\lambda^{-(1-\delta)m_{1}}>{\bar{L}}\lambda^{-(1-\delta)m_{1}}.

By Proposition
6.12
,
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{1}}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
, and hence,
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-j}}
is sufficiently vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-j}}
. Thus,

\|DF^{-(l-j)}|_{\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-j}}}\|>\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})(l-j)}>\lambda^{-(\delta-{\bar{\varepsilon}})(l-j)}\lambda^{-(1-\delta)(l-j)}>L^{\underline{\delta}^{-1}}\lambda^{-(1-\delta)(l-j)}.

Concatenating the above estimates, we conclude that
\mathbf{p}=p_{m}
is
(m+l)
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{\mathbf{p}}
. Moreover, since

\|D_{\mathbf{p}}F^{-(m+l)}|_{\hat{E}^{v}_{\mathbf{p}}}\|>\lambda^{-(1-\delta)(m+l)},

we see that if
p_{-(m+l)}
is
M
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-(m+l)}}
, then
\mathbf{p}
is
(m+l+M)
-times backward
({\bar{L}},\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{\mathbf{p}}
. The result follows by induction.
∎

9.4.
Irregular points

Proposition 9.21
.

Let
\Delta_{c_{0}}^{c/fc}
and
\Delta_{c_{1}}^{v}
be the critical/full critical dust of
c_{0}
and the valuable dust of
c_{1}
respectively. For
K\geq 1
, let
\Lambda_{K}^{+/-/P}
be the set of all infinitely forward/backward/Pesin
(K,\delta)
-regular points in
\Lambda
. Then we have

\Lambda\setminus\left(\bigcup_{K\geq 1}\Lambda_{K}^{+}\right)\subset\Delta_{c_{0}}^{c}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Lambda\setminus\left(\bigcup_{K\geq 1}\Lambda_{K}^{-}\right)\subset\Delta^{v}_{c_{1}}.

Moreover,

\Lambda\setminus\left(\bigcup_{K\geq 1}\Lambda_{K}^{P}\right)\subset\Delta_{c_{0}}^{fc}\cup\{c_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}.

Proof.

We prove the last containment, as the other two can be proved by a similar and simpler argument.

If

p_{0}\notin\Delta_{c_{0}}^{fc}:=\Delta_{c_{0}}^{c}\cup\Delta^{v}_{c_{1}},

then

\operatorname{cd}(p_{0})\neq\infty
and
\operatorname{vd}(p_{0})\neq\infty.

By replacing
p_{0}
by
p_{-\operatorname{vd}(p_{0})-1}
if necessary, we can assume that

\operatorname{vd}(p_{0})=\varnothing.

If

d:=\operatorname{cd}(p_{0})\neq\varnothing,

let
-j<0
be the tunnel escape moment for
p_{d}
. Then by Proposition
6.9
, we see that for

q_{0}:=p_{d-j},

we have

\operatorname{cd}(q_{0})=\operatorname{vd}(q_{0})=\varnothing.

Thus,
p_{0}
is Pesin regular by Theorem
9.8
c).
∎

Proposition 9.22
.

If
F
is regularly unicritical on
\Lambda
, then there exists a unique regular critical orbit
\{c_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset\Lambda
.

Proof.

Let
q_{1}\in\Lambda\setminus\{c_{1}\}
be a generalized critical value. Since
q_{1}
cannot be Pesin regular,
q_{1}\in\Delta_{c_{0}}^{fc}
by Proposition
9.21
. For concreteness, assume
q_{1}\in\Delta_{c_{0}}^{c}
. Then
q_{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-N}}
for
N
arbitrarily large. Let
-l<0
be the slow tunnel escape moment for
q_{1+N}
. Then we have

l>{\bar{\delta}}_{1}^{-1}N.

Since
c_{-N}
is
(l-N,N)
-times
(\lambda^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}N},\varepsilon)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{-N}}
, we contradict Proposition
5.4
.
∎

10.
Total Ordering Inside a Unicritical Cover

Let
F
be the mildly dissipative, infinitely renormalizable and regularity unicritical diffeomorphism considered in Section
9
.

In Theorem
9.8
, choose

t=\mathbf{t}:={L_{1}}^{-1}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptL:=1/\underline{\mathbf{t}}.

Lastly, denote

\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}:=\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}(\mathbf{t})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptm\in\mathbb{Z},

and

\operatorname{cd}:=\operatorname{cd}_{\mathbf{t}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{vd}:=\operatorname{vd}_{\mathbf{t}},

where the
t
-critical depth
\operatorname{cd}_{t}
and the
t
-valuable depth
\operatorname{vd}_{t}
are defined as in Subsection
6.1
.

10.1.
Outer boundaries of tunnels and crescents

Let

\delta^{\prime}:=(1-{\bar{\delta}})\delta.

For
n\geq 0
and
i\in\{0,1\}
, denote

U_{c_{i}}^{\delta^{\prime}n}(a):=\mathbb{B}(a\lambda^{\delta^{\prime}n}l_{c_{i}},l_{c_{i}}):=(-a\lambda^{\delta^{\prime}n}l_{c_{i}},a\lambda^{\delta^{\prime}n}l_{c_{i}})\times(-l_{c_{i}},l_{c_{i}})\subset\mathbb{R}^{2},

where
l_{c_{i}}>0
is the regular radius at
c_{i}
. For
a>0
, let

T_{c_{0}}(a):=\{(x,y)\in U_{c_{0}}\;|\;|x|<a\mathbf{t}\;\text{and}\;|y|<|x|^{1/\underline{\delta}}\}

and

\bar{\partial}T_{c_{0}}(a):=\{(x,y)\in U_{c_{0}}\;|\;|x|<a\mathbf{t}\;\text{and}\;|y|=|x|^{1/\underline{\delta}}\}.

Let
j_{n}\geq 0
be the largest integer such that
j_{n}\leq n
and
-j_{n}
is a Pliss moment in the backward orbit of
c_{0}
. Define

\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}(a):=F^{-n}\circ\Phi_{c_{0}}^{-1}(T_{c_{0}}(a)\cap U_{c_{0}}^{\delta^{\prime}j_{n}}(a))

(10.1)

and

\bar{\partial}\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}(a):=F^{-n}\circ\Phi_{c_{0}}^{-1}(\bar{\partial}T_{c_{0}}(a)\cap U_{c_{0}}^{\delta^{\prime}j_{n}}(a)).

Note that
\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}=\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}(1)
. The
outer boundary of
\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
is defined as

\bar{\partial}\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}:=\bar{\partial}\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}(2).

A Jordan arc
\Gamma:(0,1)\to\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}(2)
is said to be
vertically proper in
\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
if
\Gamma
extends continuously to
[0,1]
, and
\Gamma(0)
and
\Gamma(1)
are in distinct components of
\bar{\partial}\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}\setminus\{c_{-n}\}
. More generally, a Jordan arc
\Gamma
in
\Omega
is said to be vertically proper in
\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
if
\Gamma\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}(2)
is.

For
a>0
, let

T_{c_{1}}(a):=F_{c_{0}}(T_{c_{0}}(a))\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\bar{\partial}T_{c_{1}}(a):=F_{c_{0}}(\bar{\partial}T_{c_{0}}(a)),

where

F_{c_{0}}:=\Phi_{c_{1}}\circ F\circ\Phi_{c_{0}}^{-1}.

For
n\geq 1
, let
i_{n}\geq 1
be the largest integer such that
i_{n}\leq n
and
i_{n}
is a Pliss moment in the forward orbit of
c_{1}
. Define

\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}(a):=F^{n-1}\circ\Phi_{c_{1}}^{-1}(T_{c_{1}}(a)\cap U_{c_{1}}^{\delta^{\prime}i_{n}}(a))

(10.2)

and

\bar{\partial}\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}(a):=F^{n-1}\circ\Phi_{c_{1}}^{-1}(\bar{\partial}T_{c_{1}}(a)\cap U_{c_{1}}^{\delta^{\prime}i_{n}}(a)).

Note that
\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}=\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}(1)
. The
outer boundary of
\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}
is defined as

\bar{\partial}\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}:=\bar{\partial}\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}(2).

Let

\mathbb{L}^{h,+}_{0}:=\{(x,0)\;|\;0<x<l_{c_{1}}\}

and

W^{h,+}_{c_{n}}:=F^{n-1}\circ\Phi_{c_{1}}^{-1}(\mathbb{L}^{h,+}_{0}\cap U_{c_{1}}^{\delta^{\prime}i_{n}}(2)).

Denote

\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{1}}^{i_{n}}(2):=\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}^{\delta^{\prime}i_{n}}(2)\cap\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}^{0}(2\mathbf{t}^{2}).

The
crescent gap
\mathcal{T}^{g}_{c_{n}}
at
c_{n}
is defined to be the connected component of
F^{n-1}(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{1}}^{\delta^{\prime}i_{n}}(2))\setminus\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}(2)
that contain
W^{h,+}_{c_{n}}
. The
filled valuable crescent at
c_{n}
is defined as

\mathcal{T}^{f}_{c_{n}}:=\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}^{2}\cup\mathcal{T}^{g}_{c_{n}}.

A Jordan arc
\Gamma:(0,1)\to\mathcal{T}^{f}_{c_{n}}
is said to be
vertically proper in
\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}
if
\Gamma
is a concatenation of three curves
\Gamma_{1}:(0,a_{1})\to\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}(2)
;
\Gamma_{2}:[a_{1},a_{2}]\to\mathcal{T}^{g}_{c_{n}}
and
\Gamma_{3}:(a_{2},1)\to\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}(2)
such that
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{3}
extends continuously to
[0,a_{1}]
and
[a_{2},1]
respectively; and for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, the endpoints of
\Gamma_{i}
are in distinct components of
\bar{\partial}\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}\setminus\{c_{n}\}
. More generally, a Jordan arc
\Gamma
in
\Omega
is said to be vertically proper in
\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}
if
\Gamma\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}(2)
is.

Lemma 10.1
.

If a
C^{1}
-curve
\Gamma
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
, and

\Gamma\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}^{f}\neq\varnothing,

then
\Gamma
is vertically proper in
\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}
.

Let

\hat{\delta}:=(1+{\bar{\delta}})\delta.

For
a>0
, let

\hat{T}_{c_{0}}(a):=\{(x,y)\in U_{c_{0}}\;|\;|x|<a\mathbf{t}\;\text{and}\;|y|<|x|^{1/\hat{\delta}}\}\supset T_{c_{0}}(a).

For
n\geq 0
, define
{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-n}}(a)
by replacing
T_{c_{0}}(a)
in (
10.1
) with
\hat{T}_{c_{0}}(a)
. Let
\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{-n}}
be a regular neighborhood at
c_{-n}
associated with a linearization of its
(\infty,n)
-time orbit with vertical direction
\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{-n}}
. This means, in particular, that whenever
-n
is a Pliss moment in the backward orbit of
c_{0}
, we have

\operatorname{radius}(\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{-n}})\asymp 1.

The set

{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-n}}:={\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-n}}(1)\cap\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{-n}}\supset\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}

(10.3)

is referred to as a
thickened critical tunnel at
c_{-n}
. A
thickened valuable crescent
{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{1+n}}
at
c_{1+n}
is defined by replacing
T_{c_{1}}(a)
in (
10.2
) with

\hat{T}_{c_{1}}(a):=\Phi_{c_{1}}\circ F\circ\Phi_{c_{0}}^{-1}(\hat{T}_{c_{0}}(a)),

and letting

{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{1+n}}:={\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{1+n}}(1)\supset\mathcal{T}_{c_{1+n}}.

(10.4)

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
0\leq m<n
. If
c_{-n}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-m}}
, then

{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-n}}\subset{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-m}}.

If
c_{n}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-m}}
, then

\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}\subset{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-m}}.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.9
, we have

n>{\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}m.

Write

(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(c_{-n+m}).

Since

\operatorname{radius}(\mathcal{U}_{c_{-n+m}})>\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}(n-m)},

we have

n>{\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|

by Proposition
5.9
. Hence, by Proposition
6.8

\operatorname{diam}({\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-n+m}})<\lambda^{\delta n}<|x_{0}|^{1/{\bar{\varepsilon}}}.

Since

\mathbb{D}_{c_{-n+m}}(k|x_{0}|^{1/\hat{\delta}})\subset{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}},

for some uniform constant
k>0
, the first claim follows.

The proof of the second claim is similar, and will be omitted.
∎

10.2.
Disjoint vertical manifolds

Let
p\in\Lambda\setminus\{c_{-n}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
. If
p\notin\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}
(where
\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}
is the critical dust of
c_{0}
), then by Proposition
9.21
,
p
has a well-defined local strong-stable manifold
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(p)\subset\mathcal{U}_{p}
. If
\operatorname{cd}(p)=\varnothing
, define

\hat{W}^{v}(p):=W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(p).

If
\operatorname{cd}(p)=d\in[0,\infty)
, define

\hat{W}^{v}(p):=W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(p)\cap{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-d}}.

For
n\geq 0
, we have
c_{-n}\notin\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}
by Proposition
6.9
. Let
\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{-n}}
be a regular neighborhood at
c_{-n}
associated with a linearization of its
(\infty,n)
-time orbit with vertical direction
\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{-n}}
. For
q\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
, define
W^{v}_{c_{-n}}(q)
to be the local vertical manifold at
q
in
\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{0}}
. Moreover, let

W^{v}_{c_{-n}}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}):=\bigcup_{q\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}}W^{v}_{c_{-n}}(q)

(which is not a curve but an open set foliated by vertical curves). If
\operatorname{cd}(c_{-n})=\varnothing
, define

\hat{W}^{v}(q):=W^{v}_{c_{-n}}(q)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptq\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}

and

\hat{W}^{v}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}):=W^{v}_{c_{-n}}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}).

If
\operatorname{cd}(c_{-n})=d\in[0,\infty)
, define

\hat{W}^{v}(q):=W^{v}_{c_{-n}}(q)\cap{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-d}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptq\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}

and

\hat{W}^{v}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}):=W^{v}_{c_{-n}}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}})\cap{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-d}}.

Proposition 10.3
.

Let
p,q\in\Lambda\setminus\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}
. If
p
and
q
are not contained in the same connected component of
\Lambda
, then

\hat{W}^{v}(p)\cap\hat{W}^{v}(q)=\varnothing.

Proof.

The result is obvious unless either
p
or
q
is equal to
c_{-n}
for some
n\geq 0
. For concreteness, suppose it is the former. Let
\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{-n}}
be a regular neighborhood at
c_{-n}
associated with a linearization of its
(\infty,n)
-time orbit with vertical direction
\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{-n}}
. Then

F^{n}(\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{-n}}^{{\bar{\delta}}n})\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}.

If

\hat{W}^{v}(q)\cap\hat{W}^{v}(c_{-n})\neq\varnothing,

then we have

F^{n}(\hat{W}^{v}(q))\subset\hat{W}^{v}(F^{n}(q))\subset{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}}.

This contradicts the fact that

{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}}\cap\hat{W}^{v}(c_{0})=\varnothing.

∎

10.3.
Unicritical cover

For
p\in\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}
, let
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
be an enclosed regular box at
p
with outer boundary
\bar{\partial}\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
(see Section
7
). The collection

\mathcal{C}:=\{\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\}_{p\in\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}}\cup\{\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

is a cover of
\Lambda
by (pinched) neighborhoods. We refer to
\mathcal{C}
as a
unicritical cover of
\Lambda
.

Proposition 10.4
.

By slightly adjusting the sizes of the elements in
\mathcal{C}
if necessary, the following properties hold for all
Q\in\mathcal{C}
.

i)

If for some
p\in\Lambda\setminus\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}
, we have
\hat{W}^{v}(p)\cap Q\neq\varnothing
, then
p\in Q
.

ii)

If for some
n\geq 0
, we have
\hat{W}^{v}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}})\cap Q\neq\varnothing
, then
\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}\subset Q
.

Proof.

Let
\{-j_{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
be the decreasing sequence of Pliss moments in the backward orbit of
c_{0}
. For
i\geq 0
, let
a_{i}\in(\lambda^{\underline{\delta}},1]
. Consider a sequence
\{{\check{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-n}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
of slightly smaller critical tunnels that satisfy

\mathcal{T}_{c_{-j_{i}}}(a_{i})\subset{\check{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-j_{i}}}\subset\mathcal{T}_{c_{-j_{i}}}

and

{\check{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-j_{i}-l}}=F^{-l}({\check{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-j_{i}}})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq l<j_{i+1}-j_{i}.

Observe that all previous results about critical tunnels still hold if we replace
\{\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
by
\{{\check{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-n}}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
.

Let
p_{1}\in\Lambda\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}
with
\operatorname{cd}(p_{1})=\varnothing
. Write

(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0}).

By Theorem
9.8
and Proposition
6.12
,
\hat{W}^{v}(p_{1})
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. If
p_{0}=c_{-m}
for some
m>0
, then by Lemma
9.5
, we have

\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-m}})<|x_{0}|^{1/{\bar{\varepsilon}}}.

If
p_{0}=c_{-m}
, let
q_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-m}}
and

\tilde{W}^{v}(q_{0}):=W^{v}_{c_{-m}}(q_{0}).

Otherwise, let
q_{0}=p_{0}
. Let
\tilde{E}^{v}_{q_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{q_{0}}
be the direction tangent to
\tilde{W}^{v}(q_{0})
. Then
\tilde{E}^{v}_{q_{1}}
and
\tilde{W}^{v}(q_{1})
are sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. Let
E^{v/h}_{q_{0}}
be the vertical/horizontal direction at
q_{0}
in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
. Then

\measuredangle(\tilde{E}^{v}_{q_{0}},E^{h}_{q_{0}})>k|x_{0}|

for some uniform constant
k>0
.

Suppose

q_{-j_{i}}\notin\mathcal{T}_{c_{-j_{i}}}
and
\tilde{W}^{v}(q_{-j_{i}})\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{-j_{i}}}\neq\varnothing.

By Proposition
6.13
,
q_{-j_{i}}
is
j_{i}
-times forward
(1,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{q_{-j_{i}}}
. By Proposition
3.11
, we see that
\tilde{W}^{v}(q_{-j_{i}})
is
\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})j_{i}}
-vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{-j_{i}}}
. On the other hand,

|x_{-j_{i}}|>\lambda^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}j_{i}}|x_{0}|>\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})\delta j_{i}}.

It follows that

\tilde{W}^{v}_{q_{-j_{i}}}\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{-j_{i}}}(1-\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})j_{i}})=\varnothing.

Thus, we may choose
{\check{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-j_{i}}}
such that

\mathcal{T}_{c_{-j_{i}}}\supset{\check{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-j_{i}}}\supset\mathcal{T}_{c_{-j_{i}}}(1-\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})j_{i}})\supset\mathcal{T}_{c_{-j_{i}}}(\lambda^{\underline{\delta}}),

and

\tilde{W}^{v}_{q_{-j_{i}}}\cap{\check{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-j_{i}}}=\varnothing.

∎

For
Q\in\mathcal{C}
, define its
critical depth

\operatorname{cd}(Q)\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\varnothing,0\}
as follows.

i)

If
Q=\mathcal{T}_{c_{-m}}
for some
m\geq 0
, then
\operatorname{cd}(Q):=m
.

ii)

If
Q=\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}
for some
n\geq 1
, then
\operatorname{cd}(Q):=\operatorname{cd}(c_{n})
.

iii)

Otherwise,
\operatorname{cd}(Q):=\varnothing
.

Let
X_{Q}
be the set of all points
p\in\Lambda\cap Q
such that
\operatorname{cd}(p)=\operatorname{cd}(Q)
.

Proposition 10.5
.

Let
Q\in\mathcal{C}
. Then for all
p\in X_{Q}
, the curve
\hat{W}^{v}(p)
is vertically proper in
Q
. If
c_{-n}\in X_{Q}
for some
n\geq 0
, then for all
q\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}\subset Q
, the curve
W^{v}_{c_{-n}}(q)
is vertically proper in
Q
.

Proof.

If
Q
is a regular box, then the result is clearly true. If
Q=\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}
, then the result follows immediately from Theorem
9.8
, Proposition
6.12
and Lemma
10.1
. If
Q=\mathcal{T}_{c_{-m}}
or some
m\geq 0
or
Q=\mathcal{T}_{c_{k}}
for some
k>1
, then the result follows immediately from the previous case and Lemma
10.2
.
∎

10.4.
Total ordering with blowups

Let
(X,\leq_{X})
be a totally ordered set;
U
a subset of
X
; and
\{(Y_{u},\leq_{Y_{u}})\}_{u\in U}
a collection of totally ordered sets. Define
\mathcal{Y}:U\to\{Y_{u}\}_{u\in U}
by

\mathcal{Y}(u):=Y_{u}.

The
blow up of
X
by
\mathcal{Y}
is defined as the set

X[\mathcal{Y}]:=(X\setminus U)\sqcup\bigsqcup_{u\in U}Y_{u}

with the total order
\leq_{X[\mathcal{Y}]}
given by the following properties. Let
x,y\in X[\mathcal{Y}]
. Then
x\leq_{X[\mathcal{Y}]}y
if one of the following holds:

i)

x,y\in X\setminus U
and
x\leq_{X}y
;

ii)

x\in X\setminus U
and
y\in Y_{u}
with
x\leq_{X}u
;

iii)

x\in Y_{u}
and
y\in X\setminus U
with
u\leq_{X}y
;

iv)

x\in Y_{u}
and
y\in Y_{v}
with
u\leq_{X}v
; or

v)

x,y\in Y_{u}
and
x\leq_{Y_{u}}y
.

For
n\geq 0
, consider sets
Y^{n}
and
U^{n}
, and a set-valued function
\mathcal{Y}^{n}
obtained inductively as follows. First, let
U^{0}
be a subset of
Y^{0}:=X
. Suppose that
Y^{n-1}
and
U^{n-1}
are given. Let
\{(Y^{n}_{u},\leq_{Y^{n}_{u}})\}_{u\in U^{n-1}}
be a collection of totally ordered sets, and define

Y^{n}:=\bigsqcup_{u\in U^{n-1}}Y^{n}_{u}.

Next, let
\mathcal{Y}^{n}:U^{n-1}\to\{Y^{n}_{u}\}_{u\in U^{n-1}}
be given by

\mathcal{Y}^{n}(u):=Y^{n}_{u}.

Lastly, let
U^{n}
be a subset of
Y^{n}
.

Let
X^{\wedge 0}:=X
, and for
n\geq 0
, inductively define an
n
th blow-up of
X
by

X^{\wedge n}:=X^{\wedge(n-1)}[\mathcal{Y}^{n}]

with the total order
\leq_{X^{\wedge n}}
defined as above. Next, we define an
infinite blow-up of
X
by

X^{\wedge\infty}:=\{x\in X^{\wedge n}\setminus U^{n}\;\text{for some}\;n\geq 0\}

with the total order
\leq_{X^{\wedge\infty}}
given by the following property. Let
x,y\in X^{\wedge\infty}
. Then
x\leq_{X^{\wedge\infty}}y
if
x\leq_{X^{\wedge}n}y
, where
n
is any integer such that
x,y\in X^{n}\setminus U^{n}
.

A sequence

u_{\infty}:=\{u_{n}\}_{n=0}^{\infty}

such that
u_{0}\in U^{0}\cap X
and
u_{n}\in U^{n}\cap Y^{n}_{u_{n-1}}
for
n>0
is called a
limit point
of
X^{\wedge\infty}
. Define the
completion
X^{\wedge\omega}
of
X^{\wedge\infty}
as the disjoint union of
X^{\wedge\infty}
and the set of all its limits points, together with the total order
\leq_{X^{\wedge\omega}}
given by the following property. Let
x,y\in X^{\wedge\omega}
. Then
x\leq_{X^{\wedge\omega}}y
if

i)

x,y\in X^{\wedge\infty}
and
x\leq_{X^{\wedge\infty}}y
;

ii)

x\in X^{\wedge n}\setminus U^{n}
and
y=\{u_{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
with
x\leq_{X^{\wedge n}}u_{n}
;

iii)

x=\{u_{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
and
y\in X^{\wedge n}\setminus U^{n}
with
u_{n}\leq_{X^{\wedge n}}y
; or

iv)

x=\{u_{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
and
y=\{v_{i}\}_{i=0}^{\infty}
, and
u_{n}\leq_{X^{\wedge n}}v_{n}
, where
n
is the smallest integer such that
u_{n}\neq v_{n}
.

10.5.
Total order on
\Lambda_{Q}

Let
Q\in\mathcal{C}
, and let
\mathcal{G}
be a collection of disjoint Jordan curves that are vertically proper in
Q
. Then we define a total order on
\mathcal{G}
as follows. If
Q=\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}
for some
n\geq 1
, let
\hat{Q}:=\mathcal{T}_{c_{n}}^{f}
. Otherwise, let
\hat{Q}:=Q
. Note that for any
\Gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{G}
, the complement
\hat{Q}\setminus\Gamma_{0}
is the disjoint union of exactly two connected components, say
\hat{Q}^{+}_{\Gamma_{0}}
and
\hat{Q}^{-}_{\Gamma_{0}}
. Thus, there is a unique (strict) total order
<_{\mathcal{G}}
on
\mathcal{G}
such that for any
\Gamma\in\mathcal{G}
distinct from
\Gamma_{0}
, we have
\Gamma<_{\mathcal{G}}\Gamma_{0}
if
\Gamma\cap\hat{Q}\subset\hat{Q}^{-}_{\Gamma_{0}}
and
\Gamma_{0}<_{\mathcal{G}}\Gamma
if
\Gamma\cap\hat{Q}\subset\hat{Q}^{+}_{\Gamma_{0}}
.

For
Q\in\mathcal{C}
, let
Y_{Q}
be the set consisting of all elements
x
that satisfy one of the following properties.

i)

x=p\in X_{Q}
such that
p\neq c_{-n}
for all
n\geq 0
. In this case, define
\Gamma^{v}_{x}:=W^{v}_{p}(p)
.

ii)

x=\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
for some
n\geq 0
such that
c_{-n}\in X_{Q}
. In this case, define
\Gamma^{v}_{x}:=W^{v}_{c_{-n}}(c_{-n})
.

Let
U_{Q}
be the set of all elements in
Y_{Q}
of type
ii)
. By Propositions
10.3
and
10.5
, the collection

\Gamma^{v}_{Q}:=\{\Gamma^{v}_{x}\}_{x\in Y_{Q}}

consists of pairwise disjoint curves that are vertically proper in
Q
. As discussed above, this implies that
\Gamma^{v}_{Q}
has a total order
\leq_{\Gamma^{v}_{Q}}
(non-strict, since we may have
\Gamma^{v}_{x}=\Gamma^{v}_{y}
with
x\neq y
), which induces a total order
\leq_{Y_{Q}}
on
Y_{Q}
.

Let
Y^{0}:=Y_{Q}
and
U^{0}:=U_{Q}
. Proceeding inductively, suppose that
Y^{n-1}
and
U^{n-1}
are given. For
x\in U^{n-1}
, define
Y^{n}_{x}:=Y_{x}
;
U^{n}_{x}:=U_{x}
; and

Y^{n}:=\bigsqcup_{x\in U^{n-1}}Y^{n}_{x}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptU^{n}:=\bigsqcup_{x\in U^{n-1}}U^{n}_{x}.

By the construction given in Subsection
10.4
, we obtain the completion
Y_{Q}^{\omega}
of an infinite blow-up of
Y_{Q}
with a total ordering
<_{Y_{Q}^{\wedge\omega}}
.

Theorem 10.6
.

For
Q\in\mathcal{C}
, let
\Lambda_{Q}:=\Lambda\cap Q
. Then

\Lambda_{Q}=Y_{Q}^{\wedge\omega},

and the set of limit points in
Y_{Q}^{\wedge\omega}
is given by
\Lambda_{Q}\cap\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}}
. Thus,
\Lambda_{Q}
has a total order
\leq_{Q}
such that for
x,y\in\Lambda_{Q}
, we have
x\leq_{Q}y
and
y\leq_{Q}x
if and only if
y\in W^{s}(x)
.

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Proposition
6.8
.
∎

Let
Q\in\mathcal{C}
. A point
x\in X\subset\Lambda_{Q}
is called an
extremal point of
X
if either

•

x\leq_{Q}y
for all
y\in X
; or

•

x\geq_{Q}y
for all
y\in X
.

The following three propositions record observations that follow immediately from the construction of the total order on
\Lambda_{Q}
.

Proposition 10.7
.

Let
Q,Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}
, and let
X:=\Lambda_{Q}\cap\Lambda_{Q^{\prime}}
. Then
\leq_{Q}
and
\leq_{{Q^{\prime}}}
induces the same total order on
X
(up to orientation). That is, either

•

x\leq_{{Q^{\prime}}}y
for all
x,y\in X
with
x\leq_{Q}y
; or

•

x\geq_{{Q^{\prime}}}y
for all
x,y\in X
with
x\leq_{Q}y
.

Proposition 10.8
.

Let
Q,Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}
with
Q\neq\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
, and let
X\subset\Lambda_{Q}
be a set satisfying
F(X)\subset\Lambda_{Q^{\prime}}
. Then
F
preserves the total order on
X
(up to orientation). That is, either

•

F(x)\leq_{{Q^{\prime}}}F(y)
for all
x,y\in X
such that
x\leq_{Q}y
; or

•

F(x)\geq_{{Q^{\prime}}}F(y)
for all
x,y\in X
such that
x\leq_{Q}y
.

Proposition 10.9
.

Let
X\subset\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}}
. Then the following holds.

i)

If
c_{0}\leq_{{\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}}}x_{0}
for all
x\in X
, then for all
y,z\in X
such that
y\leq_{{\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}}}z
, we have
F(y)\leq_{{\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}}}F(z)
.

ii)

If
x\leq_{{\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}}}c_{0}
for all
x\in X
, then for all
y,z\in X
such that
y\leq_{{\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}}}z
, we have
F(z)\leq_{{\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}}}F(y)
.

Let
Q\in\mathcal{C}
. We define a larger covering element
\hat{Q}\supset Q
as follows. If
Q=\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, then let
\hat{Q}:=Q
. Otherwise,
Q=\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
for some
p\in\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}
. Recall that
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
is a quadrilateral bound by four nearly linear arcs for which the opposite sides are nearly parallel. Moreover, the horizontal width and the vertical height of
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
are approximately
1/L_{1}
and
1/L_{2}
respectively, where

{\bar{L}}<L_{1}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\overline{L_{1}}<L_{2}.

Let
\hat{Q}
be a strictly larger encased regular box at
p
whose horizontal width and the vertical height are approximately
1/L_{1}^{\prime}
and
1/L_{2}^{\prime}
respectively, where

{\bar{L}}<L_{1}^{\prime}<\underline{L_{1}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\overline{L_{1}^{\prime}}<L_{2}^{\prime}<\underline{L_{2}}.

Proposition 10.10
.

For
0\leq i\leq n
, let
Q_{i}\in\mathcal{C}\setminus\{\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}\}
. Suppose there exist
x_{0},y_{0},z_{0}\in\Lambda_{Q_{0}}
such that

x_{0}\leq_{{Q_{0}}}y_{0}\leq_{{Q_{0}}}z_{0},

and
x_{i},z_{i}\in\Lambda_{Q_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq n
. Then
y_{n}\in\Lambda_{\hat{Q}_{n}}
and

x_{n}\leq_{{\hat{Q}_{n}}}y_{n}\leq_{{\hat{Q}_{n}}}z_{n}.

Proof.

The result clearly holds if
Q_{i}=\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Suppose the
Q_{i}
’s are encased regular boxes. Let
\Gamma(x_{n})
and
\Gamma(z_{n})
be two proper sufficiently vertical arcs in
\hat{Q}_{n}
containing
x_{n}
and
z_{n}
respectively. By Proposition
3.19
, the preimages
\Gamma(x_{0})
and
\Gamma(z_{0})
of
\Gamma(x_{n})
and
\Gamma(z_{n})
under
F^{-n}
are proper and sufficiently vertical in
Q_{0}
. Let
Q_{0}(x_{0},z_{0})
be the component of
Q_{0}\setminus(\Gamma(x_{0})\cup\Gamma(z_{0}))
containing
y_{0}
. Applying Proposition
3.19
again, we see that the image of
Q_{0}(x_{0},z_{0})
under
F^{i}
is still nearly a parallelogram whose height increased by no more than a factor of
{\bar{L}}
. It follows that
F^{i}(Q_{0}(x_{0},z_{0}))\subset\hat{Q}_{i}
. The claim about the order of
x_{n},y_{n}
and
z_{n}
follows immediately from Proposition
10.8
.
∎

11.
Connected Components of the Limit Set

Let
F
be the mildly dissipative, infinitely renormalizable and regularity unicritical diffeomorphism considered in Section
10
.

The
forward/backward/Pesin
\delta
-regularity of
p\in\Lambda
is the smallest value
\mathcal{L}^{+/-/P}_{\delta}(p)\in[1,\infty]
such that
p
is forward/backward/Pesin
(\mathcal{L}^{+/-/P}_{\delta}(p),\delta)
-regular. We say that
p
is
forward/backward/Pesin
\delta
-regular
if
\mathcal{L}^{+/-/P}_{\delta}(p)<\infty
(i.e.
p\in\Lambda^{+/-/P}_{\delta}
).

Let
Z
be the connected component of
\Lambda
. Observe that either

Z\cap\{c_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}=\varnothing,

or there exists a unique integer
m\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
c_{m}\in Z
. In the latter case, we say that
Z
is a
critical component
if
m\leq 0
, or a
valuable component
if
m\geq 1
.

We say that
z\in Z
is an
extremal point of
Z
if for any unicritical covering element
Q\in\mathcal{C}
containing
z
, the point
z
is an extremal point for
(Z_{Q},\leq_{Q})
, where
Z_{Q}
is a connected component of
Z\cap Q
containing
z
. Denote

\mathring{Z}:=Z\setminus\{\text{extremal points of }Z\}.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
Z
be a connected component of
\Lambda
. Then
\mathcal{L}^{+/-/P}_{\delta}(p)
varies lower semi-continuously on
p\in Z
. Moreover,
\mathcal{L}^{+/-}_{\delta}(p)<\infty
for
p\in\mathring{Z}
. Lastly, if
\mathcal{L}^{P}_{\delta}(p)=\infty
for some
p\in\mathring{Z}
, then
p=c_{m}
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

For
p\in Z
, let
t_{\pm}(p)\in[0,\mathbf{t}]
with
\mathbf{t}:=L_{1}^{-1}
be the largest value such that

p\notin\bigcup_{i=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{T}_{\pm i}(t_{\pm}(p)).

Clearly,
t_{\pm}(p)
depends continuously on
p
. The first claim now follows from Theorem
9.8
.

Let
n\geq 0
such that
c_{-n}\notin Z
and

Z\cap\mathcal{T}_{-n}\neq\varnothing.

We claim that
\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
must contain an extremal point of
Z
. Otherwise, the two extremal points of
Z\cap\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}}
coincide with the two extremal points of
\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
. However, this is not possible unless
c_{-n}\in Z
. Consequently, if there exists

p\in Z\cap\Delta^{c}_{c_{0}},

then
p
must be an extremal point in
Z
. By a similar argument, we conclude that if there exists

p\in Z\cap\Delta^{v}_{c_{1}},

then
p
must be an extremal point in
Z
. The second and third claims now follow from Theorem
9.8
.
∎

Theorem 11.2

(Non-trivial components are center curves)
.

Every non-trivial component
Z_{0}
of
\Lambda
is a
C^{r}
-curve. Moreover,
Z_{0}^{\prime}(t)\in E^{c}_{Z_{0}(t)}
for all
t\in(0,|Z_{0}|)
.

The proof of Theorem
11.2
is based on the following three lemmas.

Lemma 11.3
.

There exists a uniform constant
\kappa>0
such that the following holds. Let
Z_{0}
be a non-valuable connected component of
\Lambda
, and let
p_{0}\in Z_{0}
be a Pesin
\delta
-regular point. If there exists

y_{0}\in(Z_{0}\cap W^{ss}(p_{0}))\setminus\{p_{0}\},

then there exists a Pliss moment
-N<0
in the backward orbit of
p_{0}
such that

\operatorname{dist}(F^{-N}(y_{0}),F^{-N}(p_{0}))>\kappa.

Proof.

Since
\operatorname{cd}(p_{0}),\operatorname{vd}(p_{0})<\infty
, by replacing
p_{0}\in Z_{0}
with
p_{-i}\in Z_{-i}
for some
i\geq 0
, we may assume that

\operatorname{cd}(p_{0})=\operatorname{vd}(p_{0})=\varnothing.

If

p_{-i}\in\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}
for
0\leq i\leq I,

then we have

\operatorname{dist}(y_{-I},p_{-I})>{\bar{L}}^{-1}\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\delta}})I}\operatorname{dist}(y_{0},p_{0}).

Let
-m<0
be a moment such that

\operatorname{cd}(p_{-m})=0,

and let
-j<0
be the tunnel escape moment for
p_{-m}
. By Proposition
6.13
, Proposition
6.13
and Theorem
9.8
,
p_{-m+1}
and
p_{-m-j}
are forward
(1,\delta)
-regular. If
y_{-m+1}\in W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(p_{-m+1})
, then

\operatorname{dist}(y_{-m+1},p_{-m+1})>k\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\delta}})m}\operatorname{dist}(y_{0},p_{0})

for some uniform constant
k>0
. Observe that in this case,
y_{-m+1}
is contained in the same connected component of
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(p_{-m+1})\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}
as
p_{-m+1}
. Otherwise,
Z_{-m+1}
must contain
c_{1}
, which contradicts the assumption that
Z_{0}
is not valuable. It follows that

y_{-m-i}\in W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(p_{-m-i})
for
0\leq i\leq j,

and

\operatorname{dist}(y_{-m-j},p_{-m-j})>k\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\delta}})(m+j)}\operatorname{dist}(y_{0},p_{0}).

Proceeding inductively, we obtain the desired result.
∎

Lemma 11.4
.

Let
Z_{0}
be a connected component of
\Lambda
, and let
p_{0}\in Z_{0}
be a Pesin
\delta
-regular point. Then there does not exist a sequence

\{y^{i}_{0}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subset(W^{ss}(p_{0})\cap Z_{0})\setminus\{p_{0}\}

such that
\operatorname{dist}(y^{i}_{0},p_{0})\searrow 0
as
i\to\infty
.

Proof.

Suppose towards a contradiction that such a sequence exists. By Lemma
11.3
, there exists a subsequence
\{y^{i_{k}}_{0}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
and a decreasing sequence
\{-N_{k}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
of Pliss moments in the backward orbit of
p_{0}
such that

\operatorname{dist}(y^{i}_{-N_{k}},p_{-N_{k}})>\kappa.

Consider the unicritical cover
\mathcal{C}
of
\Lambda
defined in Subsection
10.3
. We may assume that the vertical height of each element
Q\in\mathcal{C}
are bounded by
\kappa/2
. This means that if
p\in\Lambda_{Q}
and
q\in W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(p)
, and

\operatorname{dist}(p,q)>\kappa/2,

then
q
cannot be contained in
\Lambda_{Q}
.

By compactness of
\Lambda
, we may choose a finite subcover
\mathcal{C}_{0}\subset\mathcal{C}
. Observe that if a connected component
Z
of
\Lambda
intersects multiple elements from
\mathcal{C}_{0}
, then
Z
must contain least one of the two extremal points for each of these elements. Hence, we see that there are at most finitely many components that can intersect multiple covering elements. However, by the previous observations, the component
Z_{-N_{k}}
for all
k\geq 1
cannot be contained in a single covering element. This is a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 11.5
.

Let
Z_{0}
be a non-valuable connected component of
\Lambda
. Then there exists a constant
\kappa_{Z_{0}}>0
such that if
p_{0}\in Z_{0}
is Pesin
\delta
-regular, and

y_{0}\in(W^{ss}(p_{0})\cap Z_{0})\setminus\{p_{0}\},

then

\operatorname{dist}(y_{0},p_{0})>\kappa_{Z_{0}}.

Proof.

Consider the finite cover
\mathcal{C}_{0}
of
\Lambda
given in the proof of Lemma
11.4
. Let
N=N(Z_{0})\in\mathbb{N}
be the smallest integer such that for all
n\geq N
, the component
Z_{-n}
does not intersect multiple covering elements in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
. Then it follows from Lemma
11.3
that if
p_{0}\in Z_{0}
is Pesin
\delta
-regular, then

W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(p_{-n})\cap Z_{-n}=\{p_{-n}\}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptn\geq N.

Suppose there exists

y_{0}\in(W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(p_{0})\cap Z_{0})\setminus\{p_{0}\}.

Then by Lemma
11.3
, there exists
0\leq m<N
such that

\operatorname{dist}(y_{-m},p_{-m})>\kappa.

Consequently,

\operatorname{dist}(y_{0},p_{0})>K\lambda^{(1+\eta)N}\kappa,

for some uniform constant
K\geq 1
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
11.2
.

It suffices to prove the result for a non-valuable connected component
Z
.

Let
p_{0}\in Z_{0}
be Pesin
(K,\delta)
-regular, and let

\{\Phi_{p_{m}}:\mathcal{U}_{p_{m}}\to U_{p_{m}}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

be a linearization of the full orbit of
p_{0}
. By Proposition
11.1
and Lemma
11.5
, if
W_{0}\subset\mathcal{U}^{0}_{p_{0}}
is a sufficiently small neighborhood of
p_{0}
, and
\Gamma_{0}
is the the connected component of
Z_{0}\cap W_{0}
containing
p_{0}
, then

\gamma_{0}:=\Phi_{p_{0}}(\Gamma_{0})

is the horizontal graph of a
C^{0}
-map
g_{0}:\mathbb{I}(l)\to\mathbb{R}
for some sufficiently small
l>0
.

We claim that
g_{0}
is differentiable at
0
, and that
g_{0}^{\prime}(0)=0
. Let
\{-n_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
be the decreasing sequence of Pliss moments in the backward orbit of
p_{0}
. As in the proof of Lemma
11.5
, there exists
N=N(Z_{0})\geq 0
such that for all
q_{0}\in\Gamma_{0}
, we have

W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(q_{-n})\cap Z_{-n}=\{q_{-n}\}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptn\geq N.

For
n_{i}>N
, let

\{\Phi_{p_{-n_{i}+m}}^{i}:\mathcal{U}_{p_{-n_{i}+m}}^{i}\to U_{p_{-n_{i}+m}}^{i}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

be a linearization of the full orbit of
p_{-n_{i}}
, and let
\Gamma_{-n_{i}}^{i}
be the connected component of
\Gamma_{-n_{i}}\cap\mathcal{U}_{p_{-n_{i}}}^{{\bar{\delta}}n_{i}}
containing
p_{-n_{i}}
. Observe that

\gamma^{i}_{0}:=\Phi_{p_{0}}\circ F^{n_{i}}(\Gamma_{-n_{i}}^{i})

is a neighborhood of
0
in
\gamma_{0}
. Thus, by Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
3.9
, we see that

|g_{0}(x)|<{\bar{K}}\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})n_{i}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt|x|<{\bar{K}}^{-1}\lambda^{{\bar{\delta}}n_{i}}.

By Proposition
4.4
and Proposition
4.3
, we conclude that

\gamma_{0}\subset\{(x,y)\in U_{p_{0}}\;|\;|x|<l\;\text{and}\;|y|<|x|^{1/{\bar{\delta}}}\}.

The result follows.
∎

12.
Recurrence of the Critical Orbit

Let
F
be the mildly dissipative, infinitely renormalizable and regularity unicritical diffeomorphism considered in Section
10
.

For any
C^{r}
-curve
\gamma\subset\Omega
, let
\phi_{\gamma}:(0,|\gamma|)\to\gamma
be its parameterization by arclength. Recall that
\|\gamma\|_{C^{r}}:=\|\phi_{\gamma}\|_{C^{r}}.

Let

F_{\gamma}:=\phi_{f(\gamma)}^{-1}\circ F|_{\gamma}\circ\phi_{\gamma}.

The
distortion of
F
along
\gamma
is defined as

\operatorname{Dis}(F,\gamma):=\sup_{t,s\in(0,|\gamma|)}\frac{|F_{\gamma}^{\prime}(t)|}{|F_{\gamma}^{\prime}(s)|}.

The following statement is a straightforward generalization of 1D Denjoy Lemma.

Proposition 12.1
.

Let
\gamma_{0}\subset\Omega
be a
C^{2}
-curve. Suppose for all
0\leq i<n
, the curve

\gamma_{i}:=F^{i}(\gamma_{0}).

is well-defined and
\|\gamma_{i}\|_{C^{2}}=O(1)
. Then

\log(\mathcal{D}(F^{n}|_{\gamma}))=O\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}|F^{i}(\gamma)|\right).

Theorem 12.2
.

The critical value
c_{1}
is recurrent.

Proof.

The proof is by contradiction. Suppose there exists
R>0
such that

\mathbb{D}_{c_{1}}(R)\cap\{c_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}=\{c_{1}\}.

(12.1)

Lemma 12.3
.

Suppose (
12.1
) holds. Then the connected component
Z_{c_{1}}
of
\Lambda
containing the critical value
c_{1}
is a non-trivial arc.

Proof.

Suppose not. Then
\mathcal{D}^{n}_{1}\subset\mathbb{D}_{c_{1}}(R)
for
n
sufficiently large. However,
c_{1+lR_{n}}\in\mathcal{D}^{n}_{1}
for all
l\in\mathbb{Z}
.
∎

By Theorem
11.2
,
Z_{c_{1}}
is a
C^{1}
Jordan arc. Consider the parameterization of
Z_{c_{1}}
by its arclength. Denote

\mathring{Z}_{c_{1}}:=Z_{c_{1}}(0,|Z_{c_{1}}|).

Choose
{t}\leq R
in Theorem
9.8
, and let
L:=L(t)\geq 1
be the regularity factor. Note that we have

\Lambda\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}\subset\mathring{Z}_{c_{m}}.

Consequently, the points

z_{m}^{0}:=Z_{c_{m}}(0)
and
z_{m}^{1}:=Z_{c_{m}}(|Z_{c_{m}}|)

are Pesin
(L,\delta)
-regular. Moreover, if
m\neq l
, then

\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}\cap\mathcal{T}_{c_{l}}=\varnothing.

Lemma 12.4
.

Suppose (
12.1
) holds. If
\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}\subset Q:=\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
for some
n\geq 0
and
p\in\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}
, then either

•

z_{-n}^{0}<_{Q}p<_{Q}z_{-n}^{1}
for all
p\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
; or

•

z_{-n}^{1}<_{Q}p<_{Q}z_{-n}^{0}
for all
p\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n}}
.

For
p\in\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}
, recall the definition of
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(a)
with
a>0
(see Section
7
). Rather than setting
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}:=\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1/2)
, we modify the construction of an encased regular box in the following way. By Lemma
12.4
,
p
is accumulated by other Pesin
(L,\delta)
-regular points. If

q<_{{\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1/2)}}p\hskip 14.22636pt\text{or}\hskip 14.22636ptp<_{{\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1/2)}}q\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for all}\hskip 14.22636ptq\in\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}\cap\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1/2),

then define
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
as the quadrilateral bounded by
\bar{\partial}\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
,
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(p)
and
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(q)
. Otherwise, choose
q_{-},q_{+}\in\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}\cap\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1/2)
such that

q_{-}<_{{\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1/2)}}p<_{{\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1/2)}}q_{+},

and define
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
as the quadrilateral bounded by
\bar{\partial}\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
,
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(q_{-})
and
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(q_{+})
. Then

\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\subset\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1/2)\subset\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1).

Choose a finite unicritical cover of
\Lambda

\mathcal{C}_{0}=\{\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\}_{p\in\mathcal{P}}\cup\{\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}\}_{|m|\leq M}

where
\mathcal{P}\subset\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}
is a finite set, and
0<M<\infty
.

Lemma 12.5
.

Suppose (
12.1
) holds. There exists
I\geq 1
such that the following holds. For all
i\geq I
, there exists
p_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
and
N_{i}\geq 1
such that for all
n\geq N_{i}
, we have

Z_{c_{i}}\subset\mathcal{D}^{n}_{i}\subset\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p_{i}}.

Moreover,
\mathcal{D}^{n}_{i}
does not contain an extremal point of
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p_{i}}
.

Proof.

Clearly,
Z_{c_{i}}
cannot intersect
\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}
for
|m|\leq M
if
i>M
. If
Z_{c_{i}}
intersects multiple covering elements in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
, then it must contain at least one of the extremal points in each element. Since this can happen only finitely many times, we obtain the desired integer
I\geq 1
. Since
\mathcal{D}^{n}_{i}
converges in Hausdorff topology to
Z_{c_{i}}
, the result follows.
∎

For
n\geq N_{I}
, let
I_{n}\geq I
be the largest integer such that for
I\leq i\leq I_{n}
, we have

\Lambda^{n}_{i}:=\mathcal{D}^{n}_{i}\cap\Lambda\subset\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p_{i}}(1).

Denote the extremal points in
\Lambda^{n}_{i}
with respect to
<_{{\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p_{i}}(1)}}
by
a^{n}_{i}
and
b^{n}_{i}
. It follows from Lemma
12.4
that
a^{n}_{i}
and
b^{n}_{i}
are Pesin
(L,\delta)
-regular. Let
\hat{\mathcal{D}}^{n}_{i}
be the quadrilateral bounded by
\bar{\partial}\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p_{i}}
,
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(a^{n}_{i})
and
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(b^{n}_{i})
.

Lemma 12.6
.

Suppose (
12.1
) holds. Let
\Gamma_{I}
be a sufficiently horizontal
C^{r}
Jordan arc in
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p_{I}}
with uniformly bounded curvature. For
n\geq N_{I}
and
I\leq i\leq I_{n}
, define

\Gamma_{I}^{n}:=\Gamma_{I}\cap\hat{\mathcal{D}}^{n}_{I}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Gamma_{i}^{n}:=F^{i-I}(\Gamma_{I}^{n}).

Then
\Gamma_{i}^{n}
is sufficiently horizontal in
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p_{I}}(1)
, and has uniformly bounded curvature. Moreover,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=I}^{I_{n}}|\Gamma_{i}^{n}|=O(1).

Proof.

The first claim is an immediate consequence of Proposition
3.19
.

Observe that
\{W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(a^{n}_{i}),W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(b^{n}_{i})\}_{i=I}^{I_{n}}
partitions
\{\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1)\}_{p\in\mathcal{P}}
by nearly linear vertical arcs. Since
\hat{\mathcal{D}}^{n}_{i}
occupies each cell in this partition at most once, and
\{\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}(1)\}_{p\in\mathcal{P}}
clearly has uniformly bounded total horizontal length, the second claim follows.
∎

For
n\geq N_{I}
and
I\leq i\leq I_{n}
, let
\hat{Z}_{i}
be the quadrilateral bounded by
\bar{\partial}\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p_{i}}(1)
,
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(z_{i}^{0})
and
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(z_{i}^{1})
. Write

\Gamma_{i}^{n}:=A_{i}^{n}\cup\hat{\Gamma}_{i}\cup B_{i}^{n},

where

\hat{\Gamma}_{i}:=F^{i-I}(\Gamma_{I}\cap\hat{Z}_{I}),

and
A_{i}^{n}
and
B_{i}^{n}
are the two components of
\Gamma_{i}^{n}\setminus\hat{\Gamma}_{i}
.

Since
\hat{\mathcal{D}}^{n}_{I}
converges to
\hat{Z}_{I}
as
n\to\infty
, we see that

|A_{I}^{n}|,|B_{I}^{n}|\to 0
as

n\to\infty.

On the other hand, either
|A_{I_{n}}^{n}|
or
|B_{I_{n}}^{n}|
must be uniformly bounded below, while
|\hat{\Gamma}_{I_{n}}|\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. Applying Lemma
12.6
and Proposition
12.1
, we obtain a contradiction. Hence, (
12.1
) cannot hold, and Theorem
12.2
follows.
∎

13.
Total Order on Renormalization Pieces

Let
F
be the mildly dissipative, infinitely renormalizable and regularity unicritical diffeomorphism considered in Section
10
.

Recall that the renormalization limit set of
F
is given by

\Lambda:=\bigcap_{n=0}^{\infty}\bigcup_{i=0}^{R_{n}-1}F^{i}(D^{n}_{1}),

where

r_{n}:=R_{n+1}/R_{n}\geq 2,

and
D^{n}_{1}
is an
R_{n}
-periodic Jordan domain containing the critical value
c_{1}
. Denote

D^{n}_{i}:=F^{i-1}(D^{n}_{1})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Lambda^{n}_{i}:=\Lambda\cap D^{n}_{i}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt1\leq i\leq R_{n}.

We refer to
\Lambda^{n}_{i}
as a
renormalization piece of depth
n
. By Theorem
12.2
, there exists
N_{1}\geq 0
such that

\mathcal{D}^{n}_{1}\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Lambda^{n}_{1}\subset\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptn\geq N_{1}.

Proposition 13.1
.

Let
n\geq N_{1}
. If
\Lambda^{n}_{i}\subset\Lambda^{N_{1}}_{0}
and
\Lambda^{n}_{i}\neq\Lambda^{n}_{0}
, then either

•

x<_{{\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}}}c_{0}
for all
x\in\Lambda^{n}_{i}
; or

•

c_{0}<_{{\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}}}x
for all
x\in\Lambda^{n}_{i}
.

Proof.

For
m\geq n\geq N_{1}
, let
J(m,n)\subset\{0,\ldots,R_{m}-1\}
be the set of all indexes such that

\mathcal{D}^{m}_{j}\subset\mathcal{D}^{n}_{0}
for
j\in J(m,n).

By Theorem
9.7
, for
n\geq N_{1}
, there exists
M_{n}>n
such that for any
m\geq M_{n}
and
j\in J(m,N_{1})\setminus J(m,n)
, we have

\mathcal{D}^{m}_{j}\subset\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}\setminus\mathcal{D}^{n}_{0}.

Since
\Lambda^{n}_{i}
is a union of
\mathcal{D}^{m}_{j}
’s, the result follows.
∎

Consider a finite unicritical cover of
\Lambda
:

\hat{\mathcal{C}}=\{\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\}_{p\in\mathcal{P}}\cup\{\mathcal{T}_{m}\}_{|m|<\hat{M}},

where
\mathcal{P}\subset\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}
is a finite set, and
0<\hat{M}<\infty
.

Proposition 13.2
.

There exist at most a finite set
\mathcal{Z}
of non-trivial connected components of
\Lambda
that cannot be contained in a single covering element in
\mathcal{C}_{0}
. Moreover, let
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
. Then there exist
Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{l}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
and

0=:t_{0}<t_{1}<\ldots<t_{l}:=|Z|

such that

Z([t_{i-1},t_{i}])\subset Q_{i}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt1\leq i\leq l.

In this case,
Q_{i}
’s are all regular boxes, except possibly exactly one of
Q_{0}
and
Q_{k}
, which may be a critical tunnel or a valuable crescent. Moreover,

\operatorname{cd}(Z(t))=\operatorname{vd}(Z(t))=\varnothing\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptt\in[t_{1},t_{l-1}].

Proposition 13.3
.

There exists
\hat{N}\geq N_{1}
such that for all
n\geq\hat{N}
and
0\leq i<R_{n}
, one of the following cases hold.

i)

There exists
Q\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
such that
\Lambda^{n}_{i}\subset Q
.

ii)

The piece
\Lambda^{n}_{i}
contains exactly one component
Z
in
\mathcal{Z}
. In this case, let
Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{l}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
be the covering elements given in Proposition
13.2
. Then

\Lambda^{n}_{i}\setminus Z\subset Q_{1}\cup Q_{l}.

Proof.

Let
\bar{\mathcal{B}}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
be a regular box. If the two extremal components of
\bar{\mathcal{B}}
are not contained
\mathcal{Z}
, then
\Lambda_{\bar{\mathcal{B}}}
is a compact subset of
\bar{\mathcal{B}}
. Hence, there exists
K_{1}\geq 0
such that any renormalization domain
\mathcal{D}^{n}_{i}
of depth
n\geq K_{1}
that intersects
\Lambda_{\bar{\mathcal{B}}}
must be contained in
\bar{\mathcal{B}}
.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
be a critical tunnel or crescent. By Theorem
12.2
, there exists
K_{2}\geq 0
such that

c_{m}\in\Lambda^{K_{2}}_{m}\subset\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}}.

Denote

\tilde{m}:=m\;(\operatorname{mod}\;R_{K_{2}}).

If the two extremal components of
\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}
are not contained
\mathcal{Z}
, then
\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}}\setminus\Lambda^{K_{2}}_{\tilde{m}}
is a compact subset of
\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}\setminus\mathcal{D}^{K_{2}}_{\tilde{m}}
. Hence, there exists
K_{3}\geq K_{2}
such that any renormalization domain
\mathcal{D}^{n}_{i}
of depth
n\geq K_{3}
that intersects
\Lambda_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}}\setminus\Lambda^{K_{2}}_{\tilde{m}}
must be contained in
\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}
.

The result follows by the finiteness of
\mathcal{C}_{0}
.
∎

Theorem 13.4
.

For
n\geq\hat{N}
and
1\leq i\leq R_{n}
, there exists a total order
<_{\Lambda^{n}_{i}}
on
\Lambda^{n}_{i}
such that following holds. For
Q\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
, either

•

x<_{\Lambda^{n}_{i}}y
for all
x,y\in\Lambda^{n}_{i}\cap Q
with
x<_{Q}y
; or

•

y<_{\Lambda^{n}_{i}}x
for all
x,y\in\Lambda^{n}_{i}\cap Q
with
x<_{Q}y
.

Moreover, if
1\leq i<R_{n}
, then
F(x)<_{\Lambda^{n}_{i+1}}F(y)
for all
x,y\in\Lambda^{n}_{i}
with
x<_{\Lambda^{n}_{i}}y
.

Proof.

Suppose
\Lambda^{n}_{i}
contains a component
Z\in\mathcal{Z}
(parameterized by its arclength
|Z|
). Let
Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{l}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
be the covering elements given in Proposition
13.2
. Define a total order on
\Lambda^{n}_{i}
by the following property. For
j\in\{1,l\}
, let
p_{j},q_{j}\in Q_{j}\setminus Z
. Then

i)

p_{j}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{i}}q_{j}
if
p_{j}<_{{Q_{j}}}q_{j}
;

ii)

p_{0},q_{0}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{i}}Z(0)
;

iii)

Z(t)<_{\Lambda^{n}_{i}}Z(s)
if
0\leq t<s\leq|Z|
; and

iv)

Z(|Z|)<_{\Lambda^{n}_{i}}p_{l},q_{l}
.

The result now follows by Proposition
10.7
,
10.8
and
10.9
.
∎

Let
\hat{N}\leq m<n
and
i\geq 0
. Observe that

\Lambda^{m}_{i}=\bigsqcup_{k=0}^{R_{n}/R_{m}-1}\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}.

For
a,b\in\Lambda^{m}_{i}
with
a\leq_{\Lambda^{m}_{i}}b
, denote

[a,b]_{\Lambda^{m}_{i}}:=\{p\in\Lambda^{m}_{i}\;|\;a\leq_{\Lambda^{m}_{i}}p\leq_{\Lambda^{m}_{i}}b\}.

This notation generalizes to
(\cdot,\cdot)_{\Lambda^{m}_{i}}
in the obvious way.

A
combinatorial component
\mathcal{K}
of
\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}
in
\Lambda^{m}_{i}
is a maximal subset of
\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}
of the form

\mathcal{K}=[a,b]_{\Lambda^{m}_{i}}

for some
a,b\in\Lambda^{m}_{i}
. We say that
\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}
is
combinatorially connected in
\Lambda^{m}_{i}
if
\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}
has a unique combinatorial component
\mathcal{K}
in
\Lambda^{m}_{i}
such that

\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}=\mathcal{K}.

Theorem 13.5
.

Let
\hat{N}\leq m<n
;
i\geq 0
and
0\leq k<R_{n}/R_{m}
. Then
\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}
is combinatorially connected in
\Lambda^{m}_{i}
.

For the proof of Theorem
13.5
, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 13.6
.

The renormalization piece
\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}
has at most finitely many combinatorial components in
\Lambda^{m}_{i}
which permute cyclically under
F^{R_{n}}
.

Proof.

Suppose
\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}
has infinitely many combinatorial components. Then one can find two sequences

\{a_{l}\}_{l=1}^{\infty}\subset\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\{b_{l}\}_{l=1}^{\infty}\subset\Lambda^{n}_{i+jR_{m}}\neq\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}

such that

a_{i}<_{\Lambda^{m}_{i}}b_{i}<_{\Lambda^{m}_{i}}a_{i+1}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pti\geq 1.

By compactness, the unique limit
a_{\infty}=b_{\infty}
of these sequences must belong to both
\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}
and
\Lambda^{n}_{i+jR_{m}}
, which is impossible.

Since
\{c_{-l}\}_{l=0}^{\infty}
accumulates to every connected component of
\Lambda
, we see that every combinatorial component of
\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}
must contain
c_{-l}
for some
l\geq 0
. Consequently, every combinatorial component of
\Lambda^{n}_{i+kR_{m}}
must eventually map to the unique one containing
c_{-R_{n}+i+kR_{m}}
. The result follows.
∎

Proof of Theorem
13.5
.

It suffices to prove the result for
\Lambda^{n+1}_{1}
in
\Lambda^{n}_{1}
. Suppose there exist combinatorial components

\mathcal{B}_{1}\subset\Lambda^{n+1}_{1}
and
\mathcal{X}_{1}\subset\Lambda^{n+1}_{j}\neq\Lambda^{n+1}_{1};

and points

a^{0}_{1}\in\Lambda^{n+1}_{1}
and
y^{0}_{1}\in\Lambda^{n+1}_{j}

such that

a^{0}_{1}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{X}_{1}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{B}_{1}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}y^{0}_{1}.

Proceeding by induction, suppose for
i\geq 0
, there exist points

a^{i}_{1}\in\Lambda^{n+1}_{1}
and
y^{i}_{1}\in\Lambda^{n+1}_{j}

such that

a^{i}_{1}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{X}_{1+iR_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{B}_{1+iR_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}y^{i}_{1}.

By Theorem
13.4
, Proposition
10.9
and Proposition
13.1
, we see that after applying
F^{R_{n}}
, the following holds.

i)

If
i=-1\;(\operatorname{mod}\;r_{n})
, then either

c_{1}=:a^{i+1}_{1}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{X}_{1+(i+1)R_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{B}_{1+(i+1)R_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}y^{i+1}_{1}:=y^{i}_{1+R_{n}}

or

a^{i}_{1+R_{n}}=:a^{i+1}_{1}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{X}_{1+(i+1)R_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{B}_{1+(i+1)R_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}y^{i+1}_{1}:=y^{i}_{1+R_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}c_{1}.

ii)

If
i=-k-1\;(\operatorname{mod}\;r_{n})
, then either

c_{1}<_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}}a^{i}_{1+R_{n}}=:a^{i+1}_{1}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{X}_{1+R_{n+1}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{B}_{1+R_{n+1}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}y^{i+1}_{1}:=y^{i}_{1+R_{n}}

or

a^{i}_{1+R_{n}}=:a^{i+1}_{1}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{X}_{1+R_{n+1}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{B}_{1+R_{n+1}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}y^{i+1}_{1}:=c_{1}.

iii)

Otherwise, either

c_{1}<_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}}a^{i}_{1+R_{n}}=:a^{i+1}_{1}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{X}_{1+R_{n+1}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{B}_{1+R_{n+1}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}y^{i+1}_{1}:=y^{i}_{1+R_{n}}

or

a^{i}_{1+R_{n}}=:a^{i+1}_{1}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{X}_{1+(i+1)R_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\mathcal{B}_{1+(i+1)R_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}y^{i+1}_{1}:=y^{i}_{1+R_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}c_{1}.

Thus, we see that
\mathcal{B}_{1+iR_{n}}
does not contain
c_{1}
for all
i\geq 0
. This contradicts Lemma
13.6
.
∎

Proposition 13.7
.

Let
n\geq\hat{N}
. Then the two extremal points of
\Lambda^{n}_{1}
with respect to
<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}
are
c_{1}
and
c_{1+R_{n}}
. That is, for all
p\in\Lambda^{n}_{1}\setminus\{c_{1},c_{1+R_{n}}\}
, we have

c_{1}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}p<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}c_{1+R_{n}}.

Proof.

Suppose

c_{1+R_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}c_{1-R_{n}}.

Then

F^{R_{n}}([c_{1},c_{1-R_{n}}]_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}})=[c_{1},c_{1+R_{n}}]_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\subset[c_{1},c_{1-R_{n}}]_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}.

Consequently,

\{c_{1+iR_{n}}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subset[c_{1+2R_{n}},c_{1+R_{n}}]_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}.

Since

c_{1}\notin[c_{1+2R_{n}},c_{1+R_{n}}]_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}},

this contradicts Theorem
12.2
.

Let
z_{1}\neq c_{1}
be an extremal point of
\Lambda^{n}_{1}
. Suppose

c_{1+R_{n}}<_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}z_{1}.

Then

F^{R_{n}}([c_{1},c_{1-R_{n}}]_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}})=[c_{1},c_{1+R_{n}}]_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}\neq[c_{1},z_{1}]_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}.

This implies that

F^{R_{n}}([c_{1-R_{n}},z_{1}]_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}})=[c_{1},z_{1}]_{\Lambda^{n}_{1}}.

Since
z_{1}
cannot be a fixed point, this is a contradiction.
∎

14.
Regular Hénon-like Returns

Let
F
be the mildly dissipative, infinitely renormalizable and regularity unicritical diffeomorphism considered in Section
10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be the unicritical cover of
\Lambda
defined in Subsection
10.3
. By compactness of
\Lambda
, we may choose a finite subcover

\mathcal{C}_{0}=\{\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\}_{p\in\mathcal{P}}\cup\{\mathcal{T}_{c_{-m}}\}_{m=0}^{\hat{M}_{-}}\cup\{\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}\}_{m=1}^{\hat{M}_{+}}\subset\mathcal{C},

where
\mathcal{P}\subset\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}
is a finite set of Pesin
(L,\delta)
-regular points, and
\hat{M}_{\pm}\in\mathbb{N}
. By increasing
\hat{M}_{\pm}
if necessary, we may assume that
\hat{M}_{\pm}
is a Pliss moment in the forward/backward orbit of
c_{1}
/
c_{0}
, and that we have
\mathcal{T}_{c_{\hat{M}_{\pm}}}\subset\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p^{\pm}}
for some Pesin regular point
p^{\pm}\in\mathcal{P}
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, recall that the
n
th renormalization domain containing
c_{1}
is denoted
\mathcal{D}^{n}_{1}
. The
valuable
and the
critical pieces of depth
n
are defined as

\Lambda^{n}_{1}=\Lambda\cap\mathcal{D}^{n}_{1}\ni c_{1}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Lambda^{n}_{0}:=\Lambda^{n}_{R_{n}}=\Lambda\cap\mathcal{D}^{n}_{R_{n}}\ni c_{0}

respectively.

Let

\{\Phi_{c_{m}}:(\mathcal{U}_{c_{m}},c_{m})\to(U_{c_{m}},0)\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

be a uniformization of the orbit of
c_{0}
given in Theorem
5.8
. For
p_{0}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
, let
E^{v}_{p_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{0}}
and
E^{h}_{p_{1}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{1}}
be the directions given by

D\Phi_{c_{0}}(E^{v}_{p_{0}})=E^{gv}_{\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0})}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptD\Phi_{c_{1}}(E^{h}_{p_{1}})=E^{gh}_{\Phi_{c_{1}}(p_{1})}=DF(E^{v}_{p_{0}}).

Let
\hat{N}\in\mathbb{N}
be the integer given in Proposition
13.3
. In the rest of this section, we assume that
n\geq\hat{N}
. The Main Theorem stated in Section
1
is an immediate consequence of the following result.

Theorem 14.1

(Valuable chart)
.

There exist
\tau_{n}>0
and a
C^{r}
-diffeomorphism
\Psi^{n}:(\Phi_{c_{1}}(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}),0)\to(V^{n}_{1},0)
, where

I^{n}:=(-\tau_{n},\tau_{n})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptV^{n}_{1}:=I^{n}\times(-l_{c_{1}},l_{c_{1}}),

such that the following properties hold.

i)

We have

F^{R_{n}}(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1})\Subset\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}},

and consequently

F^{R_{n}-1}(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1})\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}
and
\Lambda^{n}_{1}\subset\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}.

ii)

We have

\|\Psi^{n}-\operatorname{Id}\|_{C^{r}}=O(\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})R_{n}})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Psi^{n}|_{I^{n}\times\{0\}}\equiv\operatorname{Id}.

iii)

For
p_{1}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}
, let

E^{v,n}_{p_{1}}:=DF^{-R_{n}+1}(E^{v}_{p_{R_{n}}})\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{1}}.

Then

D\bar{\Psi}^{n}(E^{h}_{p_{1}})=E^{gh}_{\bar{\Psi}^{n}(p_{1})}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptD\bar{\Psi}^{n}(E^{v,n}_{p_{1}})=E^{gv}_{\bar{\Psi}^{n}(p_{1})},

where
\bar{\Psi}^{n}:=\Psi^{n}\circ\Phi_{c_{1}}
.

iv)

Let
p_{1}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}
. Then
p_{1}
is
(R_{n}-1)
-times forward
(1,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{p_{1}}
, and
p_{R_{n}}
is
(R_{n}-1)
-times backward
(1,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v}_{p_{R_{n}}}
.

v)

Define

H_{n}:=\Phi_{c_{0}}\circ F^{R_{n}-1}\circ(\bar{\Psi}^{n})^{-1}.

Then we have

H_{n}(x,y)=(h_{n}(x),e_{n}(x,y))\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt(x,y)\in V^{n}_{1},

where
h_{n}:I^{n}\to h_{n}(I^{n})
is a
C^{r}
-diffeomorphism, and
e_{n}:V^{n}_{1}\to\mathbb{R}
is a
C^{r}
-map such that

e_{n}(\cdot,0)\equiv 0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\|e_{n}\|_{C^{r}}=O(\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})R_{n}}).

Lemma 14.2
.

For
p\in\Lambda^{n}_{1}
, write

\Phi_{c_{1}}(p)=(x_{p},y_{p}).

Denote

\chi_{n+1}:=\inf_{p\in\Lambda^{n}_{1}\setminus\Lambda^{n+1}_{1}}|x_{p}|.

Then we have

\sup_{p\in\Lambda^{n+1}_{1}}|x_{p}|<\chi_{n+1}+O(\chi_{n+1}^{1/{\bar{\varepsilon}}}).

Proof.

Let
q_{1}\in\Lambda^{n}_{1}\setminus\Lambda^{n+1}_{1}
be a point satisfying
|x_{q_{1}}|=\chi_{n+1}
. If
\operatorname{cd}(q_{1})=\varnothing
, then by Theorem
9.8
,
q_{1}
is forward
(1,\delta)
-regular. Let
N
be an integer satisfying

N\asymp{\bar{\varepsilon}}^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{q_{1}}|.

Then by Lemma
3.8
,
q_{i}\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{i}}
for
1\leq i\leq N
. Note that
\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})N}<|x_{q_{1}}|^{1/{\bar{\varepsilon}}}
. By Proposition
3.11
, we see that
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(q_{1})
is a
O(|x_{q_{1}}|^{1/{\bar{\varepsilon}}})
-vertical curve in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. By Theorem
13.5
,
\Lambda^{n+1}_{1}
is contained in the vertical strip bounded between
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(q_{0})
and
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(c_{1})
. Thus, the result holds in this case.

If
\operatorname{cd}(q_{1})\neq\varnothing
, let
q_{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-m}}
for some
m>0
with
\operatorname{cd}(c_{-m})=\varnothing
. By Lemma
9.5
, we see that

\operatorname{dist}(q_{1},c_{-m})<|x_{q_{1}}|^{1/{\bar{\varepsilon}}}.

Moreover, arguing as above, we see that the vertical manifold
\hat{W}^{v}(c_{-m})
at
c_{-m}
is a
O(|x_{q_{1}}|^{1/{\bar{\varepsilon}}})
-vertical curve in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. The set
(c_{1},c_{-m})_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}}
is contained in the vertical strip bounded between
\hat{W}^{v}(c_{-m})
and
W^{ss}_{\operatorname{loc}}(c_{1})
. Moreover,

\Lambda^{n+1}_{1}\subset(c_{1},c_{-m})_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}}\cup\mathcal{T}_{c_{-m}}.

The result follows.
∎

Proposition 14.3
.

We have

\mathcal{T}_{c_{-R_{n}+1}}\cap\Lambda^{n+1}_{1}=\varnothing
and
\mathcal{T}_{c_{R_{n}}}\cap\Lambda^{n+1}_{0}=\varnothing.

Proof.

Suppose there exists
p_{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-R_{n}+1}}\cap\Lambda^{n+1}_{1}
. Let

q_{0}:=p_{R_{n}}\in F^{R_{n}-1}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-R_{n}+1}})\cap\Lambda^{n+1}_{R_{n}}\subset\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}.

Observe that

\operatorname{dist}(q_{0},c_{0})<\lambda^{-\eta R_{n}}\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-R_{n}+1}})<K\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})\delta R_{n}}

for some uniform constant
K\geq 1
. Since

\operatorname{dist}(q_{1},c_{1})\asymp\operatorname{dist}(q_{0},c_{0}),

we conclude by Lemma
14.2
that

\chi_{n+1}<K\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})\delta R_{n}}.

Write

(x,y):=\Phi_{c_{1}}(c_{-R_{n}+1}).

We have

\operatorname{dist}(c_{-R_{n}+1},c_{1})\asymp|x|<\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{T}_{c_{-R_{n}+1}})+\chi_{n+1}<K\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})\delta R_{n}}\ll\operatorname{diam}(\mathcal{U}_{c_{-R_{n}+1}}).

This contradicts Proposition
5.9
.

The second claim can be proved using the same proof
mutatis mutandis
.
∎

Let
p\in\Omega
. For
m\geq 0
, define

\operatorname{cd}^{m}(p):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\operatorname{cd}(p)&:\text{ if }0\leq\operatorname{cd}(p)<m\\
\varnothing&:\text{ otherwise}\end{array}\right..

Similarly, for
k\geq 1
, define

\operatorname{vd}^{k}(p):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\operatorname{vd}(p)&:\text{ if }1\leq\operatorname{vd}(p)<k\\
\varnothing&:\text{ otherwise}\end{array}\right..

Lemma 14.4
.

For
p_{0}\in\Lambda^{n}_{0}
, let

z_{0}:=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0})
and
E^{v}_{p_{0}}:=D_{z_{0}}\Phi_{c_{0}}^{-1}(E^{gv}_{z_{0}}).

Then for
0\leq i<R_{n}
such that
\operatorname{cd}^{i}(p_{-i})=\varnothing
, the point
p_{-i}
is
i
-times forward
(O(L),\delta)_{v}
-regular along

E^{v,n}_{p_{-i}}=DF^{-i}(E^{v}_{p_{0}}).

If, additionally, we have
p_{-i}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}
, then
p_{-i}
is
i
-times forward
(1,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{p_{-i}}
.

Proof.

First, suppose that

\operatorname{cd}(p_{0})=0.

Then for all
0\leq i<R_{n}
, we have

\operatorname{cd}^{i}(p_{-i})=\operatorname{cd}(p_{-i}).

Let
0<m<R_{n}
be an integer such that

p_{-m}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
and
\operatorname{cd}^{m}(p_{-m})=0.

Let
-j<0
and
l>1
be the tunnel and crescent escape moments for
p_{-m}
and
p_{-m+1}
respectively. Then by Proposition
6.9
, we have

\operatorname{cd}^{m-l}(p_{-m+l})=\operatorname{cd}^{m+j}(p_{-m-j})=\varnothing.

Suppose the statement holds for
p_{-m+l}
, so that
p_{-m+l}
is
(m-l)
-times forward
(O(L),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{p_{-m+l}}
. Then by Lemma
9.12
, Proposition
6.12
and Proposition
6.13
, we see that
p_{-m-j}
is
(m+j)
-times forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{p_{-m-j}}
. Moreover, by Theorem
9.8
and Proposition
6.13
,
p_{-m-j}
is forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-m-j}}
. Thus, by Proposition
3.11
, we see that

\measuredangle(E^{v,n}_{p_{-m-j}},\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-m-j}})<O(\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})(m+j)})\ll 1.

Let
m_{1}<R_{n}
be the smallest integer such that
m_{1}>m+j
and
p_{-m_{1}}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
. This means that for
m+j<i<m_{1}
, we have
\operatorname{cd}^{i}(p_{-i})=\varnothing
. Consequently, by Theorem
9.8
,
p_{-i}
is
i
-times forward
(L,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{p_{-i}}
. Thus, by Proposition
3.9
,
p_{-i}
is
i
-times forward
(O(L),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{p_{-i}}
. Proceeding by induction, the claim follows.

If

\operatorname{cd}(p_{0})\neq 0,

then let

0<n_{1}<\ldots<n_{K}\leq\infty

be the increasing sequence of all integers such that
p_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n_{k}}}
for
k\geq 1
. Furthermore, let
-(l_{k}+n_{k})<0
be the tunnel escape moment of
c_{-n_{k+1}+n_{k}}
. For
0\leq i<R_{n}
, let
q_{-i}:=c_{-n_{k_{i}}-i}
, where
k_{i}
is the smallest integer such that
l_{k_{i}}>i
. This means that

p_{-i}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{-n_{k_{i}}-i}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{cd}^{i}(c_{-n_{k_{i}}-i})=\operatorname{cd}(c_{-n_{k_{i}}-i}).

If
K<\infty
, let
q_{-i}:=p_{-i}
for
i\geq l_{K}
.

Observe that for
1\leq k\leq K
, we have

\operatorname{cd}(c_{-n_{k}-l_{k}})=\operatorname{cd}(c_{-n_{k+1}-l_{k}})=\varnothing.

Hence,
c_{-n_{k}-l_{k}}
is forward
(O(1),{\bar{\varepsilon}})_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{-n_{k}-l_{k}}}
, and by Proposition
6.13
,
c_{-n_{k+1}-l_{k}}
is forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{-n_{k+1}-l_{k}}}
. Moreover, if
c_{-n_{k}-l_{k-1}}
is
l_{k-1}
-times forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{c_{-n_{k}-l_{k-1}}}
, then by Proposition
3.11
and
3.9
, it follows that
c_{-n_{k+1}-l_{k}}
is
l_{k}
-times forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{c_{-n_{k+1}-l_{k}}}
.

The result now follows by replacing
p_{-i}
in the argument for the previous case (where
\operatorname{cd}(p_{0})=\varnothing
) with
q_{-i}
.
∎

Lemma 14.5
.

For
p_{1}\in\Lambda^{n}_{1}
, let

z_{1}:=\Phi_{c_{1}}(p_{1})
and
E^{h}_{p_{1}}:=D\Phi_{c_{1}}^{-1}(E^{gh}_{z_{1}}).

For
1\leq i\leq R_{n}
such that
\operatorname{vd}^{i}(p_{i})=\varnothing
, the point
p_{i}
is
i
-times backward
(O(L),\delta)_{h}
-regular along

E^{h,n}_{p_{i}}:=DF^{i-1}(E^{h}_{p_{1}}).

If, additionally, we have
p_{i}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
, then
p_{i}
is
i
-times backward
(1,\delta)_{h}
-regular along
E^{h,n}_{p_{i}}
.

Let
p\in\Lambda^{P}_{L,\delta}
. Recall that an encased regular box
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
at
p
is nearly a parallelogram, whose horizontal width and vertical height are commensurate with
1/L_{1}
and
1/L_{2}
respectively, where

{\bar{L}}<L_{1}<\underline{L_{2}}.
