Let
\mathcal{B}_{p}
be a (non-encased) regular box at at
p
whose horizontal width and vertical height are commensurate with
1/L_{1}^{\prime}
and
1/L_{2}^{\prime}
respectively, where

{\bar{L}}<L_{1}^{\prime}<\underline{L_{1}}
and
\overline{L_{1}^{\prime}}<L_{2}^{\prime}<\underline{L_{2}}.

If
-j<-\hat{M}_{-}
is a Pliss moment in the backward orbit of
c_{0}
, let
\mathcal{B}_{c_{-j}}
be a regular box at
c_{-j}
whose horizontal width and vertical height are uniformly bounded below.

Let
Q\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
. We define a larger covering element
\hat{Q}\supset Q
as follows. If
Q=\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
for some
p\in\mathcal{P}
, let
\hat{Q}:=\mathcal{B}_{p}
. Otherwise, we have
Q=\mathcal{T}_{c_{m}}
for some
-\hat{M}_{-}\leq m\leq\hat{M}_{+}
. In this case, let
\hat{Q}:={\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{m}}
, where
{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{m}}
is a thickened tunnel/crescent at
c_{m}
defined in (
10.3
)/(
10.4
).

For
p_{0}\in\Lambda^{n}_{0}
, let

(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0})
and

W^{v}(p_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}^{-1}(\mathbb{L}^{v}_{x_{0}}\cap U_{c_{0}}).

For
0\leq i<R_{n}
, define

W^{v,n}(p_{-i}):=F^{-i}(W^{v}(p_{0})).

Lemma 14.6
.

Let
p_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
. Let
-j<0
and
i>1
be the tunnel and crescent escape time for
p_{0}
and
p_{1}
respectively. Let

\{\hat{\Phi}_{c_{-j+m}}:\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{-j+m}}\to\hat{U}_{c_{-j+m}}\}_{m=-\infty}^{j}

be a linearization of the
(-\infty,j)
-orbit of
c_{-j}
with vertical direction
\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{-j}}
. Similarly, let

\{\check{\Phi}_{c_{i+m}}:\check{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{i+m}}\to\check{U}_{c_{i+m}}\}_{m=-i+1}^{\infty}

be a linearization of the
(-i+1,\infty)
-orbit of
c_{i}
with vertical direction
\hat{E}^{v}_{c_{i}}
. Denote the connected components of
\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{-j}}^{{\bar{\varepsilon}}j}\setminus{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-j}}
that are disjoint from
\hat{W}^{v}(c_{-j})
by
\hat{\mathcal{U}}^{-}
and
\hat{\mathcal{U}}^{+}
. Then the following statements hold.

i)

Let
\Gamma_{0}^{v}
be
C^{1}
-curve so that
\Gamma_{i}^{v}
is sufficiently vertical in
\check{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{i}}
and the distances between
p_{i}
and the endpoints of
\Gamma_{i}^{v}
are at least
1/\overline{L_{2}}
. Then
\Gamma_{-j}^{v}
is sufficiently vertical and proper in
\mathcal{B}_{c_{-j}}
.

ii)

We have

F^{j}(\hat{\mathcal{U}}^{-}\cup{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-j}}\cup\hat{\mathcal{U}}^{+})\subset{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}}.

iii)

Let
\Gamma^{h}_{-j}\ni p_{-j}
be a sufficiently horizontal curve in
\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{-j}}
. Then

\Gamma_{i}^{\pm}:=F^{j+i}(\Gamma_{-j}^{h}\cap\hat{\mathcal{U}}^{\pm})

is a sufficiently horizontal curve in
\check{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{i}}
.

Proof.

Write

(x_{1},y_{1}):=\Phi_{c_{1}}(p_{1}).

Note that

i>(1+{\bar{\delta}})\delta^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{1}|.

By Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
3.9
, we see that
\Gamma_{1}^{v}
is
|x_{1}|^{(1+{\bar{\delta}})/\delta}
-vertical in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. Furthermore,

\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_{1}^{v})>|x_{1}|^{-(1+{\bar{\delta}})/\delta}/\overline{L_{2}}>1\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\operatorname{dist}(\Gamma_{1}^{v})\asymp|x_{1}|.

This implies that
\Gamma_{1}^{v}
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
, and vertically proper in
{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{1}}
.

Let
q_{0}\in\Gamma_{0}^{v}\cap\bar{\partial}{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}}
, and write

(x_{0},y_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(p_{0})
and
(u_{0},v_{0}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(q_{0}).

Note that

j>(1+{\bar{\delta}})\delta^{-1}\log_{\lambda}|x_{0}|.

By Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
3.9
, we have

|v_{-j}|>\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})j}|v_{0}|>\lambda^{-(1-{\bar{\varepsilon}})j}|x_{0}|^{1/\hat{\delta}}>1.

Additionally, Proposition
6.13
implies that
\Gamma_{-j}
is sufficiently vertical in
\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{-j}}
. The first claim follows.

The second claim follows from Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
3.9
.

The third claim follows from Proposition
6.12
and Proposition
6.14
.
∎

Proposition 14.7
.

Let
p_{0}\in\Lambda^{n}_{0}
. For
\hat{M}_{-}\leq i<R_{n}
, if

p_{-i}\in Q\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
and
\operatorname{cd}^{i}(p_{-i})=\varnothing,

then
W^{v,n}(p_{-i})
is sufficiently vertical and vertically proper in
\hat{Q}
. If, additionally,
Q=\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}
, then
W^{v,n}(p_{-i})
is sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
.

Proof.

Denote

\Gamma_{-i}^{v}:=W^{v,n}(p_{-i}).

Let

0=:m_{0}<m_{1}<\ldots<m_{K}<R_{n}

be an increasing sequence of integers such that

p_{-m_{k}}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}
and
\operatorname{cd}^{m_{k}}(p_{-m_{k}})=0.

Let
(m_{k}-1-l_{k})>1
and
-(j_{k}-m_{k})<0
be the crescent and tunnel escape time for
p_{-m_{k}+1}
and
p_{-m)l}
respectively. By Proposition
6.9
, we see that

\operatorname{cd}^{l}(p_{-l_{k}})=\operatorname{cd}^{j}(p_{-j_{k}})=\varnothing\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptj_{k-1}<l_{k}<m_{k}<j_{k}<l_{k-1}.

If

\operatorname{diam}(\Gamma_{-l_{k}}^{v})>1/\overline{L_{2}},

then by Lemma
14.6
i) and Proposition
3.18
i),
\Gamma_{-j_{k}}^{v}
is sufficiently vertical and proper in any regular box
\hat{Q}
for which we have
p_{-j_{k}}\in Q
.

The result now follows from Proposition
3.19
ii).
∎

Let
p_{1}^{-},p_{1}^{+}\in\Lambda^{n}_{1}
. By Proposition
14.7
,
W^{v,n}(p_{1}^{\pm})
is vertical proper in
\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}
and sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
. Let
\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}(p_{1}^{-},p_{1}^{+})
be the connected component of
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}\setminus(W^{v,n}(p_{1}^{-})\cup W^{v,n}(p_{1}^{+}))
that contain
p_{1}^{-}
and
p_{1}^{+}
in its boundary. For
1\leq i\leq R_{n}
, denote

\mathcal{V}^{n}_{i}(p_{i}^{-},p_{i}^{+}):=F^{i-1}(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}(p_{1}^{-},p_{1}^{+})).

Proposition 14.8
.

There exist a uniform constant
\hat{K}\in\mathbb{N}
, and a set

\{q^{0}_{1},\ldots,q^{\hat{K}}_{1}\}\subset\Lambda^{n}_{1}

such that

c_{1}=:q^{0}_{1}<_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}}\ldots<_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}}q^{\hat{K}}_{1}:=c_{1+R_{n}},

and for all
0\leq k<\hat{K}
and
\hat{M}_{+}\leq i\leq R_{n}
, we have

\mathcal{V}^{n}_{i}(q^{k}_{i},q^{k+1}_{i})\subset\hat{Q}

for some
Q\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
Z
be a non-trivial component in the finite set
\mathcal{Z}
given in Proposition
13.2
, so that

Z([t_{i-1},t_{i}])\subset Q_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt1\leq i\leq l.

Observe that if
\Gamma_{1}\ni Z(t_{1})
is a sufficiently vertical proper Jordan arc in
Q_{1}
, and
\tilde{Q}_{1}
is the component of
Q_{1}\setminus\Gamma_{1}
containing
Z([t_{0},t_{1}))
, then

\Lambda\cap\tilde{Q}_{1}=\{p\in\Lambda_{Q_{1}}\;|\;p\leq_{Q_{1}}Z(t_{1})\}.

Moreover, the analogous property holds at
Z(t_{l-1})
in
\Lambda_{Q_{l}}
.

Let

\{z^{1},\ldots,z^{\hat{K}-1}\}\subset\bigcup_{Z\in\mathcal{Z}}Z

be the finite set of points such that each point
z^{j}
with
1\leq j<\hat{K}
is a point analogous to
Z(t_{i})
with
1\leq i<l
discussed above for some component
\tilde{Z}\in\mathcal{Z}
. By Theorem
13.4
, we can define

\{q^{1}_{1},\ldots,q^{\hat{K}-1}_{1}\}\subset\Lambda^{n}_{1}

as the set of points ordered with respect to
<_{\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}}
such that for
1\leq j<\hat{K}
, there exist unique
1\leq k<\hat{K}
and
1\leq m\leq R_{n}
such that

z^{j}=q^{k}_{m}.

By Proposition
14.3
, this means that

\operatorname{cd}(q^{k}_{1})=\operatorname{vd}(q^{k}_{R_{n}})=\varnothing\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt1\leq k<\hat{K}.

For
0\leq k<\hat{K}
, denote

q_{1}^{-}:=q_{1}^{k},\hskip 14.22636ptq_{1}^{+}:=q_{1}^{k+1}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{n}_{1}:=\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}(q_{1}^{-},q_{1}^{+}).

By Theorem
13.4
, we see that for all
1\leq i<R_{n}
, there exists
Q^{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
such that we have
q_{i}^{\pm}\in Q^{i}
.

Let

0=:m_{0}<m_{1}<\ldots<m_{I}:=R_{n}

be the increasing sequence of all integers such that for
0\leq i\leq I
, we have

q_{m_{i}}^{-},q_{m_{i}}^{+}\in\mathcal{T}_{c_{0}}.

Further denote

k_{i}:=m_{i}-\hat{M}_{-}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptl_{i}:=m_{i}+\hat{M}_{+}.

Then for
l_{i}\leq m\leq k_{i+1}
, there exists
p^{m}\in\mathcal{P}
such that

q_{m}^{-},q_{m}^{+}\in\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{p^{m}}.

We claim that for all
0\leq i<l
and
k_{i+1}\leq m\leq l_{i}
, the following holds. Let
q_{1}\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{n}_{1}
. Then
q_{m}\in\mathcal{B}_{p^{m}}
. Moreover, if
q_{m}
is not contained in a thickened valuable crescent of the form
{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{m-m_{j}}
for some
j\leq i
(which contains either
q_{m}^{-}
or
q_{m}^{+}
), then
E^{h,n}_{q_{m}}
is sufficiently horizontal in
\mathcal{B}_{p^{m}}
.

Proceeding by induction, suppose the claim holds for all
m<k_{i}
. Let
1<j<i
be an integer such that

\operatorname{vd}^{m_{j}}(q_{m_{j}}^{-})=\operatorname{vd}^{m_{j}}(q_{m_{j}}^{+})=0.

Then by Proposition
6.9
, if
-k^{\pm}<0
is the the tunnel escape moment for
q_{m_{j}}^{\pm}
, then

\operatorname{vd}^{m}(q_{m}^{\pm})=0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptm_{j}-k^{\pm}\leq m\leq m_{j}.

By the induction hypothesis and Lemma
14.6
ii), we see that
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{n}_{m_{j}}\subset{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}}
.

Suppose that for some
1<j<i
, we have

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{n}_{m_{j}}\subset{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}}.

Let
-k^{\pm}<0
and
l^{\pm}>1
be the tunnel and crescent escape moments for
q_{m_{j}}^{\pm}
and
q_{m_{j}+1}^{\pm}
respectively. Then by Proposition
10.4
, for
m_{j}-k^{\pm}\leq m\leq m_{j}+l^{\pm}
, we have
\mathcal{T}_{c_{m-m_{j}}}\subset Q^{m}
.

By these observations, we can conclude using the induction hypothesis and Lemma
14.6
ii) that

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{n}_{m_{j}}\subset{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for all}\hskip 14.22636pt1<j\leq i.

Let
q_{m_{i}}\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{n}_{m_{i}}\subset{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}}
. Denote by
-k<0
and
l>1
the tunnel and crescent escape time for
q_{m_{i}}
and
q_{m_{i}+1}
. Since
m_{i}-k<k_{i}
, by the induction hypothesis and Lemma
14.6
iii), we see that
q_{m_{i}+l}\in\mathcal{B}_{p^{m_{i}+l}}
, and
E^{v,n}_{q_{m_{i}+l}}
is sufficiently horizontal in
\mathcal{B}_{p^{m_{i}+l}}
.

Lastly, observe that if the induction hypothesis holds for all
m<m^{\prime}
with
l_{i}\leq m^{\prime}<k_{i+1}
, then by Proposition
3.19
i), and (
3.7
) applied to
W^{v,n}(q)
for all
q\in\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{n}_{m}
that is not contained in a thickened critical tunnel of the form
{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{-m_{j}+m}}
with
i<j\leq I
(which contains either
q_{m}^{-}
or
q_{m}^{+}
), the induction hypothesis must also hold for
m
. The result follows.
∎

Let
p_{1}\in\Lambda^{n}_{1}
. By Lemma
14.4
,
p_{1}
is
R_{n}
-times forward
(1,\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{p_{1}}
. Let

\{\tilde{\Phi}_{p_{m}}:\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{m}}\to\tilde{U}_{p_{m}}\}_{m=1}^{R_{n}}

be a linearization of the
(R_{n}-1)
-times forward orbit of
p_{1}
with vertical direction
E^{v,n}_{p_{1}}
. Note that

F^{R_{n}-1}(\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{1}}^{{\bar{\delta}}R_{n}})\subset\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{R_{n}}}\subset\mathcal{U}_{c_{0}}.

For
q_{1}\in\tilde{\mathcal{U}}_{p_{1}}^{{\bar{\delta}}R_{n}}
, write

z_{R_{n}}=(x_{R_{n}},y_{R_{n}}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(q_{R_{n}}),

and let

W^{v}(q_{R_{n}}):=\Phi_{c_{0}}^{-1}(\mathbb{L}^{v}_{x_{R_{n}}}\cap U_{c_{0}}).

Moreover, for
1\leq i\leq R_{n}
, define

W^{v,n}(q_{i}):=F^{i-R_{n}}(W^{v}(q_{R_{n}})).

By Proposition
3.9
and
14.7
, we see that
W^{v,n}(q_{1})
is vertically proper in
\mathcal{T}_{c_{1}}
and sufficiently stable-aligned in
\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}
.

Choose
\tilde{c}_{1}^{n}\in\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{1}}^{{\bar{\delta}}R_{n}}
and
\tilde{c}_{1+R_{n}}^{n}\in\hat{\mathcal{U}}_{c_{1+R_{n}}}^{{\bar{\delta}}R_{n}}
such that

\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}:=\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}(\tilde{c}_{1}^{n},\tilde{c}_{1+R_{n}}^{n})\supset\{p\in\mathcal{U}_{c_{1}}\;|\;\operatorname{dist}(p,\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}(c_{1},c_{1+R_{n}}))<\lambda^{{\bar{\delta}}R_{n}}\}.

(14.1)

Denote

V^{n}_{1}:=\Phi_{c_{1}}(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}),

and for
1\leq i\leq R_{n}
,

\mathcal{V}^{n}_{i}:=F^{i-1}(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}).

Lemma 14.9
.

Let
q_{0}\in{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}}
. Let
-k<0
and
l>1
be the tunnel and crescent escape moment for
q_{0}
and
q_{1}
respectively. Suppose that
q_{l}
is
N
-times forward
(O(L),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{q_{l}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{q_{l}}
for some

N>-\log_{\lambda}{\bar{L}}.

Then
q_{-k}
is
(N+k+l)
-times forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{q_{-k}}
.

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Proposition
3.11
,
6.12
and
6.13
.
∎

Lemma 14.10
.

Let
p_{0}\in{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}}
and
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{0}}\in\mathbb{P}^{2}_{p_{-j}}
. Let
-k_{0}<0
be the tunnel escape moment for
p_{0}
, and let
-k_{1}\leq-k_{0}
be a Pliss moment in the backward orbit of
c_{0}
. Suppose
p_{-j}
is
j
-times forward
(O(L),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-j}}
and
c_{-j}\in\mathcal{B}_{p_{-j}}
for all
k_{0}<j<k_{1}
. If
p_{-k_{0}}
is
k_{0}
-times forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-k_{0}}}
, then
p_{-k_{1}}
is
k_{1}
-times forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
\tilde{E}^{v}_{p_{-k_{1}}}
.

Proof.

The result follows immediately from Proposition
3.11
.
∎

Proposition 14.11
.

The statement of Lemma
14.4
generalizes to
q_{0}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}_{R_{n}}
.

Similarly, the statement of Lemma
14.5
generalizes to
q_{1}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
14.8
, there exist
q_{0}^{-},q_{0}^{+}\in\Lambda^{n}_{0}
such that the following holds for all
0\leq i<R_{n}
. Let

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{n}_{-i}:=\mathcal{V}^{n}_{R_{n}-i}(q_{-i}^{-},q_{-i}^{+})\ni q_{-i}.

Then we have

\tilde{\mathcal{V}}^{n}_{-i}\subset\hat{Q}^{-i}

for some
Q^{-i}\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
.

Let

0=:m_{0}<m_{1}<\ldots<m_{I}<R_{n}

be the increasing set of all moments such that for
0\leq i\leq I
, we have

q_{-m_{i}}\in{\hat{\mathcal{T}}}_{c_{0}}
and
\operatorname{cd}^{m_{i}}(q_{-m_{i}})=0.

Let
(-k_{i}+m_{i})<0
and
(l_{i}-m_{i})>1
be the tunnel and crescent escape moments for
q_{-m_{i}}
and
q_{-m_{i}+1}
respectively.

Proceeding by induction, suppose that the claim is true for
m<m_{i}
. By Theorem
13.4
and Proposition
14.8
, there exists
p_{-k_{i}}\in\Lambda_{Q^{-k_{i}}}
such that
p_{-m}\in Q^{-m}
for
l_{i}\leq m\leq k_{i}
, and

\operatorname{cd}^{k_{i}}(p_{-k_{i}})=\varnothing.

For simplicity, assume that

p_{-k_{i}}=q_{-k_{i}}^{+}.

In general,

p_{-k_{i}}=c_{-d}

where
d=k_{i}-m_{j}
for some
j<i
, and
\mathcal{T}_{c_{-d}}
contains either
q_{-k_{i}}^{-}
or
q_{-k_{i}}^{+}
. The argument in this case requires some minor technical adjustments which we will omit.

By the induction hypothesis and Lemma
14.9
,
q_{-k_{i}}
is
k_{i}
-times forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{q_{-k_{i}}}
. By Lemma
14.10
,
q_{-k_{i}}^{+}
is also
k_{i}
-times forward
(O(1),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{q_{-k_{i}}^{+}}
. Moreover, by Lemma
3.11
, we see that
E^{v,n}_{q_{-k_{i}}}
is sufficiently vertical in a regular box
\mathcal{B}_{q_{-k_{i}}^{+}}
at
q_{-k_{i}}^{+}
.

By Lemma
14.4
, for
k_{i}<m<m_{i+1}
, the point
q_{-m}^{+}
is
m
-times
(O(L),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{q_{-m}^{+}}
. Since
q_{-m}\in\mathcal{B}_{q_{-m}^{+}}
, we conclude by Proposition
3.9
that
q_{-m}
is
m
-times
(O(L),\delta)_{v}
-regular along
E^{v,n}_{q_{-m}}
. The first part of the result follows.

The proof of the second part is nearly identical to that of Theorem
9.8
b), and will be omitted.
∎

Proof of Theorem
14.1
.

By Proposition
14.11
, the set
\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}
can be covered by regular charts obtained by linearizing the
(R_{n}-1)
-time forward orbit of any point
p_{1}\in\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}
with vertical direction
E^{v,n}_{p_{1}}
. The desired diffeomorphism
\bar{\Psi}^{n}_{c_{1}}:\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}\to V^{n}_{1}
is produced by gluing these charts. It remains to prove that

F^{R_{n}}(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1})\Subset\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1}.

Write

(x,y):=\Phi_{c_{1}}(c_{1+R_{n}}),\hskip 14.22636pt(a,b):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(c_{R_{n}})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt(u,v):=\Phi_{c_{0}}(c_{2R_{n}}).

Observe that

\Phi_{c_{1}}(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{1})\supset(-\lambda^{{\bar{\delta}}R_{n}},x+\lambda^{{\bar{\delta}}R_{n}})\times\mathbb{I}(l_{c_{1}}).

By Proposition
14.11
, we also see that

\Phi_{c_{0}}(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{R_{n}})\subset(-\lambda^{{\bar{\delta}}R_{n}}+a,u+\lambda^{{\bar{\delta}}R_{n}})\times\mathbb{I}(\lambda^{(1-{\bar{\delta}})R_{n}}).

Since for

(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}):=\Phi_{c_{1}}\circ F\circ\Phi_{c_{0}}^{-1}(u,v),

we have
0<u^{\prime}<x
, the result now follows from Theorem
5.8
.
∎

Appendix A
Regular Unicriticality in 1D

Let
f:I\to I
be a
C^{2}
-unimodal map on an interval
I\subset\mathbb{R}
with the critical point at
0\in I
. Suppose that
f
is infinitely renormalizable. That is, there exist a nested sequence
I=:I^{0}_{1}\Supset I^{1}_{1}\Supset\ldots
of intervals and a sequence of natural numbers
1=:R_{0}<R_{1}<\ldots
such that
I^{n}_{1}
is
R_{n}
-periodic and

r_{n-1}:=R_{n}/R_{n-1}\geq 2\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptn\in\mathbb{N}.

We assume that
f
is
of bounded type
: there exists a constant
K>1
such that
r_{n}<K
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Let

I^{n}_{0}:=f^{-1}(I^{n}_{1})\ni 0\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptI^{n}_{i}:=f^{i}(I^{n}_{0})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq i<R_{n}.

The renormalization limit set of
f
is given by

\Lambda:=\bigcap_{n=1}^{\infty}\bigcup_{i=0}^{R_{n}-1}f^{i}(I^{n}_{1}).

Denote the orbit of
c_{0}:=0
by

c_{-n}:=(f^{n}|_{\Lambda})^{-1}(0)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptc_{n}:=f^{n}(0)\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636ptn\in\mathbb{N}.

For
x\in\Lambda\setminus\{c_{0}\}
, define
0pt_{c}(x)
as the largest integer
d\geq 0
such that

x\in I^{d}_{0}\setminus I^{d+1}_{0}.

Also define
\operatorname{entry}_{d}(x)
as the integer
0<s<r_{d}
such that

f^{sR_{d}}(x)\in I^{d+1}_{0}.

Theorem A.1

(Real
a priori
bounds)
.

There exist uniform constants
\sigma\in(0,1)
and
\nu>0
such that the following statements hold for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

i)

For

x\in I^{n}_{0}\setminus I^{n+1}_{0},

let

s:=\operatorname{entry}_{n}(x).

Then

|(f^{iR_{n}})^{\prime}(x)|>\nu\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq i\leq s.

ii)

We have

|(f^{iR_{n}}|_{I^{n}_{1}})^{\prime}|>\nu\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq i<r_{n}.

Fix
\varepsilon_{0}>0
sufficiently small. For
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, let

{\bar{\varepsilon}}:=\varepsilon^{\alpha}
and
\underline{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon^{1/\alpha}

for some universal constant
\alpha\in(0,1)
. For
t>0
and
n\geq 0
, denote

D^{t}_{-n}:=[c_{-n}-te^{-\underline{\varepsilon}n},c_{-n}+te^{-\underline{\varepsilon}n}].

Definition A.2
.

We say that
f
is
(\varepsilon,\underline{\varepsilon})
-regularly unicritical on
\Lambda
if for any
t>0
, there exists
L=L(t)\geq 1
such that the following condition holds. If

x\in\Lambda\setminus\bigcup_{i=0}^{N-1}D^{t}_{-i}

for some
N\in\mathbb{N}
, then

|(f^{n})^{\prime}(x)|>Le^{-\varepsilon n}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt1\leq n\leq N.

Theorem A.3
.

For any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, the infinitely renormalizable unimodal map
f
is
(\varepsilon,\underline{\varepsilon})
-regularly unicritical on its renormalization limit set
\Lambda
.

To prove Theorem
A.3
, we first need the following lemma.

Lemma A.4
.

For
t>0
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let

C^{t}_{n}(0):=[-te^{-\underline{\varepsilon}R_{n}},te^{-\underline{\varepsilon}R_{n}}]\subset I^{n}_{0}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636ptC^{t}_{n}:=\bigcup_{i=0}^{R_{n}-1}(f^{i}|_{I^{n}_{R_{n}-i}})^{-1}(C^{t}_{n}(0)).

Then

C^{t}_{n}\subset\bigcup_{i=0}^{R_{n}-1}D^{t}_{-i}.

Proof.

Write

i=s_{n-1}R_{n-1}+\ldots+s_{0}R_{0},

where
0\leq s_{j}<r_{j}
for
0\leq j<n
. By Theorem
A.1
i), for any
J\subset I^{j+1}_{R_{j+1}}
, we have

\left|f^{-s_{j}R_{j}}|_{I^{j+1}_{R_{j+1}}}(J)\right|<\nu^{-1}|J|.

It follows that for

C^{t}_{n}(i):=(f^{i}|_{I^{n}_{R_{n}-i}})^{-1}(C^{t}_{n}(0)),

we have

|C^{t}_{n}(i)|<\nu^{-n}|C^{t}_{n}(0)|.

∎

For
x\in\Lambda\setminus\{c_{1}\}
, define
0pt_{v}(x)
as the largest integer
d\geq 0
such that

x\in I^{d}_{1}\setminus I^{d+1}_{1}.

Proof of Theorem
A.3
.

Let

x_{0}\in\Lambda\setminus\bigcup_{i=0}^{N-1}D^{t}_{-i}

for some
N\in\mathbb{N}
. For
0\leq n\leq N
, let
n=m+k+1
, where

d_{c}:=0pt_{c}(x_{m})=\max_{0\leq i\leq n}0pt_{c}(x_{i}).

Let

d_{v}:=0pt_{v}(x_{m+1})=\max_{0\leq i\leq n}0pt_{v}(x_{i}).

Decompose
m
as

m=s_{0}R_{0}+\ldots+s_{d_{c}-1}R_{d_{c}-1},

with
0\leq s_{l}<r_{l}
for
0\leq l<d_{c}
. Similarly, we have

k=t_{0}R_{0}+\ldots+t_{d_{v}-1}R_{d_{v}-1},

with
0\leq t_{l}<r_{l}
for
0\leq l<d_{v}
.

By Theorem
A.1
, we have

|(f^{m})^{\prime}(x_{0})|>\nu^{d_{c}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt|(f^{k})^{\prime}(x_{m+1})|>\nu^{d_{v}}.

Moreover,

|f^{\prime}(x_{m})|\asymp|x_{m}|>te^{-{\bar{\varepsilon}}m}

by Lemma
A.4
. Since

d_{c},d_{v}<\log n,

the result follows.
∎

Appendix B
Pliss Lemma

Let
\alpha_{1}<\alpha_{2}<\alpha_{3}
. For some
N\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, consider a sequence
\{a_{n}\}_{n=1}^{N}\subset(\alpha_{1},\infty)
. Suppose either:

\frac{1}{i}(a_{1}+a_{2}+\ldots+a_{i})\leq\alpha_{2}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for}\hskip 14.22636pt1\leq i\leq N,

(B.1)

or more weakly (assuming
N=\infty
):

\limsup_{i\to\infty}\frac{1}{i}(a_{1}+a_{2}+\ldots+a_{i})\leq\alpha_{2}.

(B.2)

An integer
1\leq k\leq N
is a
direction-preserving Pliss moment
if

\frac{1}{i+1}(a_{k}+a_{k+1}\ldots+a_{k+i})\leq\alpha_{3}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for all}\hskip 14.22636pt0\leq i\leq N-k.

Similarly,
1\leq m\leq N
is a
direction-reversing Pliss moment
if either
m=1
, or
m>1
and

\frac{1}{i}(a_{m-i}+\ldots+a_{m-2}+a_{m-1})\leq\alpha_{3}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for all}\hskip 14.22636pt1\leq i<m.

Lastly,
1\leq n\leq N
is an
absolute Pliss moment
if it is both a direction-preserving and direction-reversing Pliss moment.

Let
\{k_{i}\},\{m_{i}\},\{n_{i}\}\subset\{1,\ldots,N\}
be the increasing sequences of all direction-preserving, direction-reversing and absolute Pliss moments respectively.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
\{j_{i}\}=\{k_{i}\}
or
\{m_{i}\}
. If (
B.1
) holds, then we have

\frac{i}{j_{i}}\geq\frac{\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{3}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for all}\hskip 14.22636pti\geq 1.

If (
B.2
) holds, then we have

\liminf_{i\to\infty}\frac{i}{j_{i}}\geq\frac{\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{3}}.

Lemma B.2
.

Let
\{n_{i}\}\subset\{1,\ldots,N\}
be the increasing sequence of all absolute Pliss moments. If (
B.1
) holds, then

\frac{i+2}{n_{i}}\geq\frac{2\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{3}}.

If (
B.2
) holds, then

\liminf_{i\to\infty}\frac{i}{n_{i}}\geq\frac{2\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{3}}{\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{3}}.

Remark B.3
.

By replacing
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}
and
a_{n}
’s by their negative values, we can obtain the same lower density bounds as in Lemma
B.1
and Lemma
B.2
for sequences that have lower bounds on their average sums rather than upper bounds.

Stony Brook University

Stony Brook, NY, USA

Enrique Pujals

Jonguk Yang
